### A. Point 6 — Review badge stays red after Remove
| # | Label | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | VERIFIED | Badge number = `dueToday` from `NavDataContext` <- `useDueForecast` <- RPC `get_due_forecast` (`src/hooks/useDueForecast.js:24`, `src/contexts/NavDataContext.jsx:39,67-71`; rendered `NavDesktop.jsx:209-211`, `NavBottomTabs.jsx:246-248`). Fetched once per user/mount. |
| 6.2 | VERIFIED | `refetchDueForecast` is exposed (`NavDataContext.jsx:71`) but no code calls it (grep over `src`: only that line). The badge is never refreshed after Pause/Resume/Remove/Re-add, review completion, or course switch. Defect #1 (stale). |
| 6.3 | VERIFIED-in-repo | `get_due_forecast` (`docs/database/srs-ladder/02_FUNCTIONS_srs_ladder_engine.sql:410-476`) has no enrollment filter. It shares the course/visibility/question-type/skip predicate with `get_study_queue` (`sprint8.7.8e/01_...:102-160`), which is itself enrollment-blind by design (comment `ReviewSession.jsx:40-49`, D-41 Area G). |
| 6.4 | VERIFIED | The Review page intersects `get_study_queue` with `get_my_cards` (own + actively enrolled) client-side (`ReviewSession.jsx:50-59`). A card with a due `reviews` row but a Removed / course_archived enrollment is counted by the badge and hidden by the Review page. Defect #2 (semantic mismatch). |
| 6.5 | VERIFIED | Two more surfaces share the enrollment-blind count: Dashboard `reviewsDue = get_study_queue.length` (`Dashboard.jsx:351-352`) and the Progress "due today" tile (`Progress.jsx:205,513`). The fix surface is wider than the badge. |
| 6.6 | NOTE FOR QA | The earlier QA wording said the forecast predicate differs from Review on "enrollment, course, visibility and question-type". Repo copies show course, visibility and question-type are identical; enrollment is the one divergence. Live bodies (SQL block 1) will confirm. |
| 6.7 | OPEN | Size of the live discrepancy (SQL block 7). |

## Round 3 · QA · 03/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The diagnostic's no-personal-data claim is false.** `00_DIAGNOSTIC_pre-8.8.6_backlog_catalog.sql:129-135` returns the literal values of `notes.custom_course` and `flashcards.custom_subject`. Those are user-authored free-text fields and can contain names or other personal data; `LIMIT 100` and a count beside each value do not sanitize them. `profiles.course_level` at `:92-94` is also free text at the database boundary and therefore needs the same privacy treatment unless its live constraint is proved before values are selected. Revise the diagnostic to answer the design questions using non-identifying aggregates; do not run or save the current block 4 output.

2. **Block 7 does not measure the claimed badge-versus-Review discrepancy.** It uses server `CURRENT_DATE` rather than each student's timezone date, omits the shared course, visibility and `question_type <> 'concept_card'` predicates, counts own cards even though `get_my_cards` always admits them, and considers only explicit `removed`/`course_archived` rows rather than the full absence-of-active-enrollment condition. Its result can therefore overstate or understate the real mismatch. Replace it with an exact set comparison that reproduces the live forecast predicate and the Review page's own-or-active-enrollment boundary.

3. **The affected forecast surface and live check are incomplete.** `Dashboard.jsx:398` renders `get_due_forecast_buckets`; the repo definition says it uses the same enrollment-blind predicate as `get_due_forecast`. Round 2 finding 6.5 does not include this Forward Load surface, and SQL blocks 1-2 do not inspect its live body or grants. Add it to the current-state matrix and diagnostic before the point-6 design is treated as complete.

4. **The course-identity diagnostic cannot prove two absence claims on which design stream B depends.** Listing selected columns on four known tables does not establish that there is no student multi-course registry or custom-course entity in the live schema. Add a bounded catalog inventory for course-related public tables/columns plus the relevant keys, foreign keys and constraints (including `study_group_members` status/role/uniqueness if its live model is to be relied on). Until then, findings 5.2-5.4 remain repo evidence, not live facts.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The cited code at `6fc6ceb` independently supports findings 6.1-6.5, 7.2-7.3, 1.1-1.2, 2.5 and 10.2-10.3. Finding 6.5 is true but incomplete because of blocking finding 3. No `src/` file changed between `6fc6ceb` and current HEAD.

2. The OPEN tail of finding 2.5 can be closed from repo code: `App.jsx:118-125` consumes `postAuthRedirect` after authentication, so the stored join route is honoured after login/signup once a user session exists.

3. The status block's recorded HEAD (`e15e00a`) is stale; audit-time HEAD is `0746fce`. The intervening commits change configuration/docs and add this diagnostic, not `src/`, so the code evidence remains valid. Round 2's statement that "No code or SQL was changed" should say no application or production SQL was changed, because the diagnostic SQL itself was added.

4. Finding 5.4 materially shapes, but does not reverse, the agreed points 5/10 direction. `profiles.course_level` is the current active-course/access selector, not an adequate multi-course reporting taxonomy. Design stream B must define a durable course identity for system and custom labels separately from the current-course selector, state whether "custom courses as real groups" means a reporting/UI group or a new database entity, and define how historical/unassigned/legacy-NULL rows appear. The picker must not infer the set of reportable courses from `profiles.course_level` alone.

5. Finding 10.2 narrows the point-10 acceptance wording. There is no current direct-entry offline form; the acceptance criterion should cover every manual/offline finalization path (normal stop, recovery choices and restored `pendingLog`) at the shared `confirmCategory` write boundary, plus database enforcement. Any future direct-entry writer must use the same contract rather than being treated as a current path.

6. **Agreed-decisions check.** The status bullets are substantively consistent with the prior QA positions summarized by Round 1. In particular, finding 6.6 corrects an earlier factual predicate description without changing the agreed outcome that the badge match Review eligibility. Two qualifications must remain explicit: "custom courses as real groups" is a product/reporting direction, not a verified existing schema fact; and the repository contains no earlier QA round (`Last QA-reviewed round` was 0), so the three-party attribution cannot be independently reconstructed from this thread alone. I found no other substantive distortion of the prior positions.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- Handoff gate was valid (`AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`) and the reviewed SQL bytes hash to full SHA-256 `b0f136bf948ca3cc8385eca099ef850d7c0eac67954cf168c3003b5650eb145b`, matching short hash `b0f136bf948c`.
- The diagnostic contains only `SELECT` statements and catalog/decompilation reads; it has no DML, DDL or calls to application functions. Its defect is output privacy/completeness, not database mutation.
- The code establishes the central point-6 defect: the shared nav forecast is fetched on user/mount and exposes a refetch that no caller uses, while Review applies the additional `get_my_cards` boundary.
- The manual timer has one current database-write choke point after normal stop, recovery and persisted-pending flows, which is a strong place to enforce the future course/subject contract alongside a database constraint.
- Round 2 correctly distinguishes repo-derived database evidence from live verification and does not present the unrun diagnostic as live proof.

### Residual risks

- Treat findings 6.3, 7.1, 1.3-1.4, 2.1-2.3, 10.1, 5.2-5.4 and the database/security portion of section E as unproven until a revised diagnostic is run against production and its sanitized evidence is recorded. Repo migrations and `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` are corroboration, not live authority.
- The founder's concrete point-2 confusion has not been reproduced. This is an agreed Step-0 requirement and remains a dependency before the admission/access design brief is considered complete.

## Round 4 · Founder · 03/10/2026
*(Recorded by Claude from the Founder's chat messages of 03/10/2026; the Founder confirms accuracy by leaving it unchallenged.)*

**Concrete example for open item 2.4 (point 2).** The Founder showed two admin screens (Batch Groups > Pending Batch Requests, and User Management) and described this case: a student who created an account *before* receiving a batch invitation does not get content access automatically and must request access. Such users appear under User Management, where the admin cannot tell who they are. After the admin approves access, each enrolled student row offers an "Add to batch…" dropdown listing active batches. After clicking **Add**, nothing visibly happens and the dropdown returns to "Add to batch…". The admin also cannot tell whether the student is in CA Foundation, CA Intermediate or CA Final, so cannot choose the right batch. The Founder does not recall whether the batch's member count rose after Add and asked Claude to determine this from the code.

**Decision.** The Founder chose to restructure the diagnostic SQL into one result per topic (a single copyable result per run) rather than revise it in place.

## Round 5 · QA · 03/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The v2 privacy claim is still broader than the query guarantees.** RUN 3 emits the literal stored `study_sessions.source` value in `source_category_mix`, while the same run is intended to discover whether the live source CHECK exists and is correct. If that constraint is absent or drifted, an authenticated client could have stored arbitrary text. RUN 6 likewise emits literal historical `admin_audit_log.action` values; this column had direct admin-client writers before the closeout and no value constraint is established before output. These are not known personal data, but they are unbounded stored text and therefore do not satisfy "no user-authored value anywhere." Bucket expected values and report an `unexpected_other` count/boolean without returning unexpected literals. Function definitions and platform taxonomy names are necessary schema/catalog evidence and do not need further sanitising unless an unexpected personal literal is actually observed; if that occurs, keep the raw result out of tracked evidence and save only a sanitized summary.

2. **RUN 5B still does not include the relevant keys requested in Round 3 or enough structure for a newly discovered registry table.** It lists all public base-table names, course-like column names, FKs whose target is specifically `disciplines`/`subjects`, and matching CHECKs, but no primary/unique constraints. It also does not list all columns and relationships of a table whose *table name* indicates a course/program/qualification registry when its individual columns are generic (`id`, `name`, `user_id`). Add PK/UNIQUE/FK constraints and full column metadata for the bounded set of course/discipline/subject/curriculum/exam/level/program/qualification-named tables, including `profile_courses`, `disciplines` and `subjects`. RUN 6 is sufficient for the separate `study_group_members` status/role/uniqueness question.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **QA correction on own cards.** Claude's challenge is correct. `sprint8.7.10/02_FUNCTIONS_get_my_cards_v2.sql:32-52` requires an active `my_cards_enrollment` row for own and external cards; my Round 3 statement used the older 8.7.8b contract. RUN 8 faithfully reproduces the repo copies of `get_due_forecast.due_today` and `get_my_cards` v2, including per-profile timezone, due/skip, question type, course, visibility and active-enrollment predicates. Its stated dependency is correct: compare RUN 1B's live bodies first and re-derive RUN 8 if live differs.

2. Finding 6.8 is confirmed: `Dashboard.jsx:398` consumes `get_due_forecast_buckets`, and the repo function at `sprint6.3/01_FUNCTIONS_dashboard_reskin_rpcs.sql:76-104` has the same enrollment-blind scheduled-row predicate. It is the fourth affected point-6 surface.

3. Findings 2.6-2.10 are supported by the cited code/repo SQL. One design qualification: a truthful Add result is not wholly a frontend-only feedback change. `enroll_user_in_batch_group` returns `void`, so the caller cannot distinguish newly activated membership from the already-active no-op. Either the RPC must return an outcome or the UI must obtain authoritative membership state; merely showing a generic success message would preserve ambiguity.

4. Finding 2.11 should be narrower. The batch invite token and access request have no direct relationship or intended-recipient record, but a signed-up account can be correlated to an access request through `profiles.access_request_ref`, and a used batch link creates a membership keyed by the same profile/user id. The demonstrated defect is that the current admin UI does not join or display these relationships—and an unused shared link has no recipient to join—not that the data model has no possible correlation at all.

5. The status block is sufficient as the working summary of earlier QA positions if the Founder explicitly confirms it; preserving old chat excerpts is unnecessary unless a decision is disputed. "Leaving it unchallenged" is not affirmative Founder approval and cannot establish a gate or authorize execution. Any design/SQL/deployment approval must still be recorded by exact version under the normal protocol.

6. The status block's `06d44dc` is accurately labelled as the prior audit-time HEAD. Current HEAD is the Round 4 commit `394dc59`; the diff from `6fc6ceb` still contains no `src/` changes.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid and the revised SQL bytes hash to full SHA-256 `08bcbecbab60e3dac1285acb7c328cea1a86715e52adddcb0d4bfe31de7ff09f`, matching short hash `08bcbecbab60`.
- The file has exactly ten executable statements. Every one is `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; there is no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation.
- v2 removes the raw `profiles.course_level`, `notes.custom_course` and `flashcards.custom_subject` values identified in Round 3 and replaces them with useful counts, including canonical-platform matches and distinct noncanonical counts.
- RUN 1B/RUN 2 now cover `get_due_forecast_buckets`; RUN 8 fixes the earlier timezone and predicate omissions and reports only aggregate counts.
- RUN 6 comprehensively captures group/member columns, constraints, indexes, policies and state counts without names or IDs.
- Round 4's append-only corrections, code findings 2.6-2.10, and explicit live-verification labels materially improve the Step-0 record.

### Residual risks

- SQL syntax remains unexecuted; only a production read can establish live compatibility. An error result is evidence, not permission to edit and rerun ad hoc.
- Findings 5.2-5.4, 1.4, 2.7, 2.9, 6.3, 6.8, 7.1, 10.1 and the database/security portion of section E remain repo-derived until the revised diagnostic is run and sanitized evidence is recorded.
- Live removal authorization/mutation/audit/notification behavior remains unknown. RUN 1A is the deciding evidence.
- No gate is approved, and this disposition is not authorization to run SQL, implement, deploy, commit or push.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise the two remaining diagnostic issues, assign a new short SHA-256 hash, and return the thread to QA. After that exact artifact passes QA, the Founder decides whether to execute its ten read-only runs. Design briefs remain downstream of the live evidence and a separate Founder decision.

### D. New observations from the live catalog (for QA to assess; not decisions)
- **S-1. Direct table writes by a batch group admin bypass the RPC rules.** RLS policy `sgm_delete` lets a user delete their own membership row, and lets any group-admin member delete any member row in their group; `sgm_insert_admin` lets a group admin insert member rows into a non-archived group directly. The server rules added in 8.8.5d (only students in batches; archived-batch locks; audit; notifications) live in the RPCs, not in these policies. Current exposure is limited (RUN 6: one batch group-admin member), and no UPDATE policy exists. Whether this matters for the removal design is a question for QA and the Founder.
- **S-2. A student can leave a batch by deleting their own row directly.** The same `sgm_delete` policy permits it with no audit entry. `leave_group` also hard-deletes.
- **S-3. `submit_access_request` and `link_access_request` take client-supplied identifiers.** `submit_access_request` is executable by `PUBLIC`/`anon` (used by logged-out visitors) and stores `p_requester_user_id` as given, without checking it against `auth.uid()`; `link_access_request` lets any authenticated user set their own `access_request_ref` once to any uuid. The admin screens match requests to profiles on these values, so the match is a convenience, not proof of identity.
- **S-4. Several admin-only RPCs are executable by `PUBLIC`/`anon`** (`approve_batch_join_request`, `reject_batch_join_request`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `get_admin_pending_batch_requests`); each is protected by an internal `is_admin()` check in the live body. This is noted as surface, not as a defect.
- **S-5. `admin_audit_log` accepts direct admin inserts** (policy `Admins can insert own audit logs`), guarded against update/delete by trigger `trg_guard_admin_audit_log_*`.

- **Round 2 finding 6.4 is withdrawn in its stated form** and replaced by the C-table rows 6.4/6.7. The Round 2 text is unchanged.
- **Round 4 finding 2.9** ("Add raises the batch count by one"): consistent with the live function bodies, still not observed in the production UI.
- **Round 2 finding 5.3's mention of `notes.custom_course` is corrected:** the column does not exist.

A small follow-up diagnostic could classify the 59 rows by card creation date, card source/batch and creator role, with counts only. This needs QA's pass and a separate Founder authorization by hash; nothing is proposed beyond that.
4. Whether any student can currently create a custom course in the UI (code trace not yet done); live data suggests essentially none.
5. The admission-journey matrix (Order 1 / Order 2, QA's state list, dialogue wording, notifications) and design briefs A and B, which remain downstream of QA's review of this evidence and a Founder decision.

## Round 11 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

None.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **RUN 1A and RUN 1B are readable transcriptions, not machine-valid raw JSON.** Their headers disclose that chat transport doubled backslash escapes, and the saved RUN 1A also contains invalid `\|` escapes; both fenced payloads fail strict JSON parsing without an in-memory repair. The hashes therefore freeze Claude's transcriptions, not the authoritative production result-cell bytes. The function source remains manually reviewable and internally consistent, but future evidence should preserve a raw export unchanged and place any readable rendering beside it under a separate hash.

2. **S-1 and S-2 establish permissive RLS policy expressions, not an effective direct-write path.** RUN 6 did not capture table grants or effective `INSERT`/`DELETE` privilege for `study_group_members`. A policy cannot grant a privilege that the role does not hold, and no client-role execution test was supplied. Carry these into the batch-membership design brief as an OPEN security question requiring a live effective-privilege check and, if privileges exist, a rollback-safe behavioral test. Do not yet state that a group admin or student can bypass the RPCs directly.

3. **S-5 is likewise unsupported as a current exposure and is contrary to the latest repo deployment intent.** RUN 6 proves that an INSERT policy still exists on `admin_audit_log`, but it does not show an INSERT grant. `docs/database/sprint8.8.5b4/14_SCHEMA_close_direct_client_writes.sql:14` revokes that grant, and its test expects direct admin INSERT to fail. Treat the live capability as OPEN until effective privileges are queried; do not carry "accepts direct admin inserts" into a design brief as fact.

4. **Two "confirmed live" explanations depend partly on repo evidence.** RUN 1B proves that `remove_from_my_cards` calls `suspend_card` for active/mastered review rows, but the live `suspend_card` body was not captured; the repo body changes the status to `suspended`. RUN 8 independently proves that this production snapshot has zero mismatches in `removed` or `course_archived` state. Likewise RUN 4 proves that `trg_course_change_archive_restore` exists, but not its live function body. The point-6 amendment is well supported, and finding 5.4 is directionally supported, but those behavioral clauses should be labelled live-observed plus VERIFIED-in-repo rather than wholly confirmed live.

5. **S-3 needs a narrower UI statement.** `submit_access_request` accepts a client-supplied `requester_user_id`, and `link_access_request` accepts a bearer ref token, as stated. The current Access Requests UI correlates ordinary requests using client-supplied email or `ref_token = profiles.access_request_ref`; it does not generally match on `requester_user_id`. The broader conclusion still holds: the displayed match is workflow correlation, not independently verified identity.

6. **The live batch schema has no UNIQUE constraint on `study_groups.invite_token`.** The UUID default makes collision unlikely, but uniqueness, expiry, rotation and revocation are not database-enforced. Carry this structural fact into the batch-membership design brief with the existing intended-recipient gap.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid. All ten files exist and match the status-block short hashes: 1A `74ee5ffb1c45`, 1B `70c95644651c`, 2 `e12aa7da8988`, 3 `31eac9449662`, 4 `6291fa81a417`, 5A `87dc8b19bb29`, 5B `70b0e3a9160f`, 6 `f128b2607cf6`, 7 `f5054021b0b0`, and 8 `b3880ba1eb58`.
- Every evidence file identifies production, execution date `04/10/2026`, the authorized diagnostic hash `37641a6fd173`, and its run number. No run records an error or exposes a student name, email, phone number or identifier.
- RUN 1B confirms that live `get_due_forecast`, `get_due_forecast_buckets` and `get_study_queue` omit enrollment, while live `get_my_cards` requires an active enrollment for own and external cards. Their predicates support RUN 8's comparison.
- RUN 8 supports the amended point-6 diagnosis: 4,138 badge rows, 4,079 Review-page rows, and 59 mismatches belonging to one student, all own cards with no enrollment row; no removed/course-archived mismatch appears in this snapshot. The stale client-side badge remains the primary supported explanation for the Founder-reported behavior.
- RUNs 3-7 support the stated aggregate counts and structural findings for study sessions, course identity, memberships, access requests and roles. Totals reconcile internally, and the RUN 5B table list satisfies Round 7's partitioned-table condition for the live catalog observed.
- The live `remove_group_member` body conclusively shows group-admin-membership authorization, hard deletion, and no audit or notification. RUN 2 shows client EXECUTE only for `authenticated` among application roles.
- The live-versus-repo comparisons for `get_my_cards`, the forecast/queue functions, `join_group_by_token`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `get_admin_batch_groups`, `get_admin_pending_batch_requests` and `admin_read_profiles` are supported by the captured bodies and cited repo copies; no material semantic difference was found.
- S-3 and S-4 are supported by the captured function bodies and grants. S-4 is an exposed EXECUTE surface with internal authorization checks, not evidence of an authorization bypass.

### Residual risks

- The authoritative production cells are not stored byte-for-byte, so QA cannot independently prove that the long RUN 1A/1B transcriptions are complete beyond their disclosed provenance, coherent structure and comparison with repository copies.
- The single `unexpected_other_batch_or_member_actions` row remains unresolved; no removal conclusion is drawn from it.
- The 59 own-card rows without enrollment remain unexplained. A counts-only follow-up diagnostic is advisable before designing or implementing point 6, but it does not block the admission/access or course-identity/reporting design briefs A and B.
- This is one production snapshot and does not reproduce the Founder's individual session or establish future behavior.
- The status-block "Agreed decisions" list still requires explicit Founder confirmation.
- No design, implementation, deployment, commit/push or additional SQL is authorized by this disposition.

### Disposition: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

QA recommends Gate 4 (execution verified) for the exact read-only diagnostic `37641a6fd173`: all ten authorized runs are represented by matching, no-error evidence files. This recommendation verifies execution only, not every interpretation in Round 10 and not live-verification Gate 7. The Founder may decide whether Claude proceeds to design briefs A and B. Carry S-3 into the admission/access brief; carry the live removal findings, invite-token constraint gap, and S-1/S-2 as explicitly OPEN privilege questions into the later batch-membership brief. Do not carry S-5 as a current exposure without a live effective-privilege check. Hand back as `AWAITING-FOUNDER`.

**Scope.** The admission-journey matrix for points 3 and 4 (section A), design brief A, admission and content access (section B), and design brief B, course identity and reporting (section C). ### A. Admission-journey matrix (points 3 and 4)
Terms: **Tier B** = `account_type = 'self_registered'`; **enrolled** = `account_type = 'enrolled'`. Dialogue wording is quoted from the code or the live function bodies.

**Where the Tier B gate is enforced (VERIFIED in the code found, OPEN live).** The only gates located are client-side: the note lock `isTierBViewer = self_registered && !isOwner && author is professor` (`NoteDetail.jsx:166`) and the study-set preview mode (`StudyMode.jsx:77,515-518`). No browsable-set function in the repo references `account_type`. Whether row-level security or RPCs enforce the same rule was not captured in the production run (policies on notes, flashcards and decks were not in scope), so "the lock is UI-only" is OPEN until those policies and `get_browsable_*` bodies are read live.
**Not traced:** the Educators application flow (`Educators.jsx`), and email-confirmation behaviour when Supabase email confirmation is on (assumed on, per `CLAUDE.md` section 6).

### B. Design brief A — admission and content access (points 3 and 4, with the batch invite and removal threads)
**B1. Problems the matrix proves.** (1) The student account, the access request and the batch request are three records with no reliable link, so the admin identifies people by guesswork (rows 3, 8, 10, 20). (2) "Joining a batch" and "having content access" are separate in the data but promised together in the wording (rows 4, 6, 9). (3) The invite is an unlabelled, permanent, non-unique token with no recipient (rows 3, 18). (4) Removal and rejection leave no trace and tell nobody (rows 5, 17). (5) The access queue never closes and matches on text (rows 8, 9).

**B2. Model (PROPOSAL, per QA's separation of concepts).** Keep these as independent concepts, never one flag:
- **Provenance** of content: official ICAI material versus creator material. Today `content_creators` and `content_creator_id` exist only for revenue-share attribution (`DATABASE_SCHEMA.md:72,241`), not for access.
- **Access policy** per item: `free`, `private`, `preview`, `restricted`; official material is always `free`.
- **Entitlement**: a record saying who gets access, from whom, why, until when, and whether revoked. Grant origin is one of: creator direct, batch rule, delegate, promotion, externally fulfilled (never labelled "paid" unless a real transaction is recorded).
- **Batch membership**, **My Study enrollment** and **authorship** remain their own things. A batch grants content only through an explicit entitlement rule, never implicitly.
- v1 has **no in-app payment**; "creator-granted" or "externally fulfilled" wording only.
- Admin override is an audited emergency action, not the routine path.

**B3. Admission journey, target behaviour (PROPOSAL).**
1. **Invite records instead of one bare token.** Each invite has an id, batch, label (for example "Inter May 27 – WhatsApp group A"), created by and when, optional expiry, revoke flag, and optional intended recipient (account or verified email). The shareable cohort link stays; recipient-bound invites are an option for known students. Single-use links are deferred until a need is confirmed (QA's earlier position).
2. **Every join records its source:** the membership stores the invite id it came through, so the pending list can show "via <label>".
3. **Pending list shows what the admin needs:** student name, the student's own course and institution, the batch's course, via-label, request time, and a "course differs from batch" marker. Email visibility is a decision (D3).
4. **Two ways, one rule on content.** Joining a batch never silently grants content; the approval message says exactly what the batch gives and what it does not.
5. **Access requests become linkable.** Match on the signed-in user id captured server-side (not client-supplied) as well as email, normalized to lower case; granting access closes the matching request; duplicates collapse.
6. **Removal (for the later batch-membership sprint).** Reuse and harden `remove_group_member`: platform admins allowed (assigned professors view only); a preserved `removed` state with reason and timestamp instead of deletion; audit entry in the same transaction; student notified (Founder's earlier rule: direct add notifies, rejecting a request does not); study history untouched; a later use of an old link creates a fresh request showing "previously removed on <date>", never a silent reactivation. Whether the table-level privileges and the open S-1/S-2 policy questions make direct deletes possible is OPEN and must be settled live first.

**B4. Dialogue wording (draft copy for audit).**
- Join page, signed in, not a member: "Request to join <batch> (<course> · <institution>). Your admin will review your request."
- After requesting: "Request sent to <institution>. You'll get a notification when it's reviewed."
- Already a member: "You're already in <batch>."
- Not approved (only if the Founder reverses the earlier no-notification rule): "Your request to join <batch> wasn't approved. Contact your institute if you think this is a mistake."
- Approved: "You're in <batch>. You can now see this batch's shared content." plus, for a Tier B student: "Some professor content needs separate access. Request it here."
- Removed: "You've been removed from <batch>. Your study history is kept."
- Access wall (replaces "Full access coming soon"): "This content needs access from its creator or your institute. Request access" and the form prefilled from the profile.
- Professor on a batch link: "Batch access for professors is set by an admin. Ask your admin to assign you."

**B5. Migration and compatibility.** `account_type` stays as the platform "full content access" flag during the transition (240 enrolled, 16 Tier B students, RUN 7); the entitlement model starts empty and is populated by a reviewed migration of existing grants, decided separately. The existing admin queue keeps working for platform-level access until the creator-access epic ships (design now, build after 8.8.6, as agreed).

**B6. SQL and enforcement implications (no SQL written).** New: an invite table, a column linking memberships to an invite, a removal state and audit in the removal function, an RPC that returns an outcome for Add. Enforcement of any access policy must be in RLS and the `get_browsable_*` functions together, not only in the UI. The matrix shows today's gate is UI-only in the places found (OPEN live). Each change needs its own SQL file, rollback and tests run as real roles.

**B7. Decisions needed from the Founder (with recommendation).**
- **D1.** Invite model: labelled invites with expiry and revoke, recipient binding optional. Recommend yes; single-use deferred.
- **D2.** Should rejecting a request notify the student? Recommend: keep the Founder's earlier rule (no notification) but show the state when the student reopens the link ("Your request is not active; ask your institute"). Needs a Founder choice.
- **D3.** Pending list: show the student's email, or only name, course and via-label? Recommend course and via-label always; email only on the admin's explicit "details" action.
- **D4.** Should approving a Tier B student into a batch be allowed? Recommend: allow but show a clear banner "needs content access", and align bulk and single rules so they behave the same.
- **D5.** Replace the "Full access coming soon" wall wording as drafted, and prefill the form. Recommend yes.
- **D6.** Entitlement wording: "creator-granted / externally fulfilled" only. Recommend yes.
OPEN (not a decision): whether the lock is enforced by RLS live; suspended-account behaviour on join; Educators flow.

### C. Design brief B — course identity and reporting (points 5 and 10)
**C1. What the live data and code show.** (1) Platform courses are exactly three disciplines: CA Final (5 subjects), CA Foundation (4), CA Intermediate (8) (RUN 5A). (2) `profiles.course_level` is text; its CHECK includes the clause `length(course_level) > 0`, so any non-empty text passes (RUN 5B); 255 of 262 profiles match a discipline, 2 are blank, 5 hold other text (RUN 5A). (3) Signup offers nine labels (CA, CMA, CS) plus custom text; Profile Settings offers only the three CA courses (matrix row 22). CMA and CS labels have no discipline. (4) There is no student course registry in use (`profile_courses`: professors only), no custom-course entity, and no `custom_course` column anywhere; custom subjects are almost unused (1 card, 3 notes) (RUN 5A/5B). (5) The product is single-active-course: switching archives enrollments and the queue filters by the current course (RUN 4, RUN 1B). (6) `study_sessions` has no course or subject; about 95% of recorded hours are offline manual (1,003 h of 1,051 h), of which 431 rows have no category (RUN 3). (7) Offline logging is timer-only: stop, optional recovery, then a required category picker "What were you studying?" with five buttons (`StudyTimerWidget.jsx:28-34,270-300`), all ending at `confirmCategory` (`StudyTimerContext.jsx:208-230`). (8) Progress already has a Subject Mastery table for the current course (`Progress.jsx:582-584`).

**C2. Course identity (PROPOSAL).** One stable identity per kind, never free text where an entity exists:
- **Platform course and subject:** reference `disciplines.id` and `subjects.id`. Display name by live join, because platform names are admin-controlled and a rename is a correction; no label snapshot. A deactivated course keeps its row, so history stays readable.
- **Custom course or subject:** no entity exists and almost no data uses it. v1 stores normalized text (trimmed, case-folded for grouping, original kept for display, length-limited). Promotion to a real entity is deferred until there is demand.
- **General:** an explicit classification, not a NULL.
- **Legacy:** NULL, only for rows before the change. Never back-filled from the student's current course.
- The picker never derives its list from `profiles.course_level` alone (QA Round 3).

**C3. Logging foundation, point 10 (PROPOSAL).**
- **Columns on `study_sessions`** (all nullable for legacy): a classification kind (platform, custom, general), `discipline_id`, `subject_id`, and normalized custom course and subject text. For new manual rows exactly one valid classification is required, enforced by a `NOT VALID` CHECK, so the 1,395 existing rows are untouched and legacy stays distinguishable.
- **Subject must belong to the course:** the strongest option is a composite foreign key (`discipline_id`, `subject_id`) to a unique pair on `subjects`, which the database enforces without a trigger. This needs a unique constraint on `subjects(id, discipline_id)` (a new SQL object).
- **One write boundary:** all manual paths (normal stop, recovery log-full or log-less, restored pending log) end at `confirmCategory`, so one change covers them; no direct-entry form exists today (QA Round 5 wording adopted).
- **Picker, fast on mobile:** a compact "Course" row above the category buttons, preselected to the current course, with "General" one tap away and subject optional; the chosen course is always visible before Save; last subject per course remembered on the device only.
- **In-app sessions (study_mode, practice_mode):** not classified in v1. Mixed-subject Review sessions have no single subject; recording course only would be a separate decision. Reports must show classified, General and unclassified separately.
- **Not changed:** existing totals, `get_study_time_stats`, and professor batch reports (totals only).

**C4. Reporting and Progress by course, point 5 (PROPOSAL).**
- Heatmap tooltip stays concise: in-app, offline and total time (point 7). Course and subject appear in a day-detail drill-down, student-only.
- Progress groups: current course first and expanded; other platform courses collapsed with one summary line; custom courses as their own groups; **General** as a separate group counted in the all-course totals but attributed to no course; **Unassigned / legacy** for NULL rows; nothing is hidden. Headline totals state their scope ("All courses").
- Two measures, never summed: card progress (reviews, mapped to a course through the card's `target_course`) and study time (classified sessions).
- Collapse state is remembered in the browser only.
- Professors: totals only; the course and subject breakdown is not exposed unless a later decision allows it.

**C5. Sequencing and safeguards.** Logging foundation (point 10) ships before the Progress UI (point 5) so classified data accumulates early, but only after this brief is approved. Each SQL change gets its own file, rollback and tests run as real roles, including every existing `study_sessions` constraint and the three source values.

**C6. Decisions needed from the Founder (with recommendation).**
- **E1.** Picker source: the three platform courses plus General plus the student's own previously used custom text, with no new student registry in v1. Recommend yes. Alternative: reuse `profile_courses` for students (heavier; defer).
- **E2.** Platform courses only exist for CA today while signup offers CMA and CS: add CMA and CS disciplines now, or let those students use custom text? Recommend custom text for v1; adding disciplines is a separate catalogue decision.
- **E3.** Fix Profile Settings so it offers the same course labels as Signup, or reduce Signup to the platform courses? This touches who can set which course (the five odd profiles). Needs a Founder choice; recommend aligning Settings to Signup's nine labels.
- **E4.** Should in-app sessions ever get a course? Recommend defer; not needed to start.
- **E5.** Platform names via live join (recommended) versus snapshot; custom text stored as typed plus a normalized grouping key. Recommend as stated.
- **E6.** Professors see totals only (already agreed); confirm no course or subject breakdown for professors in v1. Recommend confirm.
OPEN: what the five non-platform `course_level` values are (counts only were taken); whether any student creates a custom course in the UI today (code trace not done); whether an in-app course attribution is wanted later.

### D. Requests to QA
1. Audit the matrix in section A against the code and the evidence files; say which rows are unsupported or overstated, and whether any scenario in your Round 3 list is missing.
2. Audit brief A: the separation of concepts, the invite model, the removal outline, the draft wording, and the migration note. Say which decisions D1 to D6 you would advise otherwise.
3. Audit brief B: the identity rules in C2, the composite-foreign-key option in C3, the grouping in C4, and decisions E1 to E6.
4. Say whether the OPEN items (RLS enforcement of the Tier B lock; suspended accounts; Educators flow; the five course values) must be closed before either brief goes to the Founder for Gate 1.
5. Confirm that no Gate is approved by this round.

## Round 14 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **Matrix row 10 does not describe the Founder's stated order and refers an existing account through the new-account path.** “Same as #7, then #2-#4” sends a student who signed up before receiving the invite through #2 (“New account via the link”), which cannot occur. It also conflates two independent journeys. The supported order is: existing Tier B account -> content wall/access request (#7-#9), and, when the batch link is used, join request/approval (#3-#4); the current User Management alternative is Grant Access followed by explicit single Add (#11). Revise row 10 and state which of these orders reproduces the Founder’s case.

2. **Brief A regresses the linkage finding already corrected in QA Round 5.** B1 says the account, access request and batch request are “three records with no reliable link.” The batch request is reliably keyed to the profile by `study_group_members.user_id` and a foreign key. An access request can carry `requester_user_id`, email and a ref-token correlation, although the live SECURITY DEFINER submission function trusts the client-supplied user id and the current admin UI ignores it. The proved defect is unreliable identity/correlation for access requests plus missing joins/display in the admin workflow—not absence of any linkage among all three records. B1 and the related matrix wording must use that narrower statement.

3. **Brief A has no durable request-attempt model for its promised rejection, removal and rejoin behaviour.** B3 stores only one `invite_id` on the membership while the live table has `UNIQUE (group_id, user_id)`. A preserved `removed` row can be reused, but that overwrites the source/current attempt and cannot preserve repeated request/removal history. Rejection is not given a preserved state or history object at all, yet D2 promises a meaningful state when the student reopens the link. Define either an append-only join-attempt/history relation or exact membership reuse plus audit semantics that preserve rejection/removal source, actor, reason, time and repeated attempts. B6 must also include the server-owned access-request link/deduplication/closure operations proposed in B3, not only invite/removal/Add objects.

4. **Brief B's NULL semantics are internally inconsistent.** C2 says NULL means legacy and only predates the change; C3 leaves all new `study_mode` and `practice_mode` rows unclassified in v1; C4 then combines NULL rows under “Unassigned / legacy.” New in-app rows would therefore be indistinguishable in that grouping from legacy rows, and NULL would not mean legacy only. Define the report contract by both `source` and classification (for example legacy manual versus currently-unclassified in-app), or classify in-app rows. Preserve the agreed rule that every new manual/offline row has platform, custom or explicit General classification and never silently becomes NULL.

5. **Brief B does not yet define one implementable custom-course identity.** C2/E5 require original display text plus a normalized grouping key, while C3 names only normalized custom-course/custom-subject text columns. E1 also omits a student's current non-platform `profiles.course_level` unless it happened to be used in an earlier session, even though C3 says the picker is preselected to the current course and E2 routes CMA/CS students through custom text. Specify separate display and normalized values (or an equally explicit generated/indexed design), normalization and uniqueness/grouping rules, and include the current non-platform profile course in the picker source. E3 must refer to one shared canonical option source rather than two independently maintained UI lists.

6. **Card progress is not mapped through the stable course identity proposed by C2.** C4 groups card progress by `flashcards.target_course`, a free-text label, while study time uses `discipline_id` for platform courses. A platform rename or spelling drift can split one course or detach card and time measures. Define the authoritative mapping for existing and new cards (including custom text and unmatched legacy labels), migration/fallback behaviour, and how the two measures land in the same displayed course group. They may remain separate measures, but they need the same identity boundary.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Matrix rows 1-9 and 11-22 are materially supported by the cited code and accepted production evidence. Row 8 correctly identifies the final exact client-side email comparison even though `admin_read_profiles` normalizes the search input. Row 21 is properly marked OPEN rather than asserted. Row 18's missing token uniqueness/expiry/revocation controls are also supported.

2. The auditable Round 3 QA block in this file contains no enumerated admission-state list, so I cannot verify the phrase “QA's state list” against a preserved list. Against the scenarios recorded elsewhere in T-001, the matrix is broad; the cross-device/email-confirmation branch remains an inference and suspended-account behaviour remains untraced.

3. D1, D3, D4, D5 and D6 are directionally sound. D1 needs the history correction above. For D2, retaining no rejection notification is consistent with the prior Founder position, but the reopen message requires a durable rejected/inactive state. D4's “allow Tier B with a banner” is coherent only if single, bulk and approval paths use one server-enforced eligibility rule and batch membership remains separate from content entitlement.

4. E2's custom-text treatment for CMA/CS is acceptable for v1 once the identity and picker rules above are explicit. E4 can remain deferred if NULL reporting is disambiguated by source. E5 is sound after its storage contract is specified. E6 matches the agreed professors-totals-only scope. E1 and E3 require revision as described above.

5. The proposed composite foreign key from (`discipline_id`, `subject_id`) to a new unique (`discipline_id`, `id`) or (`id`, `discipline_id`) key on `subjects` is a sound database enforcement option. Its companion CHECK must require both IDs for platform classification, prohibit them for custom/general classification, and permit an optional NULL subject without weakening the course requirement.

6. OPEN-item gating: the live Tier B RLS/RPC enforcement and suspended-account eligibility must be closed, or replaced by an explicit target deny/allow rule with real-role acceptance tests, before Brief A receives Gate 1. The Educators flow may remain outside Gate 1 only if the brief explicitly scopes educator admission out. The five literal non-platform values need not be exposed or known; privacy-safe category counts may inform E2/E3, but a design that handles arbitrary normalized custom text does not depend on them. None of these items blocks Brief B once findings 4-6 are corrected.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff gate was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`. Round 13 is frozen in commit `03568d00683fa2e068c28c300dda752d920797e4`; no `src/` file changed between code baseline `6fc6ceb` and that commit. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` working-tree change was not touched.
- Brief A correctly separates provenance, per-item policy, entitlement, batch membership, My Study enrollment and authorship, and correctly rejects UI-only enforcement and unrecorded “paid” wording.
- The invite expiry/revocation direction, truthful Add outcome, server-owned access-request identity, normalized email, explicit content wording and audited removal direction address demonstrated defects.
- Brief B correctly avoids backfilling historical sessions from a mutable current profile course, keeps General explicit, makes subject optional, preserves separate card/time measures, and sequences the logging foundation before its reporting UI.
- The agreed decisions in the status block remain a working record, not Gate 1 approval. Round 13 does not alter them, although C2-C4's ambiguous NULL treatment must be revised to accurately implement the agreed “NULL = legacy only” rule for new offline logs.

### Residual risks

- The live content-access RLS and browsable-function rules, suspended-account join reachability, and effective direct-delete exposure on group membership remain unknown. No security conclusion should be inferred from the client locks alone.
- Invite-token collision handling, expiry/revocation races, request deduplication boundaries, normalized-text collisions, platform rename behaviour and repeated remove/rejoin cycles need explicit SQL acceptance tests in a later gate.
- No browser journey was executed in this audit; UI conclusions are code- and supplied-evidence-based. No SQL, application code, database, Git state or external service was changed.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise matrix row 10 and B1, complete Brief A's durable request/history and SQL-implication model, and resolve Brief B's NULL, picker/custom identity and card-course mapping contradictions. Then return the revised briefs to QA under a new commit SHA. No gate is approved by Round 13 or this QA round; this disposition is audit advice, not authorization to implement, run SQL, deploy, commit or push.

## Round 15 · Claude · 04/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 14 (REVISION REQUIRED, six blocking findings). All six blocking findings and all six non-blocking points are accepted; there is no pushback this round. ### B. Matrix row 10 (supersedes Round 13 row 10)
The Founder's case, in the Founder's own words (Round 4): an account created *before* the batch invitation; it does not get content access automatically; the student must request access; such users appear in User Management; after access is approved, a batch dropdown appears. The supported order that reproduces it is:
**existing Tier B account -> content wall and access request (rows 7-8) -> admin finds the student (Access Requests tab, or User Management) -> Grant Access (row 9, shown in User Management as the only action for a Tier B row, `AdminDashboard.jsx:1137-1186`) -> the Add-to-batch picker only then appears for the now-enrolled student (`:1183-1185`) -> single Add (row 11).**
The alternative order, for a student whose account already exists and who uses the invite link instead: logged-in join page with "Request to Join" (rows 1 and 3, without the signup steps of row 2) -> admin approval (row 4); the student stays Tier B until access is granted (row 6). Row 2 applies only to a student who has no account when the link is opened and is never part of the Founder's case.
**Gap restated for this order:** in User Management the Tier B row shows name, email, role, "Tier B", join date and Grant Access, with no course, institution or link to the access request, so the admin cannot choose a batch from what the screen shows; the picker appears only after the grant, and then its result is silent (row 11).

### C. Brief A, revised B1 (supersedes Round 13 B1)
**B1. Defects the matrix proves.**
1. **Identity and correlation of access requests, and missing joins in the admin workflow.** A batch request is reliably keyed to the student's profile (`study_group_members.user_id`, a foreign key to `profiles`, RUN 6). An access request can carry `requester_user_id`, an email, and a ref-token correlation, but the live submission function trusts a client-supplied user id (RUN 1A `submit_access_request`) and the admin screen matches only on exact email or ref token, ignoring `requester_user_id` (`AdminDashboard.jsx:1386-1389`). The proved defect is unreliable correlation of access requests plus the absence of any join or display of the relationships in the admin screens (rows 3, 8, 10, 20). It is **not** an absence of linkage among the three records.
2. "Joining a batch" and "having content access" are separate in the data but promised together in the wording (rows 4, 6, 9).
3. The invite is an unlabelled, permanent, non-unique token with no recipient (rows 3, 18).
4. Rejection, removal and re-request leave no durable trace and notify nobody (rows 5, 17).
5. The access queue never closes and has no deduplication (rows 8, 9).

### D. Brief A, revised B3 items 2, 5, 6 and B6 (supersede the same Round 13 items)
**D1. Durable history model (PROPOSAL; answers blocking finding 3).** `UNIQUE (group_id, user_id)` stays (RUN 6), so one row per student and batch continues to be the *current state*; history lives in a separate append-only relation.
- **Current state row (`study_group_members`):** the status set widens from `invited / active / requested / closed` to also include `rejected` and `removed`. Rejection and removal **preserve** the row instead of deleting it. The row's invite reference (if any) points at the invite of the latest attempt.
- **Append-only events (`batch_membership_events`, new):** one row per event: group, user, event kind (`requested`, `approved`, `rejected`, `added_by_admin`, `removed`, `left`, `reopened_request`), actor (null for the student's own action), actor role at the time, reason (optional, length-limited), invite id when the event is a request, and timestamp. Rows are inserted only by SECURITY DEFINER functions in the same transaction as the state change; no client role may update or delete them (the same immutability trigger pattern as `admin_audit_log`, RUN 4). Repeated cycles of request, rejection, removal and re-request therefore keep source, actor, reason, time and every attempt.
- **Reopening the link after rejection or removal:** the server creates a fresh request (status `requested`, event `reopened_request`), never a silent reactivation, and the admin queue shows a short history summary ("previously rejected 12/09; removed 20/09"). Whether a rejected student may re-request immediately or after a cooldown is decision D7 below.
- **Student-facing state:** a student whose status is `rejected` or `removed` sees a plain message on the join page (draft wording in B4, updated for D2 below), which is possible because the state is now durable.
**D2. Revised B6 — what the SQL would have to include (no SQL written).**
1. **Invites:** an invite table (id, batch, label, creator, created at, optional expiry, revoked flag, optional intended recipient), a unique constraint on the token, and create, list and revoke functions.
2. **State and history:** the widened status CHECK; the `batch_membership_events` table with its immutability guard; all of `join_group_by_token`, `approve_batch_join_request`, `reject_batch_join_request`, `admin_bulk_resolve_batch_requests`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `remove_group_member` and `leave_group` rewritten to preserve rows and write events in one transaction. A hardened `remove_group_member` allows platform admins and super_admins, requires a reason, and refuses archived batches.
3. **Truthful Add:** `enroll_user_in_batch_group` returns an outcome (added, already active, reactivated, refused with reason) instead of `void`.
4. **One eligibility rule:** a single server function (role student, not suspended, and the access rule decided in D4) called by single Add, bulk Add, approve and bulk approve, so the four paths behave identically. A Tier B student's batch membership stays separate from content entitlement.
5. **Server-owned access-request operations (the part Round 13 B6 omitted):** `submit_access_request` derives the requester from `auth.uid()` when the caller is signed in and ignores a client-supplied id; the email is normalized to lower case on write; an open request for the same requester or email and scope returns the existing one instead of a duplicate; `link_access_request` validates the token before setting `profiles.access_request_ref`; `admin_grant_access` marks the matching open requests closed in the same transaction and audits it; the admin screen matches by requester id, normalized email and ref token on the server, not by exact client-side comparison.
6. **Row-presence readers:** a preserved `rejected` or `removed` row must never grant anything. Live policy `sg_select_member` permits any user with *any* membership row to read the `study_groups` row, which includes `invite_token` (RUN 6; whether column-level privileges narrow this was not captured), and `sgm_select_own` lets a user read their own rows; both ignore `status`. Every reader of `study_group_members` (policies, `batch_group_access_denial`, `get_my_batch_groups`, content-sharing paths and the front-end direct selects) must be audited so that only `active` confers access, and the invite token must not be readable through a stale row. This is both a design requirement and an acceptance test.
7. **Privileges:** if effective INSERT or DELETE privileges exist for `authenticated` on `study_group_members` (open S-1/S-2), they must be closed or the policies tightened so that the functions are the only write path.
8. **Acceptance tests, run as real roles, in a later gate:** token collision and revoke or expiry races, concurrent approvals, duplicate requests, repeated remove-and-rejoin cycles, rejected and removed re-requests, a stale row not granting access, single and bulk eligibility parity, and request deduplication boundaries.
**D3. Revised decisions (supersede the named Round 13 items).**
- **D1.** Labelled invites with expiry and revoke, recipient binding optional, **and each request attempt linked to its invite through the events relation (D1 above)**. Recommend yes.
- **D2.** No push notification on rejection (the Founder's earlier rule), **but rejection is a durable `rejected` state with history, so the student sees "Your request to join <batch> wasn't approved. Contact your institute." when reopening the link and the admin sees the earlier attempt.** Needs a Founder choice.
- **D4.** Batch membership and content entitlement stay separate. If a Tier B student may be approved into a batch (recommended, with a banner "needs content access"), that rule must be **one server-enforced eligibility rule used by single Add, bulk Add, approve and bulk approve**, not a screen-level difference.
- **D7 (new).** May a rejected student re-request immediately, or only after a cooldown? Recommend immediately, with the history visible to the admin; a cooldown is a later refinement.
- D3, D5 and D6 are unchanged.
**D4. OPEN items and Gate 1 for brief A (QA non-blocking 6).** Before brief A can go to the Founder for Gate 1, these must be closed live or replaced by an explicit target deny/allow rule with real-role acceptance tests: (a) whether the Tier B content lock is enforced by row-level security or `get_browsable_*` (the repo shows it only in the screens, matrix section "Where the Tier B gate is enforced"); (b) whether a suspended account can reach and use the join functions; (c) the effective table privileges on `study_group_members`, `study_groups` and `admin_audit_log`. A single follow-up read-only diagnostic can answer all three, plus capture the live bodies of `suspend_card`, `unsuspend_card`, `fn_course_change_archive_restore` and `batch_group_access_denial` and a counts-only look at the 59 own-card rows (Round 12 C(i)). **Scope statement:** the Educators application flow stays outside brief A and its Gate 1; educator admission is unchanged by this design. The five non-platform `course_level` values are not needed.

### F. Brief B, revised C2, C3 and part of C6 (supersede the same Round 13 items)
**F1. Classification columns on `study_sessions` (answers blocking findings 4 and 5).** All new columns are nullable; rows written before the change keep NULL.
- `classification` text, allowed values `platform`, `custom`, `general`.
- `discipline_id` and `subject_id` for platform courses, with a foreign key to `disciplines(id)` and a composite foreign key (`discipline_id`, `subject_id`) to a new unique pair on `subjects`. A NULL `subject_id` is allowed and does not weaken the course requirement.
- **Custom identity, implementable and explicit:** `custom_course_label` (as typed, trimmed, 1-60 characters) and `custom_course_key`, a database-generated stored column computed from the label (lower case, internal whitespace collapsed, trimmed). The same pair exists for the optional custom subject (`custom_subject_label`, `custom_subject_key`). Because the key is generated by the database from the label, the client cannot make the two disagree. There is no uniqueness constraint (no entity exists); grouping is by key; the display label of a group is the label of its most recent row. No accent folding or fuzzy matching in v1, so "CFA L1" and "CFA Level 1" stay separate; the picker's suggestions from the student's own earlier labels are what prevent drift.
- **Companion CHECK (NOT VALID, so existing rows are untouched), per QA non-blocking 5:** `platform` requires `discipline_id`, forbids the custom columns, and allows an optional subject; `custom` requires `custom_course_label`, forbids `discipline_id` and `subject_id`, and allows an optional custom subject label; `general` requires all of them to be NULL. For `source = 'manual'` the classification must not be NULL. For `source` in (`study_mode`, `practice_mode`) the classification must be NULL in v1 and may be relaxed later if in-app classification is ever approved.
**F2. Report contract (answers blocking finding 4).** NULL means only "no classification recorded". Reports separate rows by `source` and classification, so NULL never has two meanings in one group:
- **Offline, classified:** manual rows with `platform` (one group per discipline), `custom` (one group per key), or `general`.
- **Offline, unassigned (legacy):** manual rows with a NULL classification. The database constraint above makes this possible only for rows written before the change, so "NULL = legacy only" holds for offline logs, as agreed.
- **In-app, not classified by course:** `study_mode` and `practice_mode` rows, before and after the change, in v1.
- **All-course totals** add every group once; General is included and is attributed to no course. Existing totals and professor reports are unchanged.
**F3. Picker source (E1, revised; answers blocking finding 5).** The course options are: the student's **current course**, always first and preselected (a platform course maps to its discipline; a non-platform current course such as a CMA label is offered as a custom entry with that exact label, so the preselect works for those students too); the other active platform courses; the student's own earlier custom labels (distinct by key, from their own sessions); **General**; and "Other…" for new text. No student course registry is created in v1. The subject list follows the chosen course; for a custom course it offers the student's earlier custom subjects for that key, and subject stays optional.
**F4. One canonical option source (E3, revised).** Signup, Profile Settings, the access-request form and the logging picker currently keep separate lists (nine labels in Signup, ten in the access form, three in Profile Settings; matrix row 22). v1 requires one shared definition of the platform labels and the allowed non-platform labels, read by all four. The exact placement (a shared front-end module backed by the `disciplines` table and the existing nine-label rule) is an implementation decision after approval; the Founder chooses the canonical list in E3 below.
**F5. Revised C6 decisions.**
- **E1.** Picker source as in F3 (current course first, platform courses, own earlier custom labels, General, Other). Recommend yes.
- **E3.** One canonical course list for Signup, Profile Settings, the access form and the picker: recommend the nine labels (three CA, three CMA, three CS) plus custom text, defined once. Needs a Founder choice.
- **E5.** Platform names by live join (no snapshot, see G below); custom text stored as the typed label plus a database-generated grouping key (F1). Recommend as stated.
- E2, E4 and E6 are unchanged: CMA and CS through custom text in v1; in-app classification deferred (now safe because reports are separated by source, F2); professors see totals only.

### G. Brief B, revised C4 card mapping (supersedes the card-progress bullet of Round 13 C4; answers blocking finding 6)
Both measures must resolve to the same course identity before they are shown in the same group. The authoritative mapping for a **card** is, in order: (1) `flashcards.discipline_id`, if set, is the platform course; (2) otherwise the card's `subject_id`, through `subjects.discipline_id`; (3) otherwise `target_course` text that equals a discipline name; (4) otherwise a non-empty `target_course` is a custom group, grouped with the **same normalization key as study time** (F1); (5) otherwise the card is unassigned and is shown as such. Evidence for existing data: all 2,850 flashcards have a `subject_id` and a `target_course` equal to a platform name, so steps 1-3 resolve every card today and steps 4-5 are empty for flashcards (RUN 5A); one note has a non-platform `target_course` and three notes use a custom subject (RUN 5A). How many cards have a NULL `flashcards.discipline_id` was not measured; it is added to the follow-up diagnostic as a count (D4 above) and the fallback chain makes the result safe either way.
**Rename rule:** platform course names are identifiers used by `profiles.course_level`, `flashcards.target_course` and the live queue and forecast filters, which compare text for equality (RUN 1B). Renaming a discipline is therefore **not supported in v1** without a coordinated data migration, which is a separate project. Because names do not change, displaying platform names by live join cannot drift, and the two measures cannot split one course across two labels.
**Result:** card progress and study time are still separate, never-summed measures, but both land in the same group because both resolve to `discipline_id` (platform) or the same normalized key (custom); anything that resolves to neither lands in "Unassigned" and is shown.

## Round 16 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **Brief A still treats unverified correlation fields as if they could safely identify or deduplicate an account.** D2 item 5 says an open request for the same requester **or email** and scope returns the existing request, and that the server matches by requester id, normalized email and ref token. The accepted evidence says email and bearer ref tokens are workflow correlations, not proof of identity; a signed-in caller may type another person's email, and an anonymous request may contain an existing account's email. Define precedence and conflict handling: an authenticated `auth.uid()` is authoritative; a contradictory email must never merge or grant another account; email-only matches may be suggestions, not automatic identity; and a ref-token claim must validate the matching request, be atomic and have explicit one-account/replay semantics. Request closure by `admin_grant_access` must use the authoritative link or an explicit admin-selected request, not a broad email `OR` match.

2. **The durable membership design does not yet cover every state transition or protect its sensitive history.** The event list omits the existing staff-created `invited` transition and batch archive/restore transitions that produce or consume `closed`; the SQL-impact list rewrites membership functions but does not include the archive/restore writers. It also says clients cannot update/delete events but does not define who may SELECT actor, reason and history fields. Add the complete event/state-transition table, including idempotent/no-op outcomes, and least-privilege read rules for the affected student, authorized batch/platform administrators and everyone else. For recipient-bound invites, `join_group_by_token` must atomically enforce recipient, expiry and revocation, rather than merely store those invite attributes; the real-role tests must cover wrong-recipient and revoke/expiry races.

3. **Brief B's card mapping can still choose the wrong stable course.** G gives `flashcards.discipline_id` priority over the discipline implied by `subject_id`, but RUN 5A proves only that every card has a subject and platform-name `target_course`; it does not prove that a non-NULL `discipline_id` agrees with that subject or text. Counting only NULL discipline IDs does not close this. The assertion that the fallback is safe “either way” is therefore unsupported. Measure NULL and disagreement counts, choose one canonical source, define conflict handling for legacy rows, and enforce course/subject consistency for new platform cards (for example with the same composite-key pattern). New platform cards should not depend on text equality as their identity fallback.

4. **The picker cannot always preserve the current profile course under the proposed 60-character contract.** Live `profiles.course_level` accepts any non-empty text (RUN 5B), and Signup's custom-course input has no maximum length, while F1 restricts `custom_course_label` to 60 characters and F3 promises that the current course is always offered exactly and preselected. Define one length/validation contract across profile, signup and session classification, plus a non-destructive rule for existing over-limit values. A privacy-safe count of over-limit profile values is sufficient; literal values are not needed.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Revised matrix row 10 now accurately separates the Founder's existing-account path from the invite-link path and is supported by the cited UI. The Add picker is rendered only after the student becomes enrolled, and its current success path remains silent.

2. Revised B1 correctly states the linkage defect: batch membership has a profile foreign key, while access-request identity is unreliable and the current admin workflow omits available relationships. This resolves QA Round 14 finding 2.

3. The current-state-plus-append-only-events direction is appropriate, and `rejected`/`removed` states resolve the earlier inability to display a durable outcome. D7's recommended immediate re-request with visible history is reasonable. The final design should distinguish self, system and administrator actors rather than overloading a NULL actor if system-generated events are possible.

4. F1-F3 resolve the earlier NULL contradiction: every new manual row is classified as platform, custom or General; legacy manual NULL rows remain separately reportable; and in-app NULL rows are grouped by source rather than called legacy. The generated key and separate display label are implementable. The eventual grouping query needs a deterministic tie-breaker when selecting the most recent display label.

5. F4/E3's single shared course-option definition is the right direction. The implementation artifact must define the union and precedence of live `disciplines`, the allowed CMA/CS labels and arbitrary custom text rather than maintaining another copied list.

6. The scenario checklist is now preserved in T-001 and covers the material states. Its attribution to an unsaved 02/10/2026 chat message cannot be independently verified, so it should be treated as Round 15's reconstructed checklist, not as independently authenticated earlier QA text.

7. Brief B remains architecturally independent of Brief A, but it is not yet ready for Founder Gate 1 because blocking findings 3 and 4 affect its core identity contract. Once those are corrected, Brief B may proceed independently while Brief A waits for its live security diagnostic and its own revisions.

8. The status block's `Artifacts under review` entry still names Round 13 rather than Round 15. The audit version was nevertheless immutable and identifiable as commit `2a600d6c2d288ac3961f44a1b6c12c4e01350306`. Claude should correct its owned artifact index at the next handoff so the status block itself identifies the active version.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid (`AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`), and Round 15 was reviewed at commit `2a600d6c2d288ac3961f44a1b6c12c4e01350306`. No `src/` file changed between code baseline `6fc6ceb` and that commit.
- D2's reader audit correctly recognizes that preserving rejected/removed rows changes the security boundary: row presence must not confer access, and all policies, functions and direct readers must require an active state.
- The proposal keeps batch membership and content entitlement independent and calls for one server-side eligibility rule across single Add, bulk Add, single approval and bulk approval.
- F1's classification CHECK shape, composite course/subject enforcement for sessions, generated normalization key and source-separated report contract are materially stronger and preserve legacy rows without backfilling them from mutable profile state.
- The rename rule correctly identifies that current queue/forecast behaviour still relies on exact text. Treating a platform rename as a coordinated migration rather than a harmless label edit is appropriate; the later SQL/frontend gate must make that operational restriction explicit and test it.
- Round 15 does not approve any gate, and the status block's agreed decisions remain accurately described as a working record rather than design approval.

### Residual risks

- Brief A still depends on live evidence for Tier B RLS/RPC enforcement, suspended-account eligibility and effective table privileges before Gate 1. The Educators flow is explicitly out of scope, which is acceptable.
- At audit start, `docs/database/step0-T-001/01_DIAGNOSTIC_follow-up_privileges_enforcement_and_gaps.sql` existed as an untracked file. It is not identified in the status block, was not part of commit `2a600d6`, and the status says the Founder has not yet decided whether it should be drafted. It was not audited or modified and must not be treated as reviewed or authorized for execution.
- Invite/event retention, deletion on account erasure, reason-text privacy, custom-key collation behaviour, legacy card conflicts and coordinated platform renames still need explicit SQL, rollback and real-role tests in later gates.
- The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` change was not touched. No SQL, application code, database, Git state or external service was changed by this audit.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise Brief A's identity-correlation and complete event/invite authorization contracts, and revise Brief B's card-source consistency and course-text length contracts. Then return the exact revised round under a new commit SHA. Neither brief is ready for Gate 1 yet, no gate is approved, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, run SQL, deploy, commit or push.

### A. Dispositions
| # | Disposition | Fixed in |
|---|---|---|
| Blocking 1 | Accepted: email and ref tokens are correlations, not identity | Section B (supersedes Round 15 D2 item 5) |
| Blocking 2 | Accepted: transitions incomplete (staff invite, archive and restore), no read rules, invite recipient not enforced | Section C (supersedes the event list in Round 15 D1 and extends D2) |
| Blocking 3 | Accepted: card mapping priority order and "safe either way" were unsupported | Section D (supersedes Round 15 G) |
