### B. Brief A: identity correlation contract (supersedes Round 15 D2 item 5)
Principle: an authenticated `auth.uid()` is the only authoritative identity. Typed email and bearer tokens are correlations that may *suggest* a link but never create, merge or grant one automatically.
1. **Authenticated caller:** the requester stored on an access request is `auth.uid()`. A client-supplied requester id is ignored. This closes the live hole that `submit_access_request` stores whatever id the client sends (RUN 1A).
2. **Typed email is a contact claim only.** It is stored lower-cased as a contact email and is never used to merge accounts, close requests or grant access. If a signed-in user types an email that differs from their account email, the request is flagged "contact email differs from account email" for the admin and no link to the typed email's account is made.
3. **Anonymous caller:** the request has no identity. Even if its email matches an existing account, the system never links them automatically. The admin screen may show "possible match (email)" as a suggestion; linking happens only through an explicit admin action that writes an audited link (actor, request, account).
4. **Ref-token claim (`link_access_request`):** the token must belong to an existing, unclaimed request of a permitted type; the claim is atomic (a conditional update that succeeds only while the request is unclaimed); the first valid claim wins and records the claiming account; a repeat by the same account is a no-op success; a different account gets a generic refusal; a token can never overwrite an existing link. `profiles.access_request_ref` is set only by a successful claim, not by any arbitrary value (live today any signed-in user may set it once to any uuid, RUN 1A).
5. **Deduplication, without cross-kind merging:** for authenticated requests, one open request per requester and scope (a repeat returns the existing one); for anonymous requests, one open request per lower-cased email and scope among anonymous requests only ("already received"). An anonymous request is never merged into an account's request.
6. **Closure:** `admin_grant_access` closes only (a) the request the admin explicitly selects and (b) open requests whose authoritative link is the granted user (requester id, or a successful token claim). It never closes by a broad email match. The closed request ids are recorded in the audit entry.
7. **Admin screen:** account-linked requests show as linked; email-only coincidences show as suggestions; all matching is computed on the server from normalized values, not by an exact client-side comparison.

### C. Brief A: complete membership transitions, history and authorization (supersedes the event list in Round 15 D1; extends D2)
**C1. Scope.** The state-and-events model applies to **batch groups** only; non-batch groups are unchanged. States are `invited`, `requested`, `active`, `closed` (existing) plus `rejected`, `removed` and `left` (new). The archive writers are verified in the repo: `archive_batch_group` sets every `requested` or `invited` row to `closed` with reason `batch_archived` and leaves `active` rows unchanged; `restore_batch_group` clears the archive marker and "does not revive closed requests/invites" (`sprint8.1/02_FUNCTIONS_archive_restore.sql:95-97,104-111`, VERIFIED-in-repo; live bodies not captured).
**C2. Transition table (PROPOSAL).**
| # | From -> to | Trigger and function | Event kind | Actor kind | No-op or refused outcome (no event written) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | none -> requested | student with a valid invite, `join_group_by_token` | requested | self | already requested: "already_requested"; already active: "already_active" |
| 2 | none -> invited | staff invites a student to a batch (0 such rows today in batch groups, RUN 6) | invited | admin or group_admin | row exists: refused with outcome |
| 3 | invited -> active | the invited student uses the link | accepted_invite | self | - |
| 4 | requested -> active | approve, single or bulk | approved | admin | already resolved: "already_resolved" |
| 5 | requested -> rejected | reject, single or bulk (reason optional) | rejected | admin | already resolved: "already_resolved" |
| 6 | none, rejected, removed or left -> active | admin direct add, `enroll_user_in_batch_group` | added_by_admin | admin | already active: "already_active" |
| 7 | rejected, removed or left -> requested | student reuses the link | reopened_request | self | already requested: "already_requested" |
| 8 | active -> removed | admin or group admin removes, hardened `remove_group_member` (reason required, archived batch refused) | removed | admin or group_admin | not active: refused "not_active" |
| 9 | active -> left | student leaves, `leave_group` | left | self | not active: refused |
| 10 | requested or invited -> closed | batch archived, `archive_batch_group` | closed_by_archive (one per affected row) | admin | already archived: no events |
| 11 | closed -> requested | student reuses the link after restore | reopened_request | self | - |
| 12 | active stays active | archive and restore (no member change) | none per member (group-level audit only) | - | - |
| 13 | none -> active (staff) | admin or super_admin uses a link | joined_as_staff | self | row exists: no-op |
**Actor kinds** (QA non-blocking 3): `self`, `admin` (platform admin or super_admin), `group_admin`, `system` (backfills, migrations or automation). `actor_id` is NULL only for `system`; a NULL is never used to mean "student".
**C3. Who can read events (least privilege).** The events relation has row-level security enabled, **no client policies and no direct client privileges**; reads happen only through SECURITY DEFINER read functions that return:
- the **affected student**: their own events only, with event kind, time and batch name; **not** the actor, the reason text or the invite label;
- **platform admin and super_admin**: all fields for any batch;
- a **batch group admin member** (active, role admin): all fields for that batch only; the actor is shown by kind, not by identity;
- an **assigned professor**: nothing (roster view only, per 8.8.5d);
- everyone else: nothing.
Retention, behaviour on account erasure (the events carry a user reference), and reason-text privacy remain explicit later-gate items (QA residual risks).
**C4. Writers.** The SQL-impact list of Round 15 D2 item 2 is extended to include `archive_batch_group` and `restore_batch_group` in addition to join, approve, reject, bulk resolve, enroll, remove and leave. Every writer changes state and inserts its event in one transaction, under the batch row lock the existing functions already take.
**C5. Recipient-bound invites are enforced, not just stored.** `join_group_by_token` resolves the token to an invite row and, in a single transaction holding a row lock on that invite, checks in order: the invite exists; it is not revoked; it has not expired (`expires_at` compared with the transaction's current time); the batch is not archived; the caller is a student; and, if the invite has a recipient, the caller matches it (a recipient account must equal `auth.uid()`; a recipient email must equal the caller's **confirmed** account email, compared lower-cased). Failure returns one generic refusal ("This invite isn't available for your account.") that does not reveal who it was meant for. Revoke takes the same row lock, so a revoke committed before the join acquires the lock wins, and a revoke after a completed join does not delete the request (the admin may reject it). `get_group_preview` returns the same "link no longer valid" shape for revoked, expired and unknown tokens and no batch details. Real-role tests must cover: wrong recipient, a second account using a recipient-bound invite, expiry at the boundary, revoke between preview and join, and concurrent revoke versus join in both orders; the shared cohort link must be unaffected.

### D. Brief B: card source of truth, consistency and label length
**D1. Canonical course source for cards and notes (supersedes Round 15 G).** Round 15's order (`flashcards.discipline_id` first) and its claim that the fallback is "safe either way" are **withdrawn**. RUN 5A shows only that every card has a subject and a platform-name `target_course`; it does not show that a non-NULL `discipline_id` agrees with them.
- **Canonical identity:** the discipline implied by the card's subject (`subject_id` -> `subjects.discipline_id`). `flashcards.discipline_id` and `target_course` are treated as copies that must agree with it.
- **Mapping chain for reporting:** (1) the subject-derived discipline; (2) if there is no subject but `discipline_id` is set, that discipline; (3) **legacy rows only:** `target_course` text equal to a discipline name; (4) a non-empty other `target_course` is a custom group using the study-time normalization key; (5) otherwise unassigned.
- **Conflicts:** where `discipline_id` disagrees with the subject-derived discipline, or `target_course` differs from the resolved discipline's name, the row is reported under **"Unassigned (conflict)"** until reconciled. No silent preference. Reconciliation is a separate data-fix task with its own SQL and rollback.
- **Measurement:** follow-up diagnostic RUN F6 counts NULL `discipline_id`, discipline-versus-subject disagreement, text disagreement and unresolvable rows for flashcards and notes. The design does not claim a result before those counts exist.
- **New platform cards and notes:** `discipline_id` is required and the (`discipline_id`, `subject_id`) pair is enforced by the same composite foreign key pattern as sessions (a `NOT VALID` constraint, so legacy rows are untouched). `target_course` becomes a derived label for new platform rows, set from the discipline by a guard trigger or the creating function (decided at implementation). A new platform row never depends on text equality for its identity.
**D2. One length and validation contract for a course label (supersedes the 60-character rule in Round 15 F1).** A course label is trimmed, 1 to **80** characters, contains no control characters, and has repeated internal whitespace collapsed when its grouping key is generated. The same rule applies to the Signup custom-course input, Profile Settings, the access-request course field and the session classification: a `NOT VALID` check on `profiles.course_level` (so new and updated values obey it, replacing today's always-true clause for new writes), `maxLength` and the same validation on the inputs, and a 1-80 check on `custom_course_label`. **Existing over-limit values are never altered or silently rejected:** the profile keeps its value; the picker shows the full current course and preselects it; if it exceeds 80 characters, the session stores it truncated at 80 on a word boundary with a visible notice before saving, and the profile value is untouched. Follow-up diagnostic RUN F9 (new) counts, without selecting any value, how many `profiles.course_level` values fall in each length band and the longest length; the 80 limit is revisited before approval if those counts show it is too tight.
**D3. Tie-breaker (QA non-blocking 4).** The display label of a custom group is the label of the row with the greatest (`created_at`, `id`) for that key; `id` breaks ties deterministically.
**D4. Canonical option source: union and precedence (QA non-blocking 5).** The single shared course-option definition returns, in order: the active `disciplines` rows; the allowed non-platform labels from one shared definition (the nine-label rule); the student's current course; the student's own earlier custom labels (never other students' text). A name that appears twice is shown once; a `disciplines` row wins over a same-named non-platform entry; a custom entry whose normalized text equals a platform name is treated as that platform course, never as a custom duplicate.

- Ten read-only runs: F1 effective privileges; F2 RLS state and policies; F3A and F3B live function bodies; F4 names that mention `account_type` or `suspended`; F5 the 59 own-card rows; F6 card and note course mapping counts; F7 invite-token collisions and memberships by profile status and role; F8 access-request correlation counts; F9 course-label length distribution (added in this round for D2). Each is one statement returning one `result` cell; no user-authored text is selected; bucketed or counted only. It was read for errors but not run.

### F. Non-blocking points
1. **Accepted.** Matrix row 10 stands as revised.
2. **Accepted.** B1 stands.
3. **Accepted.** Actor kinds are now distinct (C2); D7 stands.
4. **Accepted.** The deterministic tie-breaker is D3.
5. **Accepted.** The shared option source is defined by union and precedence in D4.
6. **Accepted.** The scenario checklist in Round 15 section E is Round 15's **reconstructed** checklist. Its attribution to the Auditor's message of 02/10/2026 cannot be independently verified from this file and should not be treated as authenticated earlier QA text.

Brief B: E1 to E6 as revised in Round 15 F5, plus new **E7**: course label limit of 80 characters with the non-destructive rule for existing values (recommend yes, subject to the F9 counts).

### H. Requests to QA

5. Confirm that no gate is approved by this round.

## Round 18 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The identity principle and ref-token claim still contradict each other, and anonymous email deduplication permits request pre-emption.** Section B says bearer tokens and typed email never create or merge an identity link automatically, but B4 makes the first bearer of an unclaimed token the authoritative linked account without any second proof. Either define token possession as an intentional identity credential with its threat model and delivery controls, or require an additional binding such as the caller's confirmed account email matching the request/contact intended for the link; the current two statements cannot both be true. Separately, B5 lets any anonymous caller submit another person's email first and suppress that person's later request as “already received.” Do not use an unverified email as a uniqueness boundary unless ownership is verified; rate-limit abuse separately. Finally, define “scope” exactly (request type, content item, creator/institute or platform-wide), because deduplication, closure and the database key cannot be audited while that term is unspecified.

2. **The over-limit compatibility rule changes course identity and can merge distinct courses.** D2 displays and preselects the full existing `profiles.course_level` but stores only a word-boundary truncation in `study_sessions`. That session no longer has the same label/key as the profile course, and two different values with the same first 80 characters can collapse into one custom group. A visible notice does not repair the identity loss. Grandfather the exact existing value, require the student to choose and confirm a distinct <=80-character alias with an explicit mapping, or use a stable custom-course identity. The limit may remain provisional until F9 is run, but Brief B cannot receive Gate 1 while its compatibility rule can conflate identities.

3. **Round 17 supersedes the whole Round 15 card-mapping section but drops its platform-rename invariant.** D1 correctly makes the subject-derived discipline canonical and marks conflicting legacy rows unassigned, but `target_course` and `profiles.course_level` still drive live queue/forecast equality. A discipline rename without coordinated migration would therefore create conflicts and change eligibility. Restate whether rename is prohibited in v1 or define the coordinated migration/guard contract; “derived label” on new writes does not keep existing rows synchronized after a rename.

4. **Diagnostic RUN F2 omits the membership policies it says it audits.** Its policy filter excludes `study_group_members` and `study_groups`, even though the diagnostic description promises content **and membership** RLS and Brief A specifically needs effective direct-write/read exposure for preserved rejected/removed rows. Add both tables. Include `access_requests` as well because Round 17 changes its trusted write/link model; include `study_sessions` if this run is also intended to substantiate the new classification boundary. Prior RUN 6 remains evidence of the old group policies, but it does not make this follow-up's completeness claim true.

5. **Diagnostic RUN F6 computes text disagreement using the precedence Round 17 withdrew.** It joins the resolved discipline with `COALESCE(f.discipline_id, s.discipline_id)` (and the same for notes), so a row whose stored discipline conflicts with its subject is compared to the stored discipline, not the newly canonical subject-derived discipline. Change the resolution to subject first, then stored discipline, and report the conflict buckets independently. Otherwise the result cannot validate D1 and may miscount `target_course` disagreements.

6. **Diagnostic RUN F8 does not measure the deduplication contract it is meant to inform.** It counts repeated pending emails globally, mixing authenticated and anonymous requests and ignoring scope; it does not count repeated open requests by authoritative requester-and-scope or anonymous verified-contact-and-scope. After finding 1 defines scope and the anonymous rule, make F8 report those exact aggregate collision groups. Split missing request email from a genuine requester/profile-email difference so NULL contact values are not labelled as email disagreement.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The state-and-events model is now substantially complete. It includes staff invitation, rejection, removal, leaving, archive closure, restored re-request and staff joining; actor kinds are no longer overloaded through NULL; and event reads are constrained through least-privilege server functions. D8's proposed student view is appropriate.

2. C5 gives recipient-bound invites meaningful enforcement, generic failures and useful concurrency tests. C4 should say every revised writer **will** take the batch lock; the current `remove_group_member` and not every existing writer already does. Later SQL must specify one lock order across invite, batch and membership rows to avoid deadlocks.

3. The canonical card source, conflict bucket, composite course/subject enforcement and “text is legacy fallback only” direction resolve QA Round 16's central card-identity concern once F6 is corrected. The deterministic (`created_at`, `id`) display-label tie-breaker and option-source precedence are also sound.

4. The diagnostic contains exactly ten executable statements. Each is a `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; there is no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation. Its output is limited to catalog definitions, bounded taxonomy buckets, dates reduced to month, and aggregate counts; no stored name, email, phone number, identifier or other user-authored literal is selected.

5. F1 uses effective privilege checks rather than inferring capabilities from policies. F3A/F3B capture the previously repo-only function bodies; F4 provides a bounded discovery pass for functions and policies mentioning `account_type` or suspension; F5 and F7 are useful count-only diagnostics. If F4 discovers an unexpected enforcement helper, its name is evidence that the helper's body needs a separately frozen follow-up, not authority to infer its behaviour.

6. Brief B remains architecturally independent of Brief A, but it is not ready for Founder Gate 1 until findings 2-3 are resolved and the relevant corrected F6/F9 results are audited. It may proceed independently after that; Brief A additionally depends on the corrected security diagnostic and finding 1.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid (`AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`). Round 17 is frozen at commit `974c74992e8d68bda83b30360dd2f94f87a364ce`, with no `src/` changes since baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- The diagnostic's full SHA-256 is `3614da8d2052b5e2aea2107a36585d0e5b7e869598eafd2a2b0da9b4b97225d7`, exactly matching the full and short (`3614da8d2052`) hashes recorded in the status block.
- Access-request closure is no longer proposed by broad email matching, authenticated requester identity is server-derived, and contradictory contact email is surfaced rather than silently linked.
- Membership history is append-only, state changes and events are proposed as one transaction, stale non-active rows are explicitly denied access, and recipient/expiry/revocation checks are assigned to the server.
- New platform cards/notes and study sessions converge on the same composite course/subject enforcement pattern, while legacy conflicts remain visible rather than silently reassigned.
- Round 17 correctly states that the diagnostic is not authorized to run and that no gate is approved.

### Residual risks

- Static review cannot prove live compatibility or the absence of catalog drift. After a revised diagnostic passes QA, only the Founder may authorize the exact new hash; every result or error must be preserved without ad-hoc query changes.
- Function and policy definitions can theoretically contain unexpected personal literals. If any are observed in a result cell, do not save that raw cell in tracked evidence; preserve a sanitized account and return for QA direction.
- Event retention/account erasure, reason-text handling, invite lifecycle, custom-key collation, legacy data reconciliation and coordinated course renames remain later SQL/rollback/test concerns.
- The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` change was not touched. No SQL, application code, database, Git state or external service was changed by this audit.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise the two design contracts in findings 1-3 and diagnostic runs F2, F6 and F8, then return the diagnostic under a new SHA-256 hash and the revised round under a new commit SHA. The current diagnostic hash is read-only and privacy-safe but is **not passed** because it is incomplete and one metric uses the wrong identity precedence. It remains unauthorized to run. Neither brief is ready for Gate 1, no gate is approved, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

## Round 19 · Claude · 04/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 18 (REVISION REQUIRED, six blocking findings, six non-blocking points). All are accepted; there is no pushback. This round changes **how** the work is delivered: the two design briefs are now two complete, self-contained files (listed in section E), not further patches in this thread, and every cross-artifact check I ran is reported in section D. Read-only: no SQL run, no application code. Round 17 was audited at commit `974c749`; this round will be committed together with the three files, and QA should audit that new commit.

### A. Why the same kinds of issue kept recurring (my analysis, checked against Rounds 14, 16 and 18)
1. **Patching by "supersedes" dropped content.** Round 17 replaced the whole Round 15 card-mapping section and silently lost its platform-rename invariant (Round 18 blocking 3).
2. **A design change was not carried to the artifact that depends on it.** Round 17 made the subject-derived discipline canonical, but the follow-up diagnostic's RUN F6 still resolved with the withdrawn order (Round 18 blocking 5).
3. **Descriptions were written from memory and not re-read against the query.** RUN F2's header promised membership policies; its filter did not select them (Round 18 blocking 4). RUN F8 was written before the identity rules were settled and measured something else (blocking 6).
4. **A new rule was not tested against my own stated principle.** Round 17 said tokens and typed email never create a link automatically (section B), then let the first bearer of a token become the authoritative account (B4), and deduplicated anonymous requests by an unverified email (B5) (Round 18 blocking 1).
**What changed in method.** (a) Each brief is now one complete file with numbered **invariants** (A-I1 to A-I10, B-I1 to B-I10) that every section and every dependent artifact must respect; a later change edits that file, it does not add a patch. (b) Before handing over I read every diagnostic description against its query, searched all artifacts for withdrawn rules, and checked that each invariant is honoured where it is defined (section D). (c) A register maps every design-level blocking finding of Rounds 14, 16 and 18 to the exact place that resolves it (section F), so a dropped item shows up as a missing row.

### B. Response to the six blocking findings
1. **Identity contradiction, anonymous pre-emption, undefined scope (accepted).** Resolved in brief A, invariants A-I1 to A-I5 and sections 3 and 4.
   - *The contradiction.* A ref-token claim is now only a **claim**: it is recorded, shown to the admin as unconfirmed, and can grant, close or merge nothing. It becomes an authoritative link only by (a) the claiming account's **confirmed** email equalling the request's contact email, which proves control of the mailbox the request named, or (b) explicit administrator confirmation (4.3). The educator branch of `link_access_request` is unchanged and out of scope; its bearer-token nature is flagged for a separate security review.
   - *Pre-emption.* Anonymous requests are **never deduplicated or suppressed**; every row is stored, repeats are grouped in the admin display, and admin notifications are throttled (4.2, decision D9). Abuse control is a separate rate-limit requirement that returns an explicit "try again later", never a false "already received".
   - *Scope.* The word is retired. A **request target** is (`request_type`, `content_type`, `content_id`) as already stored on `access_requests`; only `student_access` is in play; platform-level means no content item; granting platform access satisfies every open `student_access` request of that account (section 3). **Open** means status `pending` or `contacted`. The authenticated duplicate rule has a precise key: a partial unique index on (requester, request type, content type, content id) while open (4.2).
2. **Over-limit rule could merge distinct courses (accepted).** Truncation is **removed**. Labels are never truncated (B-I2). The limit L is provisional at 120 and is fixed from follow-up RUN F9 as the larger of 120 and the longest existing value rounded up to a multiple of 20, capped at 200, so every existing value up to L is used **exactly**, with identical label and key. A value above 200, not expected, gets a student-confirmed alias that is that group's identity by the student's own choice; the profile value is never changed by logging (brief B 5.1, 5.4).
3. **Rename invariant dropped (accepted).** Restored and strengthened: platform course names are identifiers, **a discipline rename is prohibited in v1** and enforced by a trigger that rejects any change to `disciplines.name` (deactivation allowed); the eight dependent text columns and the live text-equality filters are listed; a catalog test fails if a new dependent column appears unlisted (brief B B-I1, 6.4, decision E8).
4. **RUN F2 omitted membership policies (accepted, verified).** I confirmed the filter lacked them. It now covers 15 tables: the content tables, the four membership tables, `access_requests`, `study_sessions`, `admin_audit_log`, `profiles`, `my_cards_enrollment` and `reviews`; the header lists exactly those 15 and I counted them in the query.
5. **RUN F6 used the withdrawn precedence (accepted, verified).** I confirmed `COALESCE(f.discipline_id, s.discipline_id)`. F6 now resolves **subject first**, keeps the stored discipline separately, and reports independently: subject missing, stored discipline missing, both present and agree, both present and disagree, text versus subject-derived name, text versus stored name, stored-only, and neither; a search found no `COALESCE` left in the file.
6. **RUN F8 did not measure the contract (accepted, verified).** F8 is rewritten around the contract: authenticated versus anonymous; a missing contact email counted **separately** from a differing one (the difference is counted only when both emails exist); open duplicate groups per authenticated requester and target; open repeat groups per anonymous contact email and target (informational, since anonymous requests are never deduplicated); case-insensitive versus exact email matches for anonymous requests; and a definition of open that matches brief A.

### C. Non-blocking points
1. Accepted. The transition table, actor kinds and read rules stand.
2. Accepted. Brief A 6.6 now says "every revised writer **will** take the lock" (Round 17 C4 is history), and states the facts: join, approve, reject, bulk resolve, enroll, archive and restore take the batch lock; `remove_group_member` and `leave_group` take none (live bodies, RUN 1A). One lock order is defined: invite row, batch row, membership rows in ascending id order, events insert.
3. Accepted. Card canonical source, conflict bucket, composite enforcement and tie-breaker stand (brief B).
4. Accepted. The diagnostic has exactly ten statements, each a SELECT or WITH ... SELECT.
5. Accepted. If F4 names an unexpected enforcement helper, its body needs a separately frozen follow-up; no behaviour is inferred from a name.
6. Accepted. Brief B waits for the corrected F6 and F9 results; brief A waits for the corrected security diagnostic and the identity contract.

### D. Self-audit performed before handing over (what I checked, and what it found)
- **Diagnostic descriptions against queries:** table lists counted (F1: 13 tables and 8 columns; F2: 15 tables); run count (10 headers, 10 statements); no write keyword at the start of any line; no `COALESCE` precedence leftover.
- **Consistency of "open":** the briefs and F8 used different meanings; fixed to `pending` or `contacted` everywhere.
- **Invariants against sections:** each of A-I1 to A-I10 and B-I1 to B-I10 is defined once and I checked where it is honoured; A-I9 (no payment) and B-I10 (existing totals unchanged) were stated only in the invariant list, so I added them to the sections that implement them (brief A section 8, brief B section 7).
- **A design defect I found myself:** a table CHECK, even `NOT VALID`, is evaluated against every new row version, so a profile CHECK on the course label would make any legacy over-limit or untrimmed profile impossible to update for unrelated reasons. Brief B therefore uses a **trigger that fires only when `course_level` changes** (5.3), and a guard trigger instead of a table CHECK for flashcards and notes (6.3), because legacy cards lack `discipline_id`. Foreign-key checks being skipped for unchanged key columns is stated as to-be-confirmed by a real-role test.
- **Wording corrected:** "client roles" for the immutability of `study_sessions`; the admin "link to this account" action added to brief A 4.6 so that an anonymous request can be confirmed explicitly.
- **Limits of this audit:** I cannot run SQL, so database behaviour I state from PostgreSQL knowledge (the CHECK and foreign-key behaviours above, the table rewrite on adding a stored generated column) is labelled for real-role tests, not asserted as live evidence. No browser journey was executed.

### F. Findings register (design-level blocking findings, Rounds 14, 16 and 18, and where each is resolved)
| Finding | Resolved in |
|---|---|

## Round 20 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **Brief A's authenticated duplicate index does not enforce its stated platform-level rule.** Sections 3 and 4.2 define the platform target with both `content_type` and `content_id` NULL, then specify an ordinary partial UNIQUE index on `(requester_id, request_type, content_type, content_id)`. PostgreSQL's default uniqueness semantics treat NULLs as distinct, so that index permits repeated open platform-level requests for the same authenticated requester. The design must require NULL-equal uniqueness (`NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, a safe expression key, or separate target-specific indexes) and must define database validation for the target shape: platform requests have both item fields NULL; item requests have both present; `content_type` is one of the two allowed values; and the referenced item exists. The acceptance tests must cover platform and item duplicates plus malformed pairs.

2. **Brief A's single eligibility invariant is not applied to every student activation path.** A-I7 says one server-side eligibility rule governs every way of becoming a member, but section 7 calls that rule only from single Add, bulk Add, single approve and bulk approve. Transition 3 activates an invited student through `join_group_by_token`, while section 5's join checks do not include the section 7 profile-status/access rule. A suspended or otherwise ineligible invited student could therefore take an activation path outside the common rule. The join path that changes `invited` to `active` must invoke the same eligibility decision, and the acceptance tests must exercise it.

3. **Brief A defines permanent claim pre-emption without a recovery contract.** Section 4.3 lets the first token bearer record an unconfirmed claim, permanently refuses every different account, and says a claim is never overwritten. The threat model acknowledges a forwarded token but considers only unauthorized grant/closure/merge; it does not address the resulting denial of the intended claimant's self-service path. Section 4.6 can create an administrator-confirmed link to another account, but the brief does not say whether that supersedes, clears or retains the hostile/stale claim, nor what happens to `profiles.access_request_ref`. Define an audited recovery/supersession rule and test wrong-account claim followed by the rightful confirmed-email account.

4. **Brief B contradicts itself about the shared course-option source.** Section 4.5 and E1 define the logging picker as current course, other active platform courses, the student's prior custom labels, General and Other. Section 5.5 says one shared definition returns active disciplines, all allowed non-platform labels from the nine-label rule, current course and prior custom labels, and that the picker reads that definition. The latter would put CMA/CS catalogue labels into the picker even when they are neither the current course nor prior student labels, and its stated order also conflicts with current-course-first. B-I7 therefore has no single implementable contract. Define one canonical data source plus explicit per-surface ordering/filtering, or make every surface use the same exact list and order.

5. **Diagnostic RUN F5 does not isolate the 59 due mismatch rows it claims to classify.** The 59 rows in RUN 8 were selected by the live forecast predicate: active review, due by the student's timezone date, skip boundary, question-type, course and visibility rules, then no active enrollment. F5 selects every own card with any review and no enrollment, without the due/status/skip/course/question-type predicates. Its totals and month/role/batch buckets therefore may describe a materially different population and cannot answer the stated follow-up. Reuse the frozen RUN 8 predicate (subject to the captured live-body comparison) and then classify exactly its own-card/no-enrollment mismatch set.

### Non-blocking findings

1. RUN F2 labels its first result `rls_state_all_public_tables` but filters `pg_class.relkind = 'r'`, excluding partitioned parents (`'p'`). This is the same catalog caveat recorded in QA Round 7. Include both kinds or narrow the label and preserve the condition that an omitted matching parent requires follow-up.

2. RUN F8's terms `authenticated` and `anonymous` describe only whether the current row contains `requester_user_id`. The live `submit_access_request` trusted a client-supplied id, so historical non-NULL rows are not proof of an authenticated submission. Relabel these as “requester id present/absent” for the current snapshot. Also break duplicate-group counts down by request type, or limit the decision metric to `student_access`, so out-of-scope educator/institute repeats cannot be mistaken for collisions governed by brief A.

3. Brief B's over-200 fallback lacks a persistent alias binding. Section 5.4 asks the student to choose an alias, but section 4.5 keeps the over-limit profile value as the first, preselected exact-label option on later logs. If F9 finds such a row, define how the confirmed alias is remembered and preferred without changing the profile, or explicitly require reconfirmation each time.

4. Brief A's lock order should say “invite row(s), where applicable, in a deterministic order.” Several listed writers have no invite, and archive or lifecycle work may touch more than one. The current singular absolute wording is not enough to derive a complete deadlock test matrix.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid (`AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`). Round 19 is frozen at commit `907779a32b9721d998cf20c13ab11775316b02c8`, with no `src/` change since baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- All three files exactly match the status block: brief A `29f53ba0446e4044aad64755d32767a42bedc76d6d19428df95ad69f76ffbd11`, brief B `5014b827a9570333dd03d74ee48f30b248e817e4623dd5258ab356ffb45c01e1`, and diagnostic v2 `25f4c52a4025c2285286961ee44b56a6fa881cafb93dc25dde8b10578c35891d`.
- The Round 18 corrections are materially present: a claim is non-authoritative until confirmed email or administrator action; anonymous requests are stored rather than deduplicated; F2 now includes the promised membership/access/session tables; F6 uses subject-first resolution and independent conflict counts; F8 separates missing from differing email; truncation is removed; and discipline rename is explicitly prohibited and guarded.
- The findings register contains every design-level blocking finding from QA Rounds 14, 16 and 18. The new findings above arise from checking the consolidated contracts and the executable query details, not from a dropped register row.
- The diagnostic still contains ten executable statements, each `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`, with no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation. Its outputs remain aggregate/catalog based and privacy-safe for the stated boundary.
- The status block's Agreed decisions accurately reflects the previously recorded working positions, including separation of membership and entitlement, explicit General/legacy semantics, and sequencing. It remains a record of positions, not Gate 1 approval.
- Round 19 correctly leaves every new gate unticked and says the diagnostic is not authorized to run.

### Residual risks

- Static review cannot establish live syntax, effective privileges, RLS behavior, collation behavior or catalog drift. The corrected diagnostic must be frozen under a new hash, passed by QA, and separately authorized by the Founder before it is run.
- Brief A still depends on the live security/enforcement results named in section 12. Brief B still depends on corrected F6/F9 evidence before Gate 1 consideration.
- Event retention and erasure, reason-text privacy, invite lifecycle, custom-key collisions, legacy reconciliation and real-role concurrency remain later-gate concerns.
- No SQL, browser journey, application test, database operation or external service was run. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise the brief A uniqueness/target contract, invited-student eligibility path and claim-recovery contract; reconcile brief B's option-source contract; and correct diagnostic F5. Address the non-blocking diagnostic/alias/lock-order points while producing the new artifacts. Return all changed files under new SHA-256 hashes and a new commit SHA. Diagnostic `25f4c52a4025` is read-only and privacy-safe but is **not recommended for Founder authorization** because F5 does not measure its stated population. Neither brief is ready for Gate 1, no gate is approved, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

## Round 21 · Claude · 04/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 20 (REVISION REQUIRED, five blocking findings, four non-blocking). **All nine are accepted; there is no pushback.** I verified each one against the artifact text, the live evidence files and the code before changing anything, and found the same error in each: a rule was stated but not checked against the mechanism that would enforce it (section A). Read-only: no SQL run, no application code. Round 19 was audited at commit `907779a`; QA's Round 20 is committed as `8925693`; this round is committed together with the three revised files, and QA should audit that new commit. The briefs are the design; they are edited in place (v4), not patched here.

### A. What went wrong, and what I did differently this time
**The class of error.** In every blocking finding I had written a sentence that sounded complete and then not tested it against how it would actually execute:
1. *Uniqueness* (finding 1): I wrote "partial unique index on (requester, type, content type, content id)" and did not ask what PostgreSQL does with NULLs in a unique index, although the platform target is defined as NULLs.
2. *One rule for every path* (finding 2): I wrote invariant A-I7 ("every way of becoming a member") and then listed four callers by hand; the join path was missing, because I never enumerated the paths from the transition table that the invariant itself governs.
3. *A security rule without its failure mode* (finding 3): I made a claim permanent to prevent pre-emption of authority, and did not ask what a permanent claim does to the rightful person.
4. *"One shared source"* (finding 4): I stated B-I7 and then described two different lists in two sections, without putting every surface in one table.
5. *A diagnostic that did not measure its stated population* (finding 5): I rebuilt F5 from the idea of RUN 8 instead of from RUN 8's text.
**What I did this round (a method, not an intention).**
- Before editing, I read the evidence behind each finding: the RUN 8 query and result (4,138 badge rows; 59 mismatches, all own cards with no enrollment row, one student), the live `get_due_forecast` body in RUN 1B (compared with the RUN 8 predicate clause by clause: they match), the live `access_requests_content_type_check` (RUN 7: only `flashcard_deck`, `note` and NULL), the live `submit_access_request` body (RUN 1A: stores both content fields as sent), and the code that supplies the ids (`NoteDetail.jsx:327-330`, `StudyMode.jsx:296,339-341,1075`).
- For every uniqueness or constraint sentence I now state the NULL and legacy-row behaviour. For every invariant that says "every path" I enumerated the paths from the transition table. For every "one source" claim I wrote a surface-by-surface table.
- After editing I re-read the changed sections in full and searched all three artifacts for withdrawn wording. That re-read found defects of my own (section D).

### B. Response to the five blocking findings
1. **Duplicate index and target shape (accepted, verified).** The finding is correct: a PostgreSQL unique index treats NULLs as distinct, so the platform-level rule was not enforced. Brief A section 4.2 now specifies **two partial unique indexes** for `student_access` rows with an open status and a requester: (i) platform-level, unique on the requester where both content fields are NULL; (ii) item-level, unique on (requester, content type, content id) where both are present. `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` is stated as an acceptable alternative where the live PostgreSQL version supports it; the version has not been captured and is listed as OPEN, so the design does not depend on it. Section 3 now defines the **valid target shapes** (platform-level: both NULL; item-level: both present, type `note` or `flashcard_deck`, and the id exists in `notes` or `flashcard_decks`; anything else is malformed and refused). Validation is in `submit_access_request` and in a trigger that fires on insert and only when the target columns change (a table CHECK is rejected for the reason in brief B 5.3: it would block status updates on any legacy malformed row). The need to clean legacy open duplicates before the indexes can be created is stated. Acceptance tests added (platform and item duplicates, concurrent submissions, malformed pairs, unrelated update of a legacy malformed row, index creation on seeded duplicates). Diagnostic F8 now measures the target shapes and platform-level and item-level duplicate groups separately.
2. **Eligibility on every activation path (accepted, verified).** Correct: transition 3 (invited to active through the link) was not governed by the section 7 rule. Section 7 now defines `eligibility(student, batch)` as the only place the rule lives, invoked at **transitions 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 and 11** (marked in a new paragraph under the transition table in 6.2), plus the staff variant for transition 13. The join path's ordered checks (section 5) now include it, so an invited student activating through the link is checked like any other. It is re-evaluated at the moment of approval or activation (a student suspended after requesting is refused with outcome `ineligible`). A-I7, D4, the SQL list and the acceptance tests name every path, including a suspended invited student using the link.
3. **Claim pre-emption and recovery (accepted, verified).** Correct: a permanent first claim denied the rightful claimant. Section 4.3 is redesigned. Claims live in a new **claims relation**, unique per (request, account); **a claim never blocks or replaces another**, so a forwarded token cannot pre-empt anyone. Authority sits in a separate **link account** with a basis (`submitter`, `confirmed_email`, `administrator`). The rightful account whose confirmed email matches becomes the link account automatically (if none exists); otherwise an administrator confirms, dismisses, links or supersedes, each audited in `admin_audit_log`. The text states what supersession and dismissal do (closures already made stand; nothing is retroactive; no grant is reversed), that `profiles.access_request_ref` is never changed or cleared by any administrator action, and that a person's own authenticated submission is not superseded in v1. The acceptance test the finding asks for (wrong-account claim followed by the rightful confirmed-email account) is in section 10. New decision D10.
4. **Course option source (accepted, verified).** Correct: 4.5 and 5.5 described two different lists. Brief B 5.5 now defines **one catalogue** (active platform courses, the six CMA and CS labels, and the caller's own current and previously used labels) with one normalization and precedence rule, and a **projection table** giving each surface (Signup, Profile Settings, access form, logging picker) its exact content and order. The picker is a defined filter of the catalogue: CMA and CS catalogue labels appear in it only when current or previously used. 4.5, B-I7, E1, E3 and the tests were changed to match.
5. **Diagnostic F5 (accepted, verified).** Correct: F5 selected every own reviewed card with no enrollment, not the RUN 8 population. F5 now reuses the **RUN 8 predicate verbatim** (active review, due by the student's timezone date, skip boundary, not a concept card, the course rule, the visibility rule), compared clause by clause with the live `get_due_forecast` body in RUN 1B (they match). It reports the RUN 8 reconciliation keys first (`badge_rows`, `mismatch_rows`, `mismatch_rows_that_are_own_cards`, `mismatch_rows_by_enrollment_state`, with the 04/10/2026 values stated in the header), then classifies exactly the own-card, no-enrollment subset by owner role, card month, batch, visibility, days overdue, skip state and course-rule basis. The earlier wider count (any status, any date) is kept only as clearly labelled `wider_population` keys, stated not to be the RUN 8 population.

### C. Non-blocking points
1. **F2 relkind (accepted).** F2 now covers ordinary and partitioned tables (`relkind IN ('r', 'p')`), and the header says so.
2. **F8 labels and breakdown (accepted).** "authenticated" and "anonymous" are replaced by **requester id present / absent**, with the reason stated (the live function stored a client-supplied id). Decision metrics are limited to `student_access`; other request types appear only in `by_request_type` and one labelled informational by-type duplicate count.
3. **Alias binding (accepted).** Brief B 5.4 now states the rule: the over-limit profile value is never offered as a label; the student types an alias on first log; **no registry and no profile link are stored**; on later logs the earlier alias appears as an ordinary previously used label with the most recent preselected for one-tap confirmation, and the design says openly that a student whose last custom log was a different course must pick the alias again (the explicit-reconfirmation option QA allowed). E7 updated.
4. **Lock order (accepted).** Brief A 6.6 now reads: invite row(s) where the writer touches an invite, ascending id; batch row(s) ascending id; membership rows ascending id; events insert; steps that do not apply are skipped, and the deadlock matrix is derived per writer from the steps that apply. A-I8 points to 6.6.

### D. Self-audit before handing over (what I checked and what it found)
- **Diagnostic, mechanical:** 10 run headers and 10 statements; no write keyword at the start of any line; parentheses and single quotes balanced in every run (counted per run); no `COALESCE` precedence leftover; no old F8 labels left; header descriptions of F2, F5 and F8 re-read against their queries.
- **A defect in my own F8 draft:** I first wrote an existence subquery inside an aggregate `FILTER`. I cannot run SQL here and cannot assume the server accepts it, so I moved the check into a CTE column (`item_missing`) computed with an ordinary `CASE`.
- **Defects found by re-reading the briefs after editing:**
  - Brief A 4.3 said a claim "never hides" another claim while its last bullet said the screen collapses others. Reworded: collapsing is display only; every claim stays stored and one click away.
  - A dismissed claimant could claim again and the text was silent. Now: replay leaves the claim dismissed.
  - D4 still listed only Add and approve as the paths using the eligibility rule. It now names join, invite, Add and approve.
  - Brief B 5.4: my first alias wording preselected "the most recent custom session" and called it "your course". That is wrong for a student whose last custom log was another course. Replaced by the explicit-reconfirmation wording above.
  - Brief B 5.5: the de-duplication precedence (current over catalogue) conflicted with the Profile Settings order. Precedence now decides only the displayed label and kind flags; position is given by each surface's Order column.
- **Slip while editing (corrected before hashing):** a line-number splice dropped the section 7 heading of brief A and left the old paragraph behind; a failed temporary-file write dropped one bullet of brief B 5.4. I found both by listing the headings and grepping for each expected sentence, and restored them. The hashes below are of the final files.
- **Limits:** I cannot run SQL. PostgreSQL behaviour stated in the briefs (NULLs in unique indexes, trigger versus CHECK evaluation, the table rewrite for stored generated columns) is from PostgreSQL knowledge and is marked for real-role tests, not asserted as live evidence. F5, F8 and the other runs have not been executed or parsed by a server, only checked mechanically as above. No browser journey was executed.

## Round 22 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **Brief A's two indexes still cannot distinguish trusted new requester ids from client-supplied legacy ids.** A-I4 and 4.4 correctly say a legacy `requester_user_id` is not an authoritative link, but both indexes in 4.2 apply to every non-NULL requester id. All existing rows predate the proposed server-derived write rule. A legacy row on which an anonymous or hostile client supplied account X can therefore collide with X's later genuine signed-in request; the unique violation path then returns that untrusted legacy row as though it were X's duplicate. That both suppresses the genuine row and violates A-I3/A-I4. Duplicate cleanup alone does not solve a single spoofed row. The mechanism needs an immutable provenance/basis that only the server can set for a signed-in submission and that both indexes predicate on (the new `submitter` link basis could serve if its creation is atomic), or a separately reviewed migration that removes/neutralizes every legacy requester id before index creation. Add a test that seeds one open legacy row with a victim's client-supplied id, then submits the same target as that authenticated victim and proves the genuine submission is not collapsed into the legacy row.

2. **The claims relation has a write boundary but no read-authorization contract.** Section 4.7 says clients cannot write it, while 4.6 requires the admin screen to read every claimant's account details and decision state. The brief never states whether row-level security is enabled, whether `anon`/`authenticated` have direct SELECT, or which server read function exposes which fields. Claim records reveal account-to-request associations and dismissed decisions; relying on unspecified default grants would make the new recovery mechanism a privacy risk. Define the relation as RLS-enabled with no direct client privileges/policies, expose it through a platform-admin/super-admin SECURITY DEFINER reader (and state whether a claimant can read their own state), and add real-role denial tests for anonymous, ordinary student, professor and group admin callers.

3. **Brief B's active-only catalogue can turn an existing platform course into a custom identity after deactivation.** Section 5.5's platform layer contains only active disciplines, while 6.4 expressly allows deactivation. If a student's current `course_level` equals a now-inactive discipline name, it no longer matches the platform layer; it enters as a `current` overlay and the logging projection treats a non-platform current value as custom. A new session would then store a custom key instead of that discipline id, splitting card progress and study time in violation of B-I1 and B-I8. Separate identity resolution (which must recognize all discipline rows, including inactive ones) from selectable alternatives (active only), define whether an inactive current platform course remains selectable/current or forces an explicit replacement, and test this case across the picker and report grouping.

### Non-blocking findings

1. When an email-matching claimant is already the request's existing link account (for example, the signed-in submitter later replays the token), 4.3(ii) does not run because it is limited to requests with no link account. The claim is therefore left `unconfirmed` even though the same account is already authoritative. Make this a confirmed/no-op state rather than showing a false pending claim; separately require confirm/link actions to refuse an existing different `submitter` link and direct the administrator to the allowed action.

2. Brief B 5.4 says an over-limit profile value is never offered as a label, but the Profile Settings and access-form projections in 5.5 include the current value whenever it is not otherwise listed. Add the over-limit exclusion/repair presentation to those two projection rows. E7 also says later alias confirmation is always one tap, while 5.4 correctly admits that it is not when another custom course was used most recently; retain the more precise 5.4 wording.

3. RUN F8 correctly labels historical requester ids as untrusted, but its requester-id-present count spans all statuses. An open-only count split between platform and item targets would make the eventual provenance migration easier to size. This is useful but is not required for the diagnostic's current target-shape and duplicate questions.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid (`AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`). Round 21 is frozen at commit `3286a78ca13a8445be75a78fed2ae8a06ee6ae78`, and there is no `src/` change since baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- All registered bytes match: brief A v4 `219dad4ab0b2f1b67aac14e356717a708ca68b48b16d40497ff8283caeff8ee9`, brief B v4 `2181b39c93a628e91f7bef0015045f04114a41ab1c73bf5b6dcbb815bc1b5fcf`, and diagnostic v3 `2087a3d964dc927a3a381cd7f565cff936b039c9c0ba6041f3370c7ff71754ea`.
- The two-index mechanism now handles PostgreSQL's default NULL-distinct behavior for trusted future rows: the platform predicate has one requester key and the item predicate has requester/type/id; both are scoped to open `student_access` rows. The target trigger avoids blocking unrelated updates of legacy malformed rows.
- The eligibility function is now enumerated at every student request/activation transition, including `invited` to `active`, and is re-evaluated at approval. The refusal/no-event and per-row bulk outcomes are explicit and testable.
- Multiple claims can coexist, authority is separated into one link account, the rightful confirmed-email claimant is no longer pre-empted, and administrator confirm/dismiss/link/supersede effects and audit history are substantially defined.
- Brief B's projection table now gives each active-course surface one explicit list and order, with one normalization/de-duplication rule. The explicit alias reconfirmation trade-off resolves the earlier unstated persistence assumption, subject to the projection wording above.
- Diagnostic F2 now includes ordinary and partitioned public tables. F5 reproduces the frozen RUN 8/live-forecast predicate clause for clause, first emits the reconciliation counts, and classifies only the own-card/no-enrollment subset; its wider population is clearly separated. F8 limits decision metrics to `student_access`, distinguishes target shapes and missing items, and no longer treats a stored requester id as proof of authentication.
- The diagnostic has ten executable statements, all `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation was found. Its returned data remains within the aggregate/catalog privacy boundary.
- No gate is approved by Round 21 or this QA round.

### Residual risks

- The diagnostic has not been parsed or executed by PostgreSQL. Live syntax, catalog drift and the effective privilege/RLS conclusions remain unverified until the Founder authorizes this exact hash and every result or error is preserved unchanged.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so authenticated deduplication excludes untrusted legacy requester ids and so claims have an explicit least-privilege read contract. Revise brief B so discipline deactivation cannot change a platform identity into a custom identity, and address the non-blocking consistency points. Return changed briefs under new SHA-256 hashes and a new commit SHA. ## Round 23 · Claude · 04/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 22 (REVISION REQUIRED, three blocking findings, three non-blocking). All six are accepted after checking each against the artifact text; there is no pushback. QA found no blocking problem in the diagnostic, so **the diagnostic file is unchanged** (hash `2087a3d964dc`, section D). Only the two briefs changed (v5). Read-only: no SQL run, no application code. Round 21 was audited at `3286a78`; QA's Round 22 is committed as `833ffae`; this round is committed with the two revised briefs, and QA should audit that new commit.

### A. Blocking findings
1. **Legacy requester ids could collide with a genuine request (accepted, verified).** Correct. In v4 both indexes were keyed on any non-NULL `requester_user_id`, and every row that exists today may carry a client-supplied id (RUN 1A), so a spoofed legacy row for account X would have collided with X's later genuine request, and the unique-violation path would have returned the untrusted row as X's duplicate. Brief A 4.2 now has both indexes predicated on **link basis `submitter`** and keyed on the **link account**, not on `requester_user_id`. The basis is **immutable and server-set**: written only by `submit_access_request` in the same INSERT that stores the requester and link account, from `auth.uid()` of a signed-in caller; a trigger refuses setting it by UPDATE, refuses any later change to the basis, link account or requester of a `submitter` row (including by SECURITY DEFINER administrator functions), and refuses an INSERT with that basis unless link account = requester = `auth.uid()`. Legacy rows have no basis, so they are **outside both indexes** and the data-fix precondition of v4 is removed (legacy duplicates are left as they are; RUN F8 sizes them). Legacy ids are shown to administrators as "unverified id". The test QA asked for is in section 10 (seed one open legacy row with a victim's id, then submit as the victim: stored separately, not collapsed, and a repeat returns the genuine row), together with "indexes can be created while legacy duplicates exist" and "the basis cannot be set or altered by UPDATE, client or administrator function". A-I4 now says the same. One wording error of mine, caught in review: I first wrote that RUN F1 confirms the client's lack of write privilege on the link columns; those columns do not exist yet, so F1 cannot show it. It now says "to be proved by real-role tests".
2. **Claims relation had no read contract (accepted, verified).** Correct: 4.7 stated only the write boundary. New invariant **A-I11** and a contract in 4.7: row-level security enabled with no policies; `anon`, `authenticated` and `PUBLIC` hold no privilege (explicit revoke; platform default privileges are not relied on); writes only through SECURITY DEFINER functions; reads only through (a) an administrator reader (platform admin or super_admin, checked inside the function) returning claimant name and email, state and decision, and (b) a claimant reader returning the caller's **own** claim as a coarse status only (`pending review` or `linked`), never a dismissal, another claimant or any administrator identity. Professors, group admins, ordinary students and anonymous callers get nothing. Real-role denial tests for all four caller kinds, directly and through functions, plus a catalog test that no `anon`, `authenticated` or `PUBLIC` grant exists on the claims, invite and events relations. I applied A-I11 to the **invite** and **events** relations as well (sections 5 and 6.3 point to it), because the invite relation holds the tokens and the same default-grant risk applies.
3. **Deactivation could turn a platform course into a custom identity (accepted, verified).** Correct: 5.5 built the platform layer from active rows only while 6.4 allows deactivation. Brief B now separates two roles: **identity resolution** uses every `disciplines` row, active or not (B-I1, 5.5 layer 1, the de-duplication rule, 6.4), so a label equal to any discipline name always resolves to that `discipline_id` and is never stored as a custom label; **selectable alternatives** use active rows only. For a student whose current course is an inactive discipline: it stays their current course with the same identity, marked "no longer offered", is never turned into a custom label, keeps its `discipline_id` on new logs, and is not offered to anyone else; Profile Settings invites them to pick an active course. Progress groups include inactive disciplines that have data (7). 4.5, the Signup, Profile Settings, access-form and picker rows, and the tests were updated. New decision **E9** (recommend yes); whether deactivation should also force a course change is stated as outside this brief. The deactivation test across the picker and report grouping is in section 8.

### B. Non-blocking points
1. **Claim by the existing link account; submitter conflicts (accepted).** A claim by an account that is already the link account now writes no claim row (already authoritative), so no false pending claim appears. Confirm and link actions that would set a different account on a `submitter` link are refused with outcome `submitter_link_exists`, and the screen directs the administrator to dismissal as the only allowed action (4.3). Tests added.
2. **Over-limit current value, E7 (accepted).** Over-limit presentation is added to the Profile Settings and access-form projections ("Rules for the student's current value", 5.5): not listed as an option, a repair notice on Profile Settings, an empty field with the same prompt on the access form, profile unchanged until the student saves a new value. E7 now keeps the precise wording of 5.4 (one tap only when the most recently used custom label is the alias; otherwise the student picks it again).
3. **F8 open-only split (declined for this version, with the reason).** The requested split is not needed for the diagnostic's present questions, and after finding 1 the legacy ids no longer take part in any index, so sizing a provenance migration is not on the critical path. Changing F8 would also change the hash that QA has just passed and that the Founder may now be asked to authorize. If the Founder or QA wants it, it can be added as a later read-only follow-up (v4 of the file), which would then go through QA and the Founder again.

### C. Self-audit before handing over
- **Searches:** brief A for every mention of the indexes, `requester`, and A-I11 (6 mentions: the version line, the invariant, 4.7, sections 5 and 6.3, and the tests); brief B for every remaining "active" assumption (none left that governs identity), for E9 (3 mentions), and for the new test text.
- **Consistency checks:** 4.4 (a legacy requester id is not a link) now agrees with 4.2 (legacy rows are outside the indexes) and A-I4; the `submitter` immutability agrees with the "supersede" rule (a `submitter` link is never superseded) and the new `submitter_link_exists` refusal; B-I1 agrees with 6.4 (deactivation allowed, identity unchanged) and with the picker rule (an inactive current course stays first and keeps its id).

The diagnostic has still not been parsed or executed by a server.

## Round 24 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The claims reader has two incompatible caller contracts, so the Round 22 privacy finding is not fully closed.** Brief A 4.7 first authorizes a claimant reader to return the caller's own coarse status, then says that "ordinary students" get nothing and requires ordinary-student denial tests through the functions. An ordinary student is the normal claimant, so an implementation cannot satisfy both rules. The external mapping is also incomplete for a retained `dismissed_by_admin` claim: the reader exposes only `pending review` or `linked`, but the brief does not say whether dismissal returns no row or a deliberately coarse status. Define the matrix by relationship as well as role (claimant versus non-claimant), define the output for every internal claim state, and make the real-role tests distinguish an ordinary-student claimant from an ordinary-student non-claimant. The SECURITY DEFINER reader must check that relationship internally; relation RLS and revokes alone do not resolve the contradiction.

2. **The invite relation does not meet the new A-I11 rule.** Section 5 says it is reachable through create, list, revoke and join functions, but gives no caller scope or returned-field scope for create, list or revoke, even though list exposes bearer tokens. It also omits `get_group_preview`, which section 5 says reads invite validity and batch details. A-I11 requires a stated read scope per caller kind, and SECURITY DEFINER functions defaulting or being granted EXECUTE too broadly would bypass the relation-level revokes. Define who may create, list and revoke an invite (including the exact platform-admin/group-admin boundary), what each reader returns, and the deliberately narrow anonymous/authenticated preview and join behavior. Require explicit function EXECUTE revokes/grants plus internal authorization checks, and test anonymous, ordinary student, professor, group admin, platform admin and super-admin callers. Until then a token-bearing relation has no complete least-privilege behavior.

3. **Brief B's course-name identity is still not durable under catalogue writes.** B-I1 and 5.5 resolve a normalized discipline name to one `discipline_id`, but RUN 5B shows that `disciplines` has only its primary-key unique index: `name` is not unique, much less unique under the normalization used by the catalogue. Section 6.4 rejects name UPDATEs but does not reject a duplicate-name INSERT or a hard DELETE. A second row with the same normalized name makes one label resolve to two IDs; deleting a discipline can make an existing profile label cease to be a platform identity and recreate the platform-to-custom split that Round 22 identified. Enforce uniqueness using the same normalization as 5.5, prohibit hard deletion in v1 (deactivate instead), and add real-role/catalogue tests for duplicate normalized INSERT and DELETE as well as rename. The current three rows are distinct, but present data does not enforce the invariant for future behavior.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The `submitter` trigger and the privilege boundary have different jobs. The trigger described in 4.2 would accept a direct INSERT whose basis, link account and requester all equal the signed-in caller; only the promised effective denial of client writes to the link columns makes "written only by `submit_access_request`" true. At the SQL gate, do not treat a column-level revoke as sufficient while a table-level INSERT/UPDATE grant remains effective. Either make the function the only table write path or replace table grants with an explicit safe-column allowlist, and exercise direct table INSERT and UPDATE as real `authenticated` callers. The existing acceptance-test wording is sufficient to catch this if it tests effective behavior rather than catalog text alone.

2. The diagnostic's declined open-only requester-id split remains a useful optional measure, not a requirement for the current diagnostic questions. Excluding every no-basis legacy row from the two indexes removes it from the critical deduplication path.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The handoff was valid before audit: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 23 and last QA-reviewed round 22. Round 23 is frozen at commit `3dda3bdd7008d5ddd87dc916cda4ba7a89b0561e`; no `src/` change exists from the stated `6fc6ceb` baseline.
- Both reviewed brief hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v5 `f20f097905add4c3ee1dd746197ca69c3735cd432cb5711722bd92c36e758992` and brief B v5 `59f303046c453cc04b9a32c0ceb5e1cea08ce69e515df23e935f803b08f65cae`.
- The unchanged diagnostic still hashes to `2087a3d964dc927a3a381cd7f565cff936b039c9c0ba6041f3370c7ff71754ea` and has no diff from the Round 22-reviewed artifact. The Round 22 verdict therefore stands: no blocking QA finding in that read-only file, and QA continues to recommend that the Founder may separately authorize that exact hash. This round does not authorize it.
- The revised deduplication mechanism now behaves correctly for the Round 22 spoofed-legacy-row case: the legacy row has no `submitter` basis and is outside both indexes; the victim's genuine signed-in request creates its own server-provenance row; only a later repeat can collide with and return that genuine row. Freezing the link account, requester and basis on a `submitter` row also prevents later administrator relinking from corrupting the index key.
- A claim by the existing link account is now a no-op, and confirm/link against a different account on a `submitter` link is explicitly refused. Those changes remove the false-pending and impermissible-supersession behaviors from Round 22.
- For a discipline that remains in the catalogue but is deactivated, Brief B now behaves consistently: identity resolution still finds its ID; the current student's picker keeps it first and marked, Profile Settings and the access form retain it without offering it to others, new logs store the discipline ID, and Progress retains one platform group. The over-limit current-value projections and E7 wording are also consistent.
- No gate is approved by Round 23 or this QA round.

### Residual risks

- The diagnostic has not been parsed or executed by PostgreSQL. Live syntax, effective privileges, RLS behavior and catalogue drift remain unverified until the Founder authorizes the exact hash and every result or error is preserved unchanged.
- Brief A still depends on the follow-up F1/F2/F4/F7/F8 evidence or an explicit tested deny/allow replacement. Brief B still depends on F6/F9 evidence; it also needs the course-identity write guards in blocking finding 3 before Gate 1 consideration.
- Function EXECUTE ACLs, SECURITY DEFINER search paths and internal role/status checks, concurrency, rollback, claim/event retention and erasure, reason-text privacy, polymorphic target deletion, custom-key collation and normalized-label collisions remain later SQL and real-role test concerns.
- No SQL, browser journey, application test, database mutation or external service was run. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A to give the claims reader one unambiguous claimant/non-claimant behavior and to define least-privilege caller and field scopes for every invite function, including preview, with explicit function EXECUTE boundaries. Revise brief B to make normalized discipline-name identity unique and non-deletable in v1. Return changed briefs under new SHA-256 hashes and a new commit SHA. The diagnostic verdict for `2087a3d964dc` stands unchanged. Brief B is not yet ready to go to the Founder after F6/F9 because blocking finding 3 remains; no gate is approved, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

### A. Blocking findings
1. **Claims reader had two incompatible caller contracts (accepted, verified).** Correct: 4.7 said the claimant reader returns the caller's own status and also that "ordinary students get nothing", and the v5 test line repeated the contradiction. Brief A 4.7 now defines the contract **by relationship**, in a matrix: anonymous (no EXECUTE on either reader); platform admin or super_admin (administrator reader allowed); a **claimant** of that request of any role (claimant reader returns their own claim only); a **non-claimant** of any role (nothing, indistinguishable from a request that does not exist). The claimant reader checks the relationship inside the function (a claim row for `auth.uid()` or a link account equal to `auth.uid()`); relation RLS and revokes are stated as not sufficient for this. **Output per internal state:** `unconfirmed` gives `pending_review`; `confirmed_by_email`, `confirmed_by_admin` and a link account equal to the caller give `linked`; **`dismissed_by_admin` gives no row**, identical to a non-claimant, so a dismissal is never disclosed and the existence of other claimants is never revealed. The phrase "ordinary students get nothing" is withdrawn in the text, and the section 10 test line is rewritten: real-role tests separate an ordinary-student claimant from an ordinary-student non-claimant, test a professor and a group admin in each relationship, and test unconfirmed, confirmed and dismissed claims.
2. **Invite relation did not meet A-I11 (accepted, verified).** Correct: section 5 named the functions but gave no caller or field scope, listed no EXECUTE boundary, and omitted `get_group_preview`. I checked what exists today: for **batch** groups only the platform-admin screen builds the link (`AdminDashboard.jsx:1742`); the group page offers "Invite via Link" only for **non-batch** groups (`GroupDetail.jsx:717`); `get_group_preview` is on the anonymous allowlist in the repo (`docs/database/security/04_SCHEMA_revoke_execute_grants.sql`, repo copy, live ACL not measured). Brief A section 5 now has a function table: `create_batch_invite`, `list_batch_invites`, `revoke_batch_invite` for **platform admin and super_admin only** (this matches today's boundary; a batch's group admin has no invite right in v1; new decision **D11** asks whether to widen it), each with `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and `anon`, an internal authorization check, a pinned `search_path` and a stated returned-field list (the token appears only in create and list, to administrators); `join_group_by_token` to `authenticated` only; `get_group_preview` to `anon` and `authenticated` with a deliberately narrow output (valid: batch name, course, institution, and for a signed-in caller only their own relationship; every invalid case returns one identical shape; never the token, invite id, label, recipient, creator, expiry, administrators, member lists, or whether the invite is recipient-bound). A-I11 now requires, for every such function, explicit EXECUTE grants and revokes, an internal authorization check, a pinned `search_path` and a stated caller and field scope, and a catalog test of function EXECUTE ACLs. Section 10 adds the six-caller real-role matrix (anonymous, ordinary student, professor, group admin, platform admin, super_admin). Self-selected (non-batch) groups and their own `invite_token` are stated as outside this brief. New OPEN item: RUN F1 measures table and column privileges only, so the live EXECUTE ACLs of existing functions (`get_group_preview`, `join_group_by_token`, `submit_access_request`) are not measured; a separate read-only function-ACL check will be needed before the SQL gate (I have not changed the diagnostic for this).

Brief B 6.4 adds **catalogue write guards**: a unique index on the **normalized** name (the one normalization function that 5.5 and 4.1 use), over all rows including inactive ones; **no hard delete** of `disciplines` in v1 (trigger; deactivate instead); and the existing rename trigger. The three existing rows are distinct, so index creation will not fail. Tests added: exact and case or whitespace variants of a duplicate INSERT (including against an inactive row), DELETE refused and deactivation allowed, rename refused, and no client role able to TRUNCATE (TRUNCATE bypasses row triggers). B-I1 and E8 now carry the rule. I also recorded as OPEN, not assumed: whether subject deletes are guarded (the new composite foreign key will restrict deleting a referenced subject; the foreign keys on `flashcards.subject_id` and `notes.subject_id` are not measured) and the client write privileges on `disciplines` and `subjects`, which RUN F1 does not list.
