## Round 26 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The claimant reader still reports contradictory and potentially stale authority after link changes.** The 4.7 matrix says a signed-in non-claimant gets nothing, but the next paragraph returns `linked` when the caller is the request's link account; a signed-in submitter is exactly that case and deliberately has no claim row. More importantly, 4.3 permits a `confirmed_email` or `administrator` link to be superseded without changing the earlier claim, while 4.7 maps every `confirmed_by_email` or `confirmed_by_admin` claim to `linked`. The former link account would therefore continue to receive `linked` after authority moved to another account, contrary to 4.4. A dismissed claim creates another unresolved precedence case if that account is still the current link account, because "link account gives linked" and "dismissed gives no row" both apply. Define one ordered rule based on current authority, not historical state alone: for example, current link account wins and returns `linked`; otherwise a dismissed claim returns no row, an unconfirmed claim returns `pending_review`, and a formerly confirmed but superseded claim receives a defined non-linked result or an explicit retained `superseded` state. Add tests for a submitter with no claim, a confirmed claim before and after supersession, and dismissal both when the claimant is and is not the current link account.

2. **The new invite authority table contradicts the membership transition model.** Section 5 and D11 say a batch group admin has no invite right and cannot create or list a batch invite. Transition 2 in 6.2 still allows `admin or group_admin` to invite a student, transition 3 says that invited student activates through the link, and the eligibility paragraph still names the staff-invite function. Under the new table a group admin cannot create or retrieve the token needed for that activation, yet the state machine says the group admin can initiate it. Choose one behavior: either remove group admins from transition 2 and every staff-invite path in v1, or define a distinct group-admin invitation mechanism, its token delivery/read boundary, and its relationship to `create_batch_invite`/`list_batch_invites`. Then test the complete none-to-invited-to-active journey for every allowed actor. The current design cannot implement both D11 and transition 2.

3. **Normalized platform identity is not canonicalized in the exact-text columns on which live behavior still depends.** Brief B 5.5 says a label whose normalized text equals a discipline name is that platform course and is never custom, while B-I1 and 6.4 record that the live queue and forecast compare `target_course = course_level` as exact text. The profile trigger in 5.3 validates length and whitespace rules but does not say that `ca  final` is rejected or rewritten to canonical `CA Final`. Such a value is allowed by the custom input, resolves to the CA Final `discipline_id` in the catalogue and logging picker, but can still fail the live exact-text queue/forecast comparison. The normalized unique index on `disciplines` does not protect dependent text columns. Define a write rule that canonicalizes or refuses normalized platform-name variants wherever a new course label is stored, especially `profiles.course_level`; test the exact, case-variant and repeated-whitespace paths through Signup and Profile Settings. Because RUN 5A's five "other" profile values were identified only by exact comparison, add a privacy-safe aggregate check for existing values that normalize to a platform name and a repair rule if any exist. Until then B-I1 and the "one identity" behavior are not enforced end to end.

### Non-blocking findings

1. A-I11 says every added function has EXECUTE revoked from `anon`, while the invite table correctly grants `anon` access to `get_group_preview`. The later table and catalog-test exception make the intended effective ACL understandable, but the invariant should say that default/public execution is revoked first and only the explicitly listed roles are then granted, including `anon` for preview. That avoids generating a test which simultaneously requires and forbids the preview grant.

2. The optional F8 open-only requester-id split remains outside the current diagnostic's required questions. No change to the diagnostic is needed for it.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 25 and last QA-reviewed round 24. Round 25 and the two v6 briefs are frozen at commit `060e8a7`; no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- Both brief hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v6 `68e3f8088bc287630fa04f9d57ef08fad51084116d1ae4b192252c1eed2af853` and brief B v6 `4171fb6d036542fae1c5bd1a1d7b4794122c47e44e7ab896193fa87190a507f6`.
- The diagnostic still hashes to `2087a3d964dc927a3a381cd7f565cff936b039c9c0ba6041f3370c7ff71754ea` and has no diff from the artifacts reviewed in Rounds 22 and 24. The prior verdict stands: no blocking QA finding in that read-only file, and QA continues to recommend that the Founder may separately authorize that exact hash. This round does not authorize it.
- The claims relation now has explicit direct-access denial, named readers, relationship-aware checks and state-specific output. The ordinary-student claimant/non-claimant contradiction from Round 24 is removed; the remaining blocker is the interaction with current-link authority and supersession.
- The invite relation now has a strong least-privilege function boundary: create/list/revoke expose tokens only to internally verified platform administrators; join is authenticated; preview has a narrow public shape; direct relation access is denied; function ACL and six-caller tests are specified. Subject to blocking finding 2, this closes the token-exposure gap from Round 24.
- Brief B's new catalogue guards correctly make normalized discipline names unique across active and inactive rows, reject rename and hard delete, permit deactivation, and require client TRUNCATE denial. They close the duplicate-row and deletion cases from Round 24; blocking finding 3 concerns canonical dependent text, not those guards.
- No gate is approved by Round 25 or this QA round.

### Residual risks

- The diagnostic has not been parsed or executed by PostgreSQL. Live syntax, effective privileges, RLS behavior and catalogue drift remain unverified until the Founder authorizes the exact hash and every result or error is preserved unchanged.
- Live EXECUTE ACLs for existing functions are not covered by F1; the separate read-only function-ACL check identified in Brief A remains necessary before the SQL gate.
- Brief A still depends on F1/F2/F4/F7/F8 evidence or an explicit tested deny/allow replacement. Brief B still depends on F6/F9 evidence and now also on the privacy-safe normalized-profile check required by blocking finding 3.
- SQL details, rollback, real-role execution, concurrency, SECURITY DEFINER search paths, retention/erasure, subject deletion behavior and custom-key collation remain later-gate concerns.
- No SQL, browser journey, application test, database mutation or external service was run. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so claimant status is derived consistently from current link authority across submitter, dismissal and supersession cases, and reconcile D11 with the group-admin invite transition. Revise brief B so normalized platform labels cannot be stored in a non-canonical exact-text form, including a privacy-safe check and repair rule for existing values. Return changed briefs under new SHA-256 hashes and a new commit SHA. The diagnostic verdict for `2087a3d964dc` stands unchanged. Brief B is not ready to go to the Founder after F6/F9 while blocking finding 3 remains; no gate is approved, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

### B. Response to QA's Round 26
1. **Claimant reader contradictions after link changes (accepted, verified).** Correct: the v6 matrix said a non-claimant gets nothing while a later paragraph returned `linked` to the link account, and a superseded link account would have kept receiving `linked` from its old confirmed claim. Brief A 4.3 and 4.7 now use **one ordered rule on current authority**: (1) the caller is the **current link account**: `linked`, whatever any claim row says (a submitter with no claim; a confirmed claim before supersession); (2) otherwise an `unconfirmed` claim: `pending_review`; (3) otherwise (dismissed, superseded, a confirmed claim of a former link account, or no claim at all): **no row**, identical to a request that does not exist. The claim state machine is explicit: a new state `superseded` is set in the same transaction as a supersession for the former link account's `confirmed_*` claims; **dismissal of the current link account's claim is refused** (`is_current_link`), and an administrator "link" or "confirm" action sets that account's claim (even a dismissed one) to `confirmed_by_admin`; so "link account gives `linked`" and "dismissed gives no row" can never both apply. The matrix now has three relationships (link account, claimant, neither) instead of two. The tests QA listed are in 4.7: a submitter with no claim; a confirmed claim before and after supersession; dismissal when the claimant is and is not the current link account (the former is refused); a dismissed claimant later linked.
2. **Invite authority versus transition 2 (accepted, verified).** Correct: transition 2 still allowed `group_admin`, and the eligibility paragraph still named a staff-invite function, while D11 gave group admins no invite right. I chose the option consistent with today's behaviour (only the platform-admin screen builds batch invite links; no batch has an `invited` row): **transition 2 is platform admin or super_admin only**, performed by `create_batch_invite` with an **account recipient**, which writes the `invited` row and event in the same transaction, after the eligibility check; an email recipient writes no row. A new **transition 14** closes the `invited` row when its invite is revoked, so no row is stranded. The eligibility paragraph, the D4 text and D11 name `create_batch_invite`, and a test for the complete none to invited to active journey (platform admin and super_admin allowed; a group admin refused; revoke before activation; a wrong-account holder of a recipient-bound link refused) is in section 10. No distinct group-admin invitation mechanism is introduced.
3. **Normalized platform identity is not canonical in exact-text columns (accepted, verified).** Correct: B 5.5 resolves `ca  final` to the CA Final discipline while the live queue, forecast and course-change functions compare exact text (RUN 1B, F3A), and 5.3 did not canonicalize. Brief B adds **5.3a**: one immutable function, `canonical_course_label(text)`, returns the exact stored discipline name (active or not), else the exact catalogue text of a CMA or CS label, else the trimmed input; it is used by the `profiles` BEFORE trigger, which **rewrites** `course_level` on insert and on change (`ca  final` becomes `CA Final`; rewritten, not refused), by `submit_access_request` for the course field, and by the `study_sessions` insert path (a custom label equal to a discipline name is refused; a catalogue label is stored canonically). The existing AFTER course-change trigger then sees the canonical value. Tests: exact, case-variant and repeated-whitespace values through Signup, Profile Settings, the access form and the picker, with unchanged due counts and enrollments. **5.3b** adds the privacy-safe aggregate check QA asked for (run G1 of the second diagnostic: per dependent text column, exact canonical, **variant of a canonical name**, blank, other) and a **repair rule** if any variant exists (a separately reviewed data fix, only for exact normalized matches, with before and after counts, rollback, and the course-change trigger's archive and restore behaviour understood per student). Existing evidence already bounds it: F6 shows no variant between a card's or note's `target_course` and its subject-derived discipline name, and F9 shows no whitespace anomalies in profiles; case variants in profiles remain unmeasured until G1 runs. B-I1 and B-I6 now state the rule.
4. **Non-blocking 1 (accepted).** A-I11 now says default and `PUBLIC` execution is revoked first and `EXECUTE` is then granted only to the roles listed for each function (`anon` only for `get_group_preview` in this brief); the catalog test fails if a function is not executable by a role listed for it as well as if it is executable by an unlisted one, so it never both requires and forbids the preview grant.
5. **Non-blocking 2 (noted).** No change to diagnostic `2087a3d964dc` is needed.

## Round 28 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The migration does not yet remove effective client access to the old batch bearer token.** Follow-up F1 shows `anon` and `authenticated` have table-level `SELECT` on `study_groups` as well as column `SELECT` on `invite_token`; F2 shows `sg_select_member` exposes a group to any membership row. Brief A 6.7 correctly requires active-only readers, but section 12 says only that client SELECT on `study_groups.invite_token` is revoked and the old column is “dropped or ignored.” A column-level revoke does not deny a column while table-level SELECT remains effective, and merely ignoring a populated bearer-token column leaves it readable. The migration must choose an effective mechanism: drop the old column after the invite backfill and writer/reader cutover; clear it and make it inaccessible; or revoke table-level SELECT and regrant only safe columns/use a safe reader. Sequence the backfill and cutover so existing shared links continue to work (A-I10), and add real-role tests which attempt to select `invite_token` directly as an active member, stale member, authenticated non-member and anonymous caller. “Ignored” is not a security boundary.

2. **Expiry can strand the new durable `invited` membership state.** An account-recipient `create_batch_invite` writes an `invited` row; `join_group_by_token` refuses an expired invite. Transition 14 closes that row on explicit revocation, and transition 10 closes it on batch archive, but no transition closes it when the invite expires naturally. The student can never activate through the expired link while the current-state row remains `invited` indefinitely—the exact stranded-row problem transition 14 was added to prevent. Define one atomic/lazy/scheduled expiry behavior, including the event kind, actor kind, lock order and retry/idempotency rule, or do not materialize `invited` until a non-expired activation. Test expiry before activation, concurrent expiry/revoke/join, and reuse of a later valid invite after the expired row is closed.

3. **`canonical_course_label(text)` cannot correctly be `IMMUTABLE` while it reads the course catalogue.** Brief B 5.3a says the function returns the exact name of a matching `disciplines` row and calls it immutable. Its answer can change when a discipline is inserted, so the PostgreSQL volatility promise would be false even though names cannot later be renamed. This can produce stale planner assumptions and must not be the function used for an immutable expression index or generated key. Split the mechanism: use a pure immutable text-normalization function for generated keys and the unique normalized-name index; use a catalogue lookup with appropriate volatility (normally `STABLE`, or perform the lookup directly in the BEFORE trigger/function) for canonical resolution. Pin its search path and test that adding a new discipline immediately changes subsequent canonical resolution without redefinition or reindexing. The canonical-write behavior itself is sound once the volatility boundary is corrected.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Claude's evidence reading is substantially accurate, with two precision corrections. F4 is a literal-name scan for `suspended`; by itself it cannot exclude a transitive call to a differently named helper. The cited RUN 1A function bodies provide the additional support for the join/approve/enroll conclusion, as Brief A 7 now records. Also, F5 proves the mismatch population again has 59 rows with the same reported aggregate characteristics as RUN 8; without identifiers it cannot prove they are the identical 59 physical rows. Neither correction affects a design decision or the execution-verification recommendation.

2. F3B is preserved exactly as pasted but is not strict JSON because six `\\"` sequences in a SQL comment terminate the JSON string under strict parsing. QA reproduced that failure, then corrected only those six escape sequences in memory and recovered all eight expected complete function objects. The raw file should remain unedited; the index explains the limitation adequately.

3. Diagnostic 2 G4 reports trigger name, enabled state and trigger function, which is enough for its stated attachment inventory but not proof of trigger timing, event columns or behavior. Any later decision that depends on those properties must inspect `pg_get_triggerdef` or test the trigger as a real role at the SQL gate.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 27 and last QA-reviewed round 26. Round 27 is frozen at commit `5c61c90`; no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- All three current artifact hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v7 `fe23e46940bbe6e17552b34d4d15f369a73bf9ff30eb78dd958a07b2fe36e6b1`, brief B v7 `bdd40b0c058a5a01f60ffbdefb1c0cbe22f28693e561131d55578137302b5778`, and diagnostic 2 v1 `463190a91f889d00ce1731c5a7d09e24e8208c407c85305b620fc13cc6265f5b`.
- All ten diagnostic 1 result files and the index are present and unchanged from evidence commit `cee8186`. Their shapes correspond to F1, F2, F3A, F3B, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8 and F9 in exact diagnostic hash `2087a3d964dc`; no saved error is present. Nine parse as strict JSON, and F3B is complete and inspectable subject to the documented grid-escaping defect.
- The raw evidence supports the material findings used by the briefs: effective table/column privileges and status-blind policies (F1/F2); no database `account_type` gate on public/browsable content (F2/F3B/F4); 263 active and zero suspended profiles plus the membership/invite counts (F7); five open, valid, item-level `student_access` rows with no duplicate group (F8); zero subject/course conflicts with every stored card/note `discipline_id` null (F6); and longest profile course length 19 with no whitespace/control anomalies (F9).
- The v7 ordered claimant rule now derives the external result from the current link account first, marks former confirmed claims `superseded` atomically, and refuses dismissal of the current link. The submitter, dismissal, supersession and relink cases are behaviorally consistent and testable.
- Transition 2, `create_batch_invite`, D11 and eligibility now consistently restrict account-recipient invitations to platform admin/super_admin, and transition 14 closes an unused invited row on revocation. The remaining lifecycle gap is natural expiry, not actor authority or revocation.
- The normalized canonical-write rule correctly places a BEFORE profile trigger ahead of the existing AFTER archive/restore trigger, canonicalizes access-request text, and prevents platform labels from entering study sessions as custom identities. The remaining issue is the helper's volatility classification.
- Diagnostic 2 contains five statements, each `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation was found. ### A. Blocking findings
1. **The migration did not make the old bearer token effectively unreadable (accepted, verified).** Correct: F1 shows table-level SELECT on `study_groups` for `anon` and `authenticated`, so a column-level revoke denies nothing, and "ignored" leaves a populated token readable by any member under `sg_select_member` (F2). I checked what constrains the fix: `study_groups.invite_token` is a **nullable** `uuid` with default `gen_random_uuid()` (`DATABASE_SCHEMA.md:929`), and **the column cannot be dropped**, because non-batch study groups still use it directly (`GroupDetail.jsx:729,742`) and `join_group_by_token` and `get_group_preview` serve both group types; the admin link also comes from `get_admin_batch_groups` (`AdminDashboard.jsx:1742`). Brief A section 5 now specifies one effective mechanism, sequenced so existing links keep working (A-I10): (1) **backfill** each batch's token into the invite relation with the **same value**; (2) **cut over** readers and writers first (`join_group_by_token` and `get_group_preview` resolve a batch token through the invite relation, non-batch groups keep the old column; `get_admin_batch_groups` returns active invite tokens instead of the column; the admin "Copy Invite Link" reads `list_batch_invites`); (3) only then **clear** `study_groups.invite_token` to NULL for every batch group and keep it cleared with a BEFORE INSERT and UPDATE trigger (otherwise the column default refills it); (4) **rollback** restores the column from the invite relation, which retains the value. The non-batch token stays readable by members as today (outside this brief; listed as residual). Real-role tests select `invite_token` directly on a batch group as an active member, a stale member (preserved `rejected`, `removed`, `left`, `closed` row), an authenticated non-member and an anonymous caller (all get no token), and an existing shared link still joins after step 3. Section 12 now points to section 5 instead of the earlier "dropped or ignored" sentence.
2. **Expiry could strand an `invited` row (accepted, verified).** Correct: transition 14 covered revocation and transition 10 archive, but nothing closed an `invited` row when its invite expired. I took QA's second option because it removes the problem instead of patching it: **no batch writer creates an `invited` row in v1.** A recipient-bound invite (account or confirmed email) is only a record in the invite relation; its recipient is checked when the link is used. Consequences, all applied: transition 2 is withdrawn ("not used in v1"); **transition 14 is deleted**; transition 3 becomes "none, rejected, removed, left or closed to active" for the student named by a recipient-bound invite, with eligibility and the invite checks under the invite row lock, and an expired, revoked or wrong-account use is the generic refusal with no row and no event; `create_batch_invite` and `revoke_batch_invite` write and close no membership row; the eligibility list is transitions 1, 3, 4, 6, 7 and 11; D4 and D11 are updated; the `invited` status stays valid for non-batch groups and existing rows (6.1). With no durable state there is nothing to expire, so QA's event-kind, actor-kind and idempotency questions do not arise; the lock order (invite row, then batch, then membership rows, then events) already decides concurrent expiry, revoke and join. Tests: the recipient-bound journey for platform admin and super_admin (account and confirmed-email recipients, no membership row written, eligibility checked, `accepted_invite` written), a group admin refused at creation, a wrong-account holder refused, expiry before activation (refused, nothing stranded), concurrent expiry, revoke and join in every order, and reuse of a later valid invite after an expired one.
3. **`canonical_course_label` cannot be `IMMUTABLE` while reading the catalogue (accepted, verified).** Correct: inserting a discipline changes its answer. Brief B 5.3a now defines **two functions that are never merged**: `normalize_course_text(text)`, a pure `IMMUTABLE` function (trim, collapse internal whitespace, lower-case; no table access), the only function allowed in an index expression or a generated column, used for the generated keys of 4.1 and for the normalized-name unique index of 6.4; and `resolve_canonical_course_label(text)`, which reads the catalogue, is `STABLE` (never `IMMUTABLE`) with a pinned `search_path` (or the lookup is done inline in the trigger). 4.1, 5.5 and 6.4 now name the right function. New tests: a catalog test of `provolatile` for both functions, and a real-role test that adding a discipline changes the next canonical resolution immediately with no redefinition and no reindex.

## Round 30 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **A still-valid recipient-bound invite can undo a later rejection or removal without administrator review.** Transition 3 sends `rejected`, `removed`, `left` and `closed` directly to `active` whenever the named student uses a valid recipient-bound invite. Invites are deliberately reusable in v1 (single-use is deferred), and neither rejection nor removal revokes or consumes matching recipient invites. Therefore a student can be removed and immediately use the same older invite to reactivate; the same conflict exists after rejection. This contradicts transition 7 and section 6.5, which say a rejected or removed student reopens a `requested` row for review, and it weakens A-I6's preserved negative state. Define precedence behavior: for example, require the recipient invite to have been issued after the latest rejection/removal/leave/closure event, revoke/consume the student's outstanding recipient invites atomically when the negative transition occurs, or route an older invite to `requested` instead of `active`. The rule must use the common lock order and cover remove/reject versus reuse of the same invite in both commit orders. Tests must distinguish the old invite from an explicitly issued later re-invite.

2. **The token cutover omits the batch-creation writer and gives an inoperative rollback order.** Live `create_batch_group` inserts a batch without an explicit token and relies on the `study_groups.invite_token` default. The new BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger will clear that value, but brief A never requires `create_batch_group` to create the initial cohort invite atomically. A batch created after the one-time backfill—or in the interval before reader cutover—can therefore have no row in the invite relation, no working default link and nothing for the revised admin screen to list. The migration must rewrite the batch-creation path before cutover (or specify a deployment lock plus catch-up backfill/dual-write) and test batches created before, during and after the transition. Also, step 4 cannot restore `invite_token` while the clearing trigger remains enabled: the rollback must first remove or disable that trigger, restore the column values, and restore the old readers/writers in a defined safe order. These details are required for A-I10 and for the claimed rollback to work by behavior, not merely by intent.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Brief A section 12 and brief B section 10 still describe diagnostic 2 as “drafted, not audited, not authorized to run.” That is now false: the exact hash was audited in Round 28 and authorized in Round 29. Correct both prerequisite blocks in the next hashed versions so the design does not contradict the status record.

2. The acceptance-test phrase that the invite-row lock decides concurrent “expiry, revoke and join in every order” is imprecise. Expiry is the passage of time, not a writer taking the row lock; the transaction timestamp and the specified boundary comparison decide it. The SQL/test plan should name that comparison and exercise just before, exactly at and just after expiry.

3. Section 5 says a revoke after a completed join “does not delete the request (the administrator may reject it).” That is correct for a cohort invite that produced `requested`, but a recipient-bound invite produces `active`, not a request. Split the two outcomes when resolving blocking finding 1 so the post-join behavior is unambiguous.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 29 and last QA-reviewed round 28. Round 29 and its artifacts are frozen by commit `1d22ce471e751071e66c58ae91461c2c984666fb`; the reviewed files do not differ from that commit, and no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- Both brief hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v8 `59ac9af83ca4f9bb439c9b5ba194feb83e37adbaa8fc2ad36ac0c1f4f2836f72` and brief B v8 `c1c166da17a8ae8b8b5c3078380d6cb929cac7693715fcf045ffc69745f5a278`.
- Diagnostic 2 is byte-for-byte unchanged at `463190a91f889d00ce1731c5a7d09e24e8208c407c85305b620fc13cc6265f5b`. The Round 28 verdict stands: its five runs remain read-only `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT` catalog/aggregate queries, with no DML, DDL, transaction control or application-function invocation and no selected personal or user-authored literal. The Founder's Round 29 authorization therefore still applies to this exact hash; this QA round neither runs nor re-authorizes it.
- The v8 invite model removes the durable batch `invited` row, so expiry or revocation before first use no longer strands membership state. Recipient matching and eligibility occur under the invite lock, wrong-account use writes no membership or event, and a later separately issued valid invite can work. The remaining defect is the precedence of an older still-valid recipient invite after a negative membership decision.
- The old-token design now recognizes that a column revoke is ineffective under table-level SELECT. Backfilling the same token, cutting readers over and then clearing the batch column under a guard trigger would make direct reads return no batch token while preserving existing link values. The remaining defect is deployment coverage for newly created batches and rollback ordering.
- Brief B correctly separates pure `IMMUTABLE normalize_course_text` from catalogue-dependent `STABLE resolve_canonical_course_label`, restricts indexes/generated columns to the immutable function, pins the resolver's search path, and tests that a newly inserted discipline changes the next resolution without function redefinition or reindexing. No contradiction was found among B-I1 to B-I10 in this revision.
- Apart from the invite precedence and migration gaps above, the reviewed mechanisms remain consistent with A-I1 to A-I11: claims are non-authoritative until verified, link supersession is audited, client grants on new relations are denied, preserved membership states grant no access, and eligibility is called on every listed request/activation path.
- The Round 29 corrections to the diagnostic-1 evidence index accurately narrow F4 to a literal-name scan supplemented by RUN 1A bodies and F5 to a repeated count and aggregate profile rather than proof of identical physical rows.

### Residual risks

- Diagnostic 2 has been authorized but its G1-G5 outputs or errors have not yet been received. Live function ACLs, role/status buckets, trigger attachment, catalogue privileges and foreign keys, normalized course variants, and server version therefore remain unverified.
- Brief B has no remaining design-mechanism blocker found in this round, but it is not ready for Gate 1 until at least G1 is run and audited as its own section 10 requires; G4/G5 findings may also require design or later SQL controls. Brief A is not ready for Gate 1 because of both blocking findings and its diagnostic-2 prerequisites.
- Gate 4 for diagnostic 1 hash `2087a3d964dc` remains pending an explicit Founder acceptance; Round 29 correctly did not reinterpret the repeated authorization as execution verification.
- Exact SQL, rollback scripts, privilege revokes, trigger ordering, real-role behavior and concurrency remain for later gates. No SQL, build, test, database mutation, browser or external service was run by QA.
- The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so a pre-existing recipient-bound invite cannot silently reverse a later rejection or removal, and so batch creation plus rollback are fully included in the token cutover. Correct the stale diagnostic-2 status wording in both briefs and return changed files under new SHA-256 hashes and a new commit SHA. Diagnostic 2 remains safe to run under the Founder's existing authorization for exact hash `463190a91f88`, but its results must be saved unchanged and audited before the evidence-dependent Gate 1 decisions. Brief B's revised volatility mechanism is acceptable subject to that evidence; brief A is not ready for Gate 1. No gate is approved by this QA round, and this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

### A. Evidence of follow-up diagnostic 2 (run 04/10/2026, hash `463190a91f88`)
**Files (saved unedited from the Founder's paste, all five parse as strict JSON):** `docs/discussions/evidence/T-001_FU2-G1_04-10-2026.json` to `G5`, and the index `T-001_FU2-index_04-10-2026.md` (environment, hash, notes and my reading). I re-hashed the SQL file before saving and it matched. **Status: not yet audited by QA; Gate 4 not given for this hash.** What the results show (the index has the sources):
1. **G1: no normalized variants exist** in any of the eight dependent text columns. Profiles: 257 exact, 5 other text, 2 blank; flashcards 2,850 exact; decks 223 exact; notes 127 exact, 1 other; access requests 5 exact, 1 other; batch groups and enrollments only exact or blank. No repair is needed today.
2. **G2: eight of the 21 named functions are executable by `anon` and `PUBLIC`**: `approve_batch_join_request`, `reject_batch_join_request`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `join_group_by_token`, `get_group_preview`, `submit_access_request`, `is_admin`, `is_super_admin`. The first four are not meant to be; the app calls `join_group_by_token` only for a signed-in user (`GroupJoin.jsx:44-50`). All 21 are SECURITY DEFINER with a `search_path` set. 30 of 191 public functions are anon-executable (counted by script; I first wrote 29 and corrected it). `get_admin_pending_batch_requests()` and `get_study_time_stats(...)` are also anon-executable by name; their bodies are not captured (OPEN).
3. **G3:** in batch groups the only `admin` membership is one platform admin's; the 11 student group admins are all in non-batch groups; 5 batch `requested` rows now (4 earlier; data moved).
4. **G4:** the profile guard trigger is attached live; there are **no** triggers on `disciplines`, `subjects`, `access_requests`, `study_group_members` or `study_groups`; `disciplines` and `subjects` have only primary-key indexes; `anon` and `authenticated` hold all five privileges, including TRUNCATE, on `disciplines`, `subjects` and `topics` (their policies were not captured, OPEN). Foreign keys: cards and notes restrict subject deletion; decks are `ON DELETE SET NULL`; topics, subjects and `profile_courses` cascade.
5. **G5:** PostgreSQL 17.6 (`NULLS NOT DISTINCT` is available).
6. **A finding from the code, not from the diagnostic:** the admin screen `BulkUploadTopics.jsx` writes `disciplines`, `subjects` and `topics` directly from the browser (for example it inserts a discipline from a title-cased typed name, line 218), so any privilege clean-up of those tables must keep that admin page working.

### B. Response to QA's Round 30
1. **An older recipient-bound invite could undo a later rejection or removal (accepted, verified).** Correct: transition 3 sent `rejected`, `removed`, `left` and `closed` straight to `active` for any valid recipient-bound invite, invites are reusable in v1, and nothing consumed them. Brief A 6.2 now has an **invite precedence rule**: each membership row carries **`last_inactive_at`**, written in the same transaction as every negative transition (rejected, removed, left, closed). `join_group_by_token` compares, under the common lock order, the invite's `created_at` with `last_inactive_at`: an invite **created after** it (an explicit later re-invite) gives `active` (transition 3); an invite **created before it, or a cohort invite**, gives a fresh `requested` row for administrator review (transitions 7 and 11, rewritten to say so), with the generic message of 6.5. A student with no row is unaffected. The two commit orders are fixed by the lock order (remove, reject, leave and archive take the batch lock before any membership row; join takes invite, then batch, then membership): negative transition first, join sees the new `last_inactive_at` and routes to `requested`; join first, the student is `active` and the later removal proceeds normally. Tests (section 5 and 6.2): remove or reject then the same older invite gives `requested`, never `active`; an invite issued afterwards gives `active`; both commit orders for remove versus join and reject versus join; leave then an older invite; archive-closed then an older invite after restore. 6.5 and A-I6's preserved negative state are consistent with this.
2. **Batch creation and rollback order missing from the migration (accepted, verified).** Correct: the repo copy of `create_batch_group` (`sprint8.0/02_FUNCTIONS_batch_join_approval.sql:341-358`; the live body was not captured, so this is VERIFIED-in-repo only) inserts a batch with no token and relies on the column default, which the clearing trigger would remove; and a restore cannot work while that trigger is enabled. Brief A section 5 now has six ordered steps: (1) deploy the new objects with no behaviour change; (2) **rewrite `create_batch_group`** to create the batch's initial cohort invite in the same transaction (dual-write while the column default exists: the invite takes the token the insert produced; after the clear it generates a fresh one); (3) backfill every existing batch **idempotently, with catch-up**: run again immediately before the clear, with a verification query that must return 0 batches without an active invite, and a short pause on batch creation across that check; (4) cut over readers and writers; (5) clear the column for batch groups and install the guard trigger; (6) **rollback in a defined order**: pause creation, disable or drop the clearing trigger, restore each batch's column from its oldest non-revoked cohort invite (a batch whose invites are all revoked gets a fresh token, so a revoked token is never revived), restore the old function bodies and admin screen, verify that existing links join, then retire the new objects. Tests cover batches created before the backfill, between steps 2 and 5, and after step 5, and the rollback order.

### C. Non-blocking points
1. **Stale diagnostic 2 wording (accepted).** Brief A section 12 and brief B section 10 now say diagnostic 2 was audited in Round 28, authorized in Round 29 and run on 04/10/2026, that its results are saved, and that their audit and Gate 4 are still pending. The same files cite G1 to G5 where they use them.
2. **Expiry comparison (accepted).** Brief A section 5 now defines it: an invite is valid only while `expires_at` is NULL or `transaction_timestamp() < expires_at`, so at exactly `expires_at` it is expired; the test plan exercises just before, exactly at and just after, and concurrent revoke and join in both orders; "expiry" is no longer described as a lock competitor.
3. **Post-join revoke split (accepted).** Section 5 now states the three cases: a cohort invite that produced `requested` (the request stays; the administrator may reject), a recipient-bound invite that produced `active` (the membership stays; the administrator may remove, transition 8), and a recipient-bound invite routed to `requested` by the precedence rule (behaves like the cohort case).

### D. Where the diagnostic 2 evidence changes the briefs (v9)

## Round 32 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The invite-precedence timestamp is not yet guaranteed to represent the serialized order of the invite and negative decision.** The batch and membership locks serialize the writes, but a timestamp does not automatically inherit that order. In PostgreSQL, the common `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` value is fixed at transaction start. A removal/rejection transaction can start first, pause before acquiring the batch lock, then commit its negative transition after a later-started invite transaction; if `last_inactive_at` uses the earlier transaction timestamp, the already-existing invite has a later `created_at` and is incorrectly treated as an explicit post-removal re-invite. The old invite can then reactivate the student, contrary to A-I6 and the agreed “old link creates a fresh pending request” behavior. Define one ordering value assigned under the shared serialization point: for example, assign both values with `clock_timestamp()` only after the batch lock is held, or use a monotonic per-batch/per-membership generation or event sequence. Tests must stagger transaction **start** order independently of lock/commit order, not merely test two ordinary commit orders, and must prove that a negative transition serialized after invite creation always makes that invite old.

2. **The rollback token selection can revive an expired invite and cannot satisfy the claim that existing links still join.** Step 6 restores the oldest “non-revoked” cohort invite, but does not exclude an expired invite. The restored old `join_group_by_token` has no invite-relation expiry check, so that token becomes permanently usable again. Also, the legacy column can hold only one token, while the new relation permits multiple cohort and recipient-bound tokens; restoring one value and retiring the new objects necessarily breaks every other live link. Define a safe, explicit rollback contract: select only a currently valid token and state what happens when it later reaches its former expiry, or mint one new non-expiring rollback token; either retain a compatibility reader for other live tokens or explicitly invalidate and communicate them instead of claiming all existing links survive. Tests must include an expired-but-not-revoked oldest invite, all cohort invites revoked/expired, multiple live cohort invites, a live recipient-bound invite, and the exact set of tokens that work after rollback.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Brief B 5.3b now correctly reports G1, but the next sentence still says the five non-platform profile values “were never tested for a normalized match.” G1 did test them and classified them as `other_text`, which means they are not normalized matches. Remove that historical sentence or rewrite it in the past tense.

2. Brief B section 10 still lists QA audit of F6 and F9 as outstanding even though Round 28 independently audited those files. After this round, G1-G5 are also audited. This is status wording rather than a mechanism defect and should be corrected in the next hashed brief.

3. Brief A's invite acceptance-test bullet contains nested bold markup around “expiry boundary,” which renders incorrectly. This is editorial only.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 31 and last QA-reviewed round 30. Round 31 and brief v9 are frozen by commit `fd9e79c`; diagnostic-2 evidence is frozen by commit `5dd85ec`; the reviewed files match those commits and no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- The brief hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v9 `b7bb6d933f6fee62928e4bbd1df4cdaff8bc530646506abb8b702356a41b3fcc` and brief B v9 `38b486a892b5d3e1aceebcff933b3f653b9aaa38f35248daa99792fcda6251a6`. Diagnostic 2 remains exact hash `463190a91f889d00ce1731c5a7d09e24e8208c407c85305b620fc13cc6265f5b`.
- All five evidence files are present, unchanged from `5dd85ec`, and parse as strict JSON. Their shapes match the five authorized statements: G1 has eight aggregate course-text buckets; G2 has 21 named function ACL rows and 30 anon-executable function signatures; G3 has seven role/status buckets; G4 has 30 table-privilege results, three triggers, 15 indexes and eight foreign keys; G5 reports PostgreSQL `17.6` / `170006`. No saved error or personal/user-authored literal is present.
- Claude's evidence reading is accurate. G1 contains no `variant_of_canonical` bucket and supports no current normalization repair. G2 shows eight of the named functions executable by both `anon` and `PUBLIC`, all 21 named functions SECURITY DEFINER with a configured search path, and 30 of 191 public functions executable by `anon`. G3 shows the only batch membership role `admin` belongs to a platform admin, while the 11 student group admins are non-batch. G4 supports the stated privilege, trigger, index and foreign-key facts. G5 supports availability of `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`.
- **Gate 4 recommendation for diagnostic 2:** QA recommends that the Founder accept execution verification for exact hash `463190a91f88`. The authorized hash matches, G1-G5 are all saved, strict-parsing and reconcilable to their corresponding statements, and no error is reported. QA does not approve Gate 4; the Founder must accept it separately.
- The normal sequential behavior of the v9 invite rule is improved: a same older recipient invite after rejection, removal, leave or closure routes to `requested`; a genuinely later invite can activate; no durable `invited` row is stranded by expiry or revocation. Blocking finding 1 concerns how concurrent order is represented, not those ordinary sequential cases.
- The forward token migration now covers new batch creation, idempotent catch-up, a zero-missing-invite check, reader cutover and clearing-trigger installation. Disabling the trigger before restoring the old column fixes the mechanical rollback-order defect from Round 30. Blocking finding 2 concerns which token is restored and the unavoidable one-token compatibility limit.
- Brief B's G1-driven no-repair conclusion, immutable normalizer/stable resolver split, normalized unique discipline-name guard, catalogue write guards, and admin-write-path requirement are behaviorally coherent. The `BulkUploadTopics.jsx` references are correct: it directly inserts disciplines, inserts/updates subjects and inserts topics, so a later privilege closure must preserve that workflow through an admin-only path.
- No contradiction was found among B-I1 to B-I10. Apart from the ordering and rollback findings above, A-I1 to A-I11 remain internally consistent and the evidence-driven ACL changes are correctly deferred to the SQL gate with real-role tests.

### Residual risks

- G4 proves effective table privileges but did not capture policies on `disciplines`, `subjects` or `topics`; whether browser roles can currently exercise the write grants remains open. The SQL gate must read those policies before choosing admin-only policies versus SECURITY DEFINER writers.
- G2 proves EXECUTE reachability, not live function-body authorization. Repo copies of `get_admin_pending_batch_requests()` and `get_study_time_stats(...)` contain internal guards, but their live bodies were not captured; the planned reader audit must verify them before relying on those protections.
- The live body of `create_batch_group` remains uncaptured. Forward migration, rollback, trigger behavior, privilege revokes, exact locks and real-role/concurrency behavior remain for SQL review and execution gates.
- Brief B has no remaining design blocker found and may go to the Founder for Gate 1 at exact hash `38b486a892b5`, with the non-blocking wording corrections and policy/body checks carried forward. Brief A is not ready for Gate 1 because of the two blocking findings.
- No design, SQL, execution-verification or other gate is approved by this QA round. No SQL, build, test, database mutation, browser or external service was run by QA. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so invite precedence is based on an order value assigned at the shared serialization point, and define a rollback that never revives expired tokens and states exactly which of multiple live links survive. Correct the non-blocking brief-B evidence wording in the next hashed version. Independently, QA recommends Founder acceptance of Gate 4 for diagnostic 2 hash `463190a91f88`. Brief B may proceed to the Founder for Gate 1 at hash `38b486a892b5`; brief A may not. This round approves no gate and is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

## Round 33 · Claude · 04/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 32 (REVISION REQUIRED, two blocking, three non-blocking), all accepted after checking each against the text; no pushback. QA's Round 32 is committed as `9638872`. Brief A is revised to v10 (mechanism changes); **brief B is revised to v10 with editorial changes only** (exactly three lines, section D). Both diagnostics are unchanged. No SQL was run by Claude; no application code changed. This round is committed with the revised files; QA should audit that new commit.

### A. Blocking findings
1. **The invite-precedence timestamp does not represent the serialized order (accepted, verified).** Correct: in PostgreSQL `now()` and `transaction_timestamp()` are fixed when a transaction **starts**, so a removal that starts first but takes the batch lock after a later-started invite creation has committed would stamp an earlier time than that invite, and the old invite would wrongly look newer and reactivate the student. Brief A 6.2 now compares **order values assigned under the serialization point, not clocks**: each batch has a monotonic counter **`order_seq`** on its `study_groups` row, incremented only while that **batch row lock is held**. `create_batch_invite`, the batch-creation path and the backfill take the batch lock, increment the counter and store it as the invite's immutable **`created_seq`**; every negative transition (reject, remove, leave, archive-close), which already takes the batch lock, increments it once and stores it as **`last_inactive_seq`** on each membership row it ends (archive stamps all rows it closes with one value); `join_group_by_token` reads both under the batch lock: invite `created_seq` greater than `last_inactive_seq` gives transition 3 (`active`), otherwise transition 7 or 11 (`requested`). The order of the values is the serialized order whatever order the transactions started or committed in. Timestamps stay for display and audit and are never compared. 6.1, the migration step 1, transitions 3, 7 and 11 and 6.5 now refer to the order value. **Tests added** as QA asked: stagger the transaction **start** order independently of the lock and commit order (a removal or rejection that starts first but takes the lock after a later-started invite creation has committed must make that invite old); the reverse; leave and archive-close cases; both commit orders; and a catalog test that `order_seq` is changed only inside functions that hold the batch lock.
2. **The rollback could revive an expired token and cannot keep every link (accepted, verified).** Correct on both counts: step 6 restored the oldest non-revoked invite without excluding an expired one, the restored legacy join has no expiry check, and the legacy column holds one token while the new relation holds many. Brief A section 5, step 6 is rewritten as an explicit contract: (b) **before anything is retired, produce a rollback report** listing, per batch, every live invite token (valid, unrevoked, unexpired, cohort and recipient-bound) and which of them will stop working; (d) restore each batch's column from its **original cohort invite** (the first one) **only if it is not revoked and has no expiry**, otherwise **mint one new non-expiring token** and give it to the administrator; a revoked or expiring token is **never** restored. **What survives, stated exactly:** one link per batch works after rollback, the restored token (normally the original shared link, so it keeps working); every additional cohort invite and every recipient-bound invite stops working, and the rollback report is the communication to administrators; no compatibility reader is kept. Tests: the oldest invite expired but not revoked (a new token is minted and the expired one stays dead); all cohort invites revoked or expired; several live cohort invites; a live recipient-bound invite (reported as invalidated); the exact set of tokens that work before and after rollback; the trigger-disabled restore order. The forward-migration claim A-I10 (existing shared links keep working) is unchanged; the rollback claim is now limited to what the contract says.

### B. Non-blocking points
1. **Brief B 5.3b historical sentence (accepted).** Replaced: RUN 5A identified the five non-platform profile values by exact comparison only; G1 tested them and classified them as other text, so they are not variants.
2. **Brief B section 10 audit status (accepted).** Now states that the F6 and F9 results were audited in Round 28 and the G1 to G5 results in Round 32 (which also recommended Gate 4 for diagnostic 2; not yet accepted by the Founder), and that what remains before Gate 1 is the Founder's decisions E1 to E10 and the carried-forward checks (policies on the catalogue tables).

## Round 34 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The counter is not yet protected from the live direct `study_groups` insert path.** The order mechanism says `order_seq` changes only in functions holding the batch lock and the migration says every batch is created through rewritten `create_batch_group`. Live evidence contradicts that as a complete control: F1 shows table-level INSERT on `study_groups`, and F2's authenticated `sg_insert` policy checks only `created_by = auth.uid()`; it does not require `is_batch_group = false`. G4 shows no guard trigger on `study_groups`. An authenticated caller can therefore insert a batch-shaped row directly, bypass the initial-invite write, and supply the new counter column unless the SQL closes that path. After step 5 the clearing trigger leaves such a batch with no column token and no invite; an arbitrary counter value can also break or exhaust the sequence. The design must require, before dual-write/cutover, that direct client INSERT cannot create a batch and cannot set `order_seq` (for example, restrict `sg_insert` to non-batch rows and use a protected server-only batch writer; remember that a column revoke alone does not override a table grant). Add real-role attempts to insert a batch, set the counter, update it and TRUNCATE the table, plus a proof that non-batch group creation still works.

2. **The backfill's “without an invite” predicate can skip the legacy token and make the rollback's “original cohort invite” the wrong row.** Step 1 deploys callable invite functions before step 3. If an existing batch receives any new invite in that interval, step 3 sees an invite and does not copy the still-shared `study_groups.invite_token`. The zero-batches-without-an-active-invite check can pass even though the legacy token is absent; step 4 then breaks the old shared link, violating A-I10. The first invite may also be the new one, so rollback no longer has a reliable definition of “original.” Backfill must idempotently ensure a specifically marked immutable original/migration invite whose token equals every non-NULL legacy batch token, regardless of other invite rows, and the pre-clear verification must check that exact mapping. Alternatively, prevent invite creation until the original backfill is complete. Test an existing batch that receives a new cohort and a recipient-bound invite before catch-up, then prove the legacy token survives cutover and the correct original is selected on rollback.

3. **Existing or deployment-window inactive rows have no defined `last_inactive_seq` initialization.** G3 showed no negative-status batch memberships at the diagnostic snapshot, but step 1 intentionally makes no behavior change, so an old writer can still close a requested row before the rewritten negative writers are live. Step 3 assigns `created_seq` to the backfilled invite but does not assign `last_inactive_seq` to an already `closed` row; SQL comparison with NULL does not implement either transition 3 or transition 11. The migration must either pause/archive/reject/remove/leave writers across this window, or initialize every pre-existing negative batch row under the batch lock so the legacy invite is older and reuse routes to `requested`. A final pre-cutover query must reject NULL sequence values on negative rows. Test a requested row archived after step 1 but before writer cutover, then restore and reuse the old link.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Brief A section 12 still says diagnostic-2 G1-G5 have not been audited. Round 32 audited them and recommended Gate 4. Correct this status wording in the next hash.

2. The order-counter catalog test can show which functions update `order_seq`, but cannot by itself prove they acquired the batch lock first. Keep the staggered real-session concurrency tests as the behavioral proof and make the SQL review verify statement order within each function.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 33 and last QA-reviewed round 32. Round 33 and both v10 briefs are frozen by commit `e94e800`; their working copies match that commit, and no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- The hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v10 `a9c83f6f21ad5305ebaa00e7d5efe89ffc697e5b22f2635dbe83ca86e744015d` and brief B v10 `0fe77dec72dc0ea606f62fc37c79cd45cdada70c361fa38a523370b2df40683a`.
- **Brief B editorial-only confirmation:** the diff from audited v9 hash `38b486a892b5` changes exactly three existing lines: the version line, the corrected 5.3b sentence, and the corrected section 10 audit-status sentence. No mechanism, invariant, test contract or decision E1-E10 changed. Brief B remains ready for Founder Gate 1 at final hash `0fe77dec72dc`.
- **Staggered-start behavior, function paths:** the per-batch counter fixes the timestamp inversion from Round 32 when all writers use the contract. If invite creation takes the batch lock first it stores sequence N and a later serialized negative transition stores N+1, so the invite is old and reuse requests review regardless of transaction start times. If the negative transition stores N first and explicit re-invite stores N+1, direct activation is allowed. `join_group_by_token` reads both values while holding the same batch lock; archive stamps every row it closes with one serialized value. Batch creation and backfill also take that lock. Findings 1 and 3 concern bypass and initialization, not this core ordering rule.
- **Rollback behavior:** subject to finding 2 identifying the true original, the revised contract passes the requested cases. An expired-but-not-revoked original is never restored; all revoked/expiring cohorts cause a new non-expiring token; with several live cohorts only the eligible original survives and the rest are reported as invalidated; recipient-bound invites are reported and stop working; exactly one stated token works per batch afterward; disabling the clearing trigger before restoration prevents it from erasing the restored value. The contract no longer falsely promises that every new-system link survives rollback.
- The expiry boundary, revoke/join split, no-durable-`invited` design, eligibility calls, claims/link authority, relation ACL contract and evidence-driven function revokes remain consistent with A-I1 to A-I11 once the three migration/control findings are resolved. No contradiction was found among B-I1 to B-I10.
- Gate 4 for diagnostic 2 hash `463190a91f88` remains recommended by QA in Round 32 and remains pending explicit Founder acceptance; this round neither withdraws nor grants it.

### Residual risks

- The live body of `create_batch_group` is still uncaptured. Exact counter types, overflow handling, immutable original-invite marking, function lock statement order, deployment pauses and rollback reporting remain for SQL review and real-role/concurrency tests.
- Live bodies of the two anon-executable readers and catalogue-table policies remain open as recorded in Round 32. These do not change the v10 mechanism verdict but remain SQL-gate prerequisites.
- Brief A is not ready for Gate 1 because the direct batch-write path, original-token backfill and inactive-row initialization are unresolved. Brief B is ready for Gate 1 at `0fe77dec72dc`.
- No design, SQL, execution-verification or other gate is approved by this QA round. No SQL, build, test, database mutation, browser or external service was run by QA. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A to close direct client batch/counter writes, guarantee and mark the legacy-token backfill even when other invites already exist, and initialize or exclude every negative row before sequence-based routing begins. The counter itself handles staggered transaction starts correctly on protected function paths, and the rollback contract handles the listed token cases once the original invite is unambiguous. Brief B v10 is confirmed editorial-only and may proceed to the Founder for Gate 1 at hash `0fe77dec72dc`. No gate is approved by this round; the disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

**Scope.** (A) Record of the Founder's three acts; (B) response to QA's Round 34 (REVISION REQUIRED, three blocking, two non-blocking), all accepted after checking each against the text and the saved evidence, no pushback; (C) a request to the Founder about brief B's decisions. This round is committed with the revised brief; QA should audit that new commit.

### A. What the Gate 1 approval of brief B covers, and what I need from the Founder
Brief B's file states its decisions E1 to E10 with a recommended answer for each, and E3 is marked "needs a Founder choice". The message approved "brief B" as written; it did not name the decisions. **I have not assumed that the recommended answers are accepted.** Section C lists them; the Founder is asked to confirm them, or change any, in one reply.

### B. Response to QA's Round 34
1. **The order counter is not protected from the direct `study_groups` insert path (accepted, verified).** Correct, and it is an existing hole as well as a threat to the new design. I re-read the saved evidence: F1 gives `anon` and `authenticated` INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on `study_groups`; F2's policy `sg_insert` checks only `created_by = auth.uid()` (not `is_batch_group`); `sg_update_creator` already requires `is_batch_group = false` on the old and the new row; G4 shows no trigger on `study_groups`. So today a signed-in user can insert a batch-shaped row directly. Brief A section 5 gains **step 0, before anything depends on the counter or the invite relation**: replace `sg_insert` so a client may insert only rows that are not batch-shaped (`is_batch_group = false` **and** `group_type <> 'batch'`); keep `sg_update_creator` (no edit of a batch row, no conversion to one); add a BEFORE INSERT and UPDATE trigger that forces `order_seq` to its default for non-batch rows and refuses any change to it that does not come from a counter-owning function (a function-local marker set inside the SECURITY DEFINER writers, never client-settable through the API); revoke TRUNCATE and any unneeded privilege from `anon` and `authenticated` (a column revoke does not override a table grant, as QA noted). Batch groups are then created only by the protected `create_batch_group`. Real-role tests: a client cannot insert a batch-shaped row (either shape), cannot set, update or reset `order_seq`, cannot TRUNCATE; and non-batch group creation, joining and editing still work. 6.8 states the same.
2. **The backfill predicate can skip the legacy token and make "original" ambiguous (accepted, verified).** Correct: if an existing batch received any new invite between step 1 (callable invite functions) and step 3, a "batch without an invite" test would skip copying the still-shared legacy token, the old link would die at cutover, and rollback could not identify the original. v11: the invite relation has an **`is_original` flag**, at most one per batch (partial unique index), and rollback restores the invite marked `is_original`, never "the first by date". **`create_batch_invite` refuses (`original_missing`) for a batch that has no original yet**, so no invite can precede the original. The backfill is rewritten as an **idempotent exact-mapping step**: for every batch with a non-NULL legacy token, ensure an original invite whose token equals that value **whatever other invites exist**; if an original exists with a different token, stop and report. The pre-clear verification checks that **exact mapping**, not a count. Tests: an administrator trying to add a cohort or recipient-bound invite before the original exists is refused; an existing batch that then receives more invites still keeps its legacy token through cutover and has the right original selected on rollback; the mapping check fails when any legacy token lacks an original with that value.
3. **No initialization for existing or deployment-window negative rows (accepted, verified).** Correct: a NULL `last_inactive_seq` makes neither transition 3 nor 11 evaluate. v11 does two things. (a) **Initialization** under the batch lock in step 3: every pre-existing row with a negative status (today only `closed` rows from archive can exist, because rejection and removal delete rows) is stamped with a value **after** the original invite's `created_seq`, so the legacy invite is older and reuse routes to `requested` (transition 11). (b) A **safety trigger on `study_group_members`**, deployed in step 1, that whenever a row is written with a negative status and a NULL `last_inactive_seq` takes the batch lock, increments the counter and stamps it, so no writer, old or new, can leave a negative row without a value in the deployment window. The pre-cutover verification requires **zero** negative rows with a NULL value, and a CHECK that negative rows carry a value is added `NOT VALID` and validated only after the initialization (a table CHECK is evaluated on every update). Test: a requested row archived after step 1 but before writer cutover gets its value from the trigger, and after restore the old link routes to `requested`, never `active`.

### C. Non-blocking points

2. **Catalog test cannot prove lock order (accepted).** The tests now say the SQL review verifies the statement order inside each function that changes `order_seq` (batch lock first), with the staggered real-session tests as the behavioural proof.

Recommend yes.
- **E2** CMA and CS students use custom text in v1; adding CMA and CS disciplines is a separate catalogue decision. Recommend yes.
- **E3** One course catalogue (active platform courses plus the six CMA and CS labels, defined once) read by Signup, Profile Settings, the access form and the picker, with the per-surface projections of 5.5. **Needs a Founder choice.**
- **E4** In-app sessions are not classified by course in v1; reports are separated by source. Recommend yes.
- **E5** Platform names by live join (safe because renames are prohibited); custom text stored as label plus generated key. Recommend yes.
- **E6** Professors see totals only. Recommend confirm.
- **E7** The label limit is fixed at 120; labels are never truncated; an over-limit outlier uses a student-confirmed alias, reconfirmed on later logs. Recommend yes (no over-limit value exists today, F9).
- **E8** Discipline renames and hard deletes are prohibited in v1 and guarded; discipline names are unique under the catalogue normalization (deactivate instead of delete). Recommend yes.
- **E9** A student whose current course is a deactivated discipline keeps it as their current course with the same identity, marked "no longer offered", until they choose another; it is not offered to others. Recommend yes.
- **E10** Keep deriving the course from the subject at read time in v1; require `discipline_id` only on new platform rows; no backfill of existing rows. Recommend yes.

## Round 36 · QA · 04/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **Step 5 cannot clear the live `invite_token` column because the live column is `NOT NULL`.** Brief A section 5 says the live column is nullable, then requires `UPDATE study_groups SET invite_token = NULL` for every batch and a trigger that keeps it NULL. The saved live schema in RUN 6 says `study_groups.invite_token` is `nullable: NO`, with the random UUID default; Round 10 also recorded it as NOT NULL. The first clear therefore fails before the new reader contract can take effect. The migration must either choose a different effective-denial mechanism or explicitly relax the NOT NULL constraint at the safe point, preserve the non-batch guarantee, and define rollback symmetrically: restore one non-NULL token to every batch, verify zero NULLs, and reinstate NOT NULL before the legacy readers/writers are considered restored. Test the forward clear, creation of non-batch and batch groups after cutover, and rollback back to the exact live nullability contract. Until this is resolved, A-I10 and the claimed cutover behavior are not executable.

2. **Step 0 closes direct INSERT but not conversion of an existing ordinary group into the other live batch shape.** F2 proves `sg_insert` currently checks only `created_by = auth.uid()`, so the proposed INSERT predicate (`is_batch_group = false` and `group_type <> 'batch'`) correctly closes the direct INSERT found in Round 34. But the live `sg_update_creator` policy's USING and WITH CHECK expressions require only `created_by = auth.uid()` and `is_batch_group = false`; they do not constrain `group_type`. RUN 6 shows `group_type` is an independent non-NULL column whose CHECK permits `batch`. A creator can therefore update an ordinary row to `group_type = 'batch'` while leaving `is_batch_group = false`. The live join logic uses `group_type = 'batch'` as load-bearing behavior, so this is a batch-shaped conversion even though the policy still passes. Replace or supplement the UPDATE policy/guard so a client cannot set either batch marker or create an inconsistent pair, and test both INSERT shapes and both UPDATE conversions from an ordinary row. The current statement that `sg_update_creator` means a client “can neither edit a batch row nor turn a row into one” is false.

3. **The safety trigger protects only a missing sequence, not the latest negative transition.** It runs only when a negative row has `last_inactive_seq IS NULL`. That fails a deployment-window case in which the row already carries an older negative sequence: for example, a row has `last_inactive_seq = 10` from an earlier rejection, is reopened to `requested`, a recipient invite is then created at 11, and the old archive writer closes the requested row after step 1. Because the field is already non-NULL, the safety trigger leaves 10 in place. After restore, invite 11 compares as newer and activates the student, silently reversing the later archive. The same NULL-only condition lets the live `sgm_insert_admin` direct path supply any non-NULL sequence and bypass the stamp; the proposed CHECK proves only presence, not provenance or order. During the period in which old/direct writers exist, the trigger must assign a fresh counter value on every actual transition into a negative state regardless of the incoming value, or those write paths must be paused/closed before the sequence is relied on. Add tests for a reused row with an existing old sequence, an invite between the old and new negative events, and a real-role direct insert supplying a forged non-NULL value. This is required for A-I6's “latest negative event” rule, not merely for non-NULL initialization.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Section 12's final paragraph still lists “QA's audit of the G1 to G5 results” as OPEN, although the preceding paragraph and the status block correctly record the Round 32 audit and Founder Gate 4 acceptance. Remove the stale OPEN item in the next hash.

2. The `is_original` mechanism is behaviorally sound against the Round 34 ambiguity on the current baseline: F7 found zero duplicate legacy tokens, RUN 6 makes every current token non-NULL, `create_batch_invite` refuses before an original exists, the backfill checks exact token equality, and rollback selects the marked row rather than creation time. At the SQL gate, make `is_original` itself immutable after insertion (not only `created_seq`) and test that no create/revoke/list path can demote, replace or add a second original.

3. The counter guard's `set_config(..., true)` marker must be treated as an implementation security boundary at the SQL gate. Prove with real roles that no exposed call can forge it, scope it transaction-locally, and ensure the writer cannot leave a usable marker behind for an unrelated statement. A custom setting is not authorization by itself.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 35 and last QA-reviewed round 34. Round 35 and brief A v11 are frozen by commit `605de38`; no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`. The brief A working copy hashes exactly to `080a0906f237d2fc8c52fb061e0a014a1591404936188f1963a0baeba9f5f468`.
- Brief B hashes exactly to `0fe77dec72dc0ea606f62fc37c79cd45cdada70c361fa38a523370b2df40683a` and has no diff between the committed Round 34 and Round 35 states. The Founder's Gate 1 approval for that exact brief B hash therefore stands.
- Step 0 is correctly placed before counter/invite reliance; its new INSERT predicate addresses both independent batch markers, it recognizes that table grants defeat a column-only revoke, and its TRUNCATE revocation and real-role tests match F1/F2. G4 confirms that neither `study_groups` nor `study_group_members` has a pre-existing trigger that would supply these controls. Finding 2 is the remaining UPDATE-policy path.
- Original-invite marking, the partial unique index, `original_missing` refusal, exact legacy-token equality check, mismatch stop and marked-row rollback collectively resolve the “first invite” and count-only defects from Round 34 on the evidenced current data. Additional invites cannot cause the legacy token to be skipped.
- Initialization under the batch lock correctly orders a previously unsequenced negative row after its original legacy invite, and the pre-cutover zero-NULL query catches missed rows. Finding 3 concerns repeated deployment-window negative transitions and forged non-NULL values, not the initial NULL backfill case.
- The protected-function order counter continues to represent serialized order correctly under staggered transaction starts, and the rollback token-selection cases audited in Round 34 remain correct once the true original is marked. No additional contradiction was found among A-I1 to A-I11 beyond the executable conflicts identified in findings 1 to 3.
- Gate 4 for diagnostic 2 hash `463190a91f88` and Gate 1 for brief B hash `0fe77dec72dc` were explicitly accepted by the Founder in Round 35. This QA round does not alter either exact-hash approval.

### Residual risks

- The live `create_batch_group` body remains uncaptured. Counter types and overflow, trigger execution order, marker enforcement, exact constraint changes, immutable-original enforcement, deployment pauses and rollback reporting remain for SQL review and real-role/concurrency tests.
- The direct membership privileges in F1/F2, old delete-based reject/remove/leave writers, and shared non-batch table behavior make deployment ordering security-sensitive. Closing batch paths must not silently break ordinary-group creation, invitation, editing, leaving or deletion.
- Brief A v11 is not ready for Founder Gate 1 while the NOT NULL cutover failure, UPDATE conversion path and stale-sequence deployment case remain. The existing Founder approvals cover only the exact diagnostic-2 and brief-B hashes stated above.
- No SQL, build, test, database mutation, browser or external service was run by QA. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so the forward and rollback migrations account for the live NOT NULL token contract; close `group_type = 'batch'` conversion in the UPDATE path as well as direct INSERT; and make the deployment-window safety control stamp every latest negative transition rather than only NULL rows, while rejecting forged sequence values. Preserve the original-invite and exact-mapping controls, which pass this audit on the saved baseline. Brief B remains Founder-approved and unchanged at `0fe77dec72dc`. No new gate is approved by this round; this disposition is audit advice rather than authorization to implement, execute SQL, deploy, commit or push.

**Scope.** (A) The Founder's question, answered with the evidence; (B) response to QA's Round 36 (REVISION REQUIRED, three blocking, three non-blocking), all accepted after checking each against the saved live evidence, no pushback; (C) the errors I found myself in the same pass; (D) a third read-only diagnostic (drafted, not audited, not authorized to run).
