### A. The Founder's question, answered
**Findings 1 and 2 of Round 36 are my errors, made against evidence I already held.** The live schema in `RUN 6` (saved 04/10/2026) says `study_groups.invite_token` is `NOT NULL`; I wrote "nullable" from the repo document `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md:929`. The same file shows `group_type` as an independent column, and the live `join_group_by_token` (`RUN 1A`) says in its own comment that `group_type = 'batch'` is honoured "because it drifted once"; I checked the update policy against one marker only, the one the existing policy mentions. These are the same two kinds of mistake as in earlier rounds:
- **Kind 1, a fact taken from a repo document or from memory instead of from the saved live evidence.** Earlier instances: the unique-index NULL behaviour (Round 20), "identity" claims not tested against the stated principle (Round 18), and in Round 31 my statement that the bodies of `get_admin_pending_batch_requests` and `get_group_preview` were "not captured" (they are, in `RUN 1A`; the first checks `is_admin()`).
- **Kind 2, a fix narrower than the problem it serves.** Round 36 finding 3 (the safety trigger stamped only NULL values, not every transition into a negative state), finding 2 (INSERT closed, UPDATE and DELETE not, and one marker not two), and in Round 34 the counter that was protected on function paths but not on the direct-insert path. In each case the newest mechanism I added had a hole that the next audit found.
**Is QA nit-picking, or raising points that could have been raised earlier?** The three blocking findings are executable defects (a migration step that fails on a `NOT NULL` column; a way for a signed-in user to turn an ordinary group into a batch; a forgeable sequence value), not wording. Some of them were checkable earlier: the `NOT NULL` fact has been in the evidence since Round 10, and QA did not flag the "clear to NULL" step in Rounds 30, 32 or 34, so QA's audits are also incremental. Findings caught now, before any SQL exists, are the cheapest to fix, and the errors are mine as author. The three non-blocking items are hardening and editorial.
**What I did differently this time (done, not promised):** (1) every live fact in the migration block now cites its saved evidence file (RUN 6, RUN 1A, F1, F2, G4) and I list the two statements that still rest on repo copies; (2) for each mechanism I enumerated **every writer and every path** (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, direct client, definer function) and **both markers**, instead of testing the case QA named; (3) I searched brief A for every statement citing a repo document and checked each against the saved evidence; (4) I looked for other places where my own earlier statements conflict with evidence I hold, and found the errors in section C. I cannot promise zero further findings; what I can show is the checks, and section D removes the remaining "uncaptured" items QA keeps listing as residual risks.

### B. Response to QA's Round 36
1. **Step 5 cannot clear a `NOT NULL` column (accepted, verified, my error).** `RUN 6`: `study_groups.invite_token`, `uuid`, `nullable: NO`, default `gen_random_uuid()`. Brief A section 5 now **preserves NOT NULL**: after cutover the real token is **overwritten with a fresh random, unlinked `uuid`** (never NULL), the readers resolve a batch-shaped group's token only through the invite relation, and a verification checks that no batch-shaped group's column value equals any invite token. There is no clearing trigger any more, so the rollback is simpler and symmetrical: it restores a valid non-expiring token to every batch-shaped group, the column is never NULL at any point, and the exact live nullability contract holds before, during and after. Tests: the forward overwrite, creation of non-batch and batch groups after cutover, rollback to the exact live contract (zero NULLs, still `NOT NULL`), and the exact set of tokens that work before and after.
2. **Step 0 closed direct INSERT but not conversion by UPDATE (accepted, verified, my error).** `sg_update_creator` constrains `is_batch_group` only, and `group_type` is independent (`RUN 6`, F2); `sg_delete_creator` has the same gap. Brief A now defines **one term, "batch-shaped" = `is_batch_group = true` OR `group_type = 'batch'`** (the live join's own definition) and uses it in every policy, trigger, function and verification. Step 0 now: `sg_insert` WITH CHECK requires both markers false; `sg_update_creator` USING and WITH CHECK require `group_type <> 'batch'` as well (old and new row); `sg_delete_creator` requires it; `sgm_insert_admin` and `sgm_delete` apply only to groups that are not batch-shaped (so a client cannot write the memberships of a batch group, which also closes the forged-sequence insert path); a **SECURITY INVOKER guard trigger** refuses, for `anon` and `authenticated`, setting either marker, an inconsistent pair, and any change to `order_seq` or a batch-shaped row's token; TRUNCATE is revoked. The statement that `sg_update_creator` means a client "can neither edit a batch row nor turn a row into one" is withdrawn. **Whether any existing row has the two markers disagreeing is not measured**; diagnostic 3 run H1 measures it, and if any such row exists a reviewed data fix runs before the policies are tightened. Tests cover both INSERT shapes, both UPDATE conversions, delete of a drifted row, and non-batch flows still working.
3. **The safety trigger protected only a missing sequence (accepted, verified, my gap).** QA's scenario is correct: a row with an old value reused, an invite created between, then an old archive writer closes it, would keep the old value. The **stamping trigger** (step 1) is now defined as the only thing that ever sets `last_inactive_seq`: on every INSERT or UPDATE in which a row **enters a negative status** it takes the batch lock, increments `order_seq` and **overwrites** the value with the new counter value **whatever was supplied**; on an INSERT with a non-negative status it sets NULL; on any other UPDATE it keeps the old value unless `current_user` is the owner (the one-time initialization). So an old writer, a rewritten writer or a forged direct value can never leave a stale or invented value. Tests: a reused row with an existing old value, an invite between the old and the new negative event, a real-role insert and update supplying a forged non-NULL value (negative and non-negative status), and the old-archive-writer case.

### C. Non-blocking points, and errors I found myself
1. **Section 12 stale OPEN item (accepted):** removed; the status now reads that the G1 to G5 results were audited in Round 32 and Gate 4 accepted.
2. **`is_original` immutability (accepted):** a trigger on the invite relation makes `is_original`, the token and `created_seq` immutable after insertion; tests prove no path can demote, replace or add a second original.
3. **The custom-setting marker (accepted, replaced):** a custom setting is not authorization. The guard trigger is SECURITY INVOKER and tests `current_user` (the function owner inside SECURITY DEFINER writers, `anon` or `authenticated` for a client), which a client cannot forge through the API; the old marker is removed.
4. **Errors of mine found in the same pass (not raised by QA):** (a) Round 31 said the bodies of `get_admin_pending_batch_requests` and `get_group_preview` were not captured; both are in `RUN 1A`, and the first has an admin guard, so its `anon`-executable ACL is not an authorization gap (brief A section 12 corrected); what is really uncaptured is `create_batch_group`, `archive_batch_group`, `restore_batch_group` and `get_study_time_stats`. (b) Two transition rows relied on repo copies of `archive_batch_group` and `restore_batch_group`; they now say so and point to the live capture. (c) The repo document `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` is wrong about the nullability of `invite_token` (a documentation fix for the project, not done here).

## Round 38 · QA · 05/10/2026

### Blocking findings

1. **The “idempotent” step 3 initialization can overwrite a correct newer stamp with `original + 1`.** Step 1 now correctly stamps every transition into a negative state with the next live counter value, but step 3 says that every pre-existing negative row is set to `N+1` after the original invite's `N`, and the whole operation is rerun immediately before step 5. Consider original invite 1, two later invites 2 and 3, then a deployment-window archive stamped correctly at 4. A catch-up rerun can replace 4 with 2; invite 3, which is older than the archive, then compares as newer and can activate the student after restore. This violates A-I6 and recreates the stale-order failure that the trigger is meant to prevent. Initialization must update **only** negative rows whose sequence is NULL, allocate from the batch's **current** counter so the initialized value is greater than every existing invite/order value, and never change a non-NULL value already written by the stamping trigger. Test both a NULL legacy row when later invite values already exist and an idempotent rerun over a correctly stamped non-NULL row.

2. **The stamping trigger's permanent `current_user = owner` exception is not limited to one-time initialization.** The trigger is defined as `SECURITY DEFINER`. Inside a SECURITY DEFINER trigger function, `current_user` is the trigger-function owner for every invocation, including one caused by a client or another function. Therefore “keep the old value unless `current_user` is the owner (the one-time initialization)” always takes the owner exception on the non-transition UPDATE branch; it does not distinguish the step 3 migration from an ordinary later UPDATE. That contradicts the claims that the trigger is the only setter and that every other UPDATE preserves the old value. Use a separate, narrowly scoped initialization operation and then remove/disable its exception, or make the trigger itself fill only NULL legacy negatives under the batch lock without a permanent bypass. Prove that unrelated UPDATEs through both direct and SECURITY DEFINER paths cannot alter `last_inactive_seq` after initialization.

   The `current_user` check in the **SECURITY INVOKER** `study_groups` guard does distinguish a direct client statement from a definer statement, but it is still owner-wide rather than function-specific: every exposed SECURITY DEFINER function owned by that role reaches the trusted path. The SQL contract must either use a dedicated no-login owner for the approved writers or catalog and review every executable definer function that can write `study_groups`; diagnostic 3 H2 captures four named bodies, not that complete writer set. The present test statement that a client cannot reach the owner path “by any exposed call” is false because clients invoke the approved SECURITY DEFINER writers themselves; the required proof is that no exposed call can make an **unauthorized** owner-path mutation.

3. **Step 0 and section 6.8 specify incompatible final privileges for `study_group_members`.** Step 0 deliberately keeps direct INSERT/DELETE capability for non-batch groups and narrows `sgm_insert_admin`/`sgm_delete` to rows whose group is not batch-shaped, so existing non-batch invitation, leaving and deletion continue to work. Section 6.8 instead says the SQL gate must revoke client INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on the table and move invite cancellation to a function. Table privileges cannot be revoked only for batch rows: a table-level INSERT/DELETE revoke makes the retained non-batch policies unusable and breaks the flows step 0 promises to preserve. Choose one exact contract. Either retain the necessary table privileges with the batch-excluding policies and revoke only unneeded privileges such as TRUNCATE, or move every affected non-batch write to functions before revoking table DML. State the final grants and tests once, consistently.

### Non-blocking findings

1. With step 0 in place, a real client batch-membership INSERT or UPDATE carrying a forged `last_inactive_seq` should be rejected by policy before the stamping trigger becomes the defense. Test that denial as a real role, and test overwrite/nulling through a controlled old/definer-writer harness. If the stamping trigger is intended to apply to non-batch memberships too, say so and test the behavioral change; otherwise scope it explicitly to batch-shaped groups so the promised non-batch behavior remains unchanged.

2. The shadow-token overwrite should retry or fail closed if the generated value collides not only with an invite-table token but with another live `study_groups.invite_token`. The rewritten readers' batch-shaped/non-batch predicates make a batch shadow non-authoritative, but explicit cross-source uniqueness verification keeps preview/join and rollback behavior deterministic rather than relying on UUID collision probability.

3. Diagnostic 3 H4's `policy_count_by_table` object omits a table that has zero policies. The full `policies` array and the fixed three-table scope still make absence inferable, so this does not block execution; an explicit three-row/three-key zero-inclusive count would make the saved result easier to audit.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The pre-audit handoff was valid: state `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 37 and last QA-reviewed round 36. Round 37, brief A v12 and diagnostic 3 are frozen by commit `af3fac0`; no `src/` change exists from baseline `6fc6ceb`.
- The artifact hashes match the status block exactly: brief A v12 `f21b5710698ce709c5597848c9888b935998ed49b0ed58934e360ad3f68edfd3`; diagnostic 3 `a5cf2dc36147571d96fd2bcfdbc5b889ee89445869692df1c4b92b0653a37e26`; brief B `0fe77dec72dc0ea606f62fc37c79cd45cdada70c361fa38a523370b2df40683a`.
- Brief B has no diff between the approved Round 34 state and Round 37 and remains Founder-approved at `0fe77dec72dc`, with E1 to E10 confirmed in Round 37.
- **Overwrite/rollback behavior:** replacing each batch-shaped column token with a fresh unlinked non-NULL value preserves the live NOT NULL constraint. Because the cut-over readers use the invite relation exclusively for batch-shaped groups and use the column only for non-batch groups, the shadow value grants nothing; exact-mapping before overwrite keeps existing links alive. Rollback restores a non-NULL eligible original or a new non-expiring value before restoring legacy readers, so it returns to one working token per batch without reviving expired/revoked links. Subject to collision hardening in non-blocking finding 2, Round 36 finding 1 is resolved.
- **Both-marker behavior:** the unified predicate (`is_batch_group = true OR group_type = 'batch'`) now covers INSERT, the old and new sides of UPDATE, DELETE, batch-membership INSERT/DELETE, counter/token guarding, migration selection and reader cutover. H1 safely measures pre-existing drift before tightening. Subject to the privilege contradiction in finding 3 and complete definer-writer audit in finding 2, Round 36 finding 2 is resolved.
- **Stamping behavior:** on an actual non-negative-to-negative transition, overwriting any supplied value with the next counter value under the batch lock fixes the reused-row/old-archive case and defeats stale or forged values. Positive INSERTs are nulled. Finding 1 concerns the later initialization rerun and finding 2 the permanent exception, not the core transition branch.
- No contradiction was found among A-I1 to A-I11 outside the A-I6/A-I8 and privilege-contract conflicts identified above.

### Diagnostic 3 verdict

- The file contains exactly four top-level read-only statements, each a `SELECT`; it contains no write, DDL, transaction-control or application-function call. H1 returns bucketed counts only. H2-H4 return catalogue source/definition text, not stored user-authored rows, names, emails, phone numbers or identifiers. The requested function, trigger and policy facts are material to the remaining design and SQL review.
- **QA recommends Founder authorization to run diagnostic 3 at exact hash `a5cf2dc36147`** (full hash above), as four separate runs H1-H4 exactly as instructed. This recommendation is not Gate 3 authorization; only the Founder can grant it. Save each raw result or error unchanged under the named evidence convention and return it for QA audit before any interpretation is treated as verified.

### Residual risks

- H1 may require a separately reviewed marker-repair artifact before step 0. H2 may change the list of writers/readers that must be rewritten. H3/H4 may expose additional trigger or catalogue-policy work. None of those results is known until the exact diagnostic is authorized, run and audited.
- Counter type/overflow, trigger timing, dedicated ownership, complete SECURITY DEFINER ACLs, shadow-token collision handling, rollback reporting and real-role/concurrency evidence remain SQL-gate work.
- Brief A v12 is not ready for Founder Gate 1 while initialization can regress order, the stamping trigger has an always-owner exception, and the final membership privilege contract is contradictory. No existing exact-hash approval is withdrawn.
- No SQL, build, test, database mutation, browser or external service was run by QA. The unrelated pre-existing `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not touched.

### Disposition: REVISION REQUIRED

Revise brief A so catch-up initializes only NULL legacy negative rows from the current serialized counter and never overwrites a valid stamp; remove the permanent owner bypass from the SECURITY DEFINER stamping trigger and narrow the guard's trusted writer boundary; and reconcile step 0 with section 6.8 into one implementable membership privilege contract. The NOT NULL overwrite, both-marker predicates and transition-stamping branch otherwise pass behavioral review. Brief B remains unchanged and approved at `0fe77dec72dc`. Diagnostic 3 at `a5cf2dc36147` is recommended for Founder authorization, but no new gate is approved by this QA round.

## Round 39 · Claude · 05/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA's Round 38 (REVISION REQUIRED, three blocking, three non-blocking). QA's Round 38 is committed as `6157a63`. All three blocking findings and two of the three non-blocking are accepted after checking each against the text and the evidence; the third non-blocking one is declined with a reason (section C). **Brief B is unchanged** (`0fe77dec72dc`, Founder-approved). **Diagnostic 3 is unchanged** (`a5cf2dc36147`, the hash QA recommended). Brief A is revised to v13. No SQL was run by Claude; no application code changed. This round is committed with the revised brief; QA should audit that new commit.

### A. Blocking findings
1. **Initialization could overwrite a correct newer stamp (accepted, verified, my wording defect).** Correct: step 3(b) said every pre-existing negative row is set to the original invite's value plus one, and the step is rerun before step 5; in QA's example (original 1, invites 2 and 3, a deployment-window archive stamped 4) a rerun would lower 4 to 2 and make invite 3 look newer. Step 3(b) now says: **initialization touches only rows whose `last_inactive_seq` is NULL**, allocates each value **from that batch's current counter** (counter plus 1, plus 2, and so on, writing the final value back to `order_seq`), so it is greater than every existing invite value and every earlier stamp, and **never changes a non-NULL value**. It runs in one transaction holding the batch row locks in ascending id order. Tests: a NULL legacy row when later invites already exist; an idempotent rerun over a correctly stamped row (the example above must still read 4 and leave invite 3 older).
2. **The stamping trigger's `current_user = owner` exception is permanent (accepted, verified, my misunderstanding of PostgreSQL).** Correct: inside a SECURITY DEFINER function `current_user` is always the function owner, so "keep the old value unless `current_user` is the owner" took the exception on **every** non-transition UPDATE, not only during initialization. The exception is **withdrawn**: on every UPDATE that is not a transition into a negative status the trigger keeps the old value, with no exception. The one-time initialization of step 3 does not use the trigger; it runs as a separate migration statement in one transaction with the trigger **disabled and re-enabled inside that same transaction**, under the batch locks that prevent a concurrent negative write. Tests prove that unrelated UPDATEs through both a direct client path and a SECURITY DEFINER path cannot change `last_inactive_seq` after initialization.
   **The guard on `study_groups` trusted every function owned by the owner role (accepted, verified).** The guard now trusts only a **dedicated no-login role `grp_batch_writer`** that **owns only the approved batch writers** (`create_batch_group`, `rename_batch_group`, `archive_batch_group`, `restore_batch_group`, join, approve, reject, bulk resolve, enroll, remove, leave, the invite functions, the stamping trigger function). The SQL gate **catalogs every SECURITY DEFINER function that can write `study_groups`, `study_group_members` or the invite relation** (the complete writer set, which diagnostic 3 run H2 does not give) and confirms that exactly the approved writers are owned by that role. The false test statement ("no exposed call can reach the owner path") is replaced: clients invoke the approved writers and those succeed; the required proof is that **no exposed call can make an unauthorized owner-path mutation**, tested with a SECURITY DEFINER function not owned by `grp_batch_writer`.
3. **Step 0 and section 6.8 specified incompatible privileges (accepted, verified, my inconsistency).** Correct: 6.8 (written in v7) still said to revoke client INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on `study_group_members`, while step 0 (v11) keeps direct INSERT and DELETE for non-batch flows; table privileges cannot be revoked per row. **One final privilege contract is now stated once, in step 0(d), and 6.8 points to it:** `anon` loses every privilege on `study_groups` and `study_group_members`; `authenticated` **keeps** SELECT, INSERT and DELETE on `study_group_members` and SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE on `study_groups` (the non-batch flows need them under the narrowed policies) and **loses** TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER on both and UPDATE on `study_group_members` (no UPDATE policy exists and the app never uses it). I checked the app code to avoid breaking a flow: it creates groups, invites, accepts and declines through RPCs (`create_study_group`, `invite_to_group`, `accept_group_invite`, `decline_group_invite`), and its only direct writes to these tables are the two deletes at `GroupDetail.jsx:290` and `MyGroups.jsx:177`. Whether those four RPCs are SECURITY DEFINER is **not in the saved evidence**, so I did **not** revoke client INSERT and UPDATE entirely; that stricter hardening is recorded as possible later, after their bodies are captured. A catalog test asserts the final grants and every non-batch flow is exercised after the revokes.

### B. Non-blocking points, and one more hole found by my own pass
1. **Forged value at the policy layer, and scope (accepted).** A real client batch-membership INSERT or UPDATE carrying a forged `last_inactive_seq` is **denied by policy** before the trigger runs, and tested as such; the overwrite and nulling behaviour is tested through a controlled old-writer and definer-writer harness. The stamping trigger is **explicitly scoped to batch-shaped groups**; memberships of non-batch groups keep their present behaviour, and a test shows it.
2. **Shadow-token collision (accepted).** The overwrite regenerates the value if it equals any invite token **or any other `study_groups.invite_token`** and fails closed if that does not resolve; the verification also requires every `invite_token` in the table to be unique.
3. **Another hole I found while checking my own fix (not raised by QA):** the guard trusts only `grp_batch_writer`, but the migration statements of steps 3, 5 and 6 (the counter write-back, the token overwrite, the rollback restore) would be run by the ordinary migration login and refused by that guard. Brief A now says every migration statement of those steps runs with `SET LOCAL ROLE grp_batch_writer`, which the migration login is granted membership of, so the guard recognizes it and does not have to be disabled.

### C. Declined: diagnostic 3 H4's zero-inclusive count (QA non-blocking 3)
QA noted that `policy_count_by_table` omits a table with zero policies, and also said the full `policies` array and the fixed three-table scope make absence inferable and that it does not block execution. I **did not change the file**: a change would alter the hash `a5cf2dc36147` that QA recommended for authorization and would require a new QA pass and a new Founder decision for a presentation detail. If a zero count is needed, the saved array shows it.

### D. Why these three were mine, stated plainly
Finding 1 is a wording defect in text I wrote ("set every negative row to N+1" did not say "only NULL rows"). Finding 2 is a PostgreSQL semantics point I got wrong (`current_user` inside a SECURITY DEFINER function). Finding 3 is a contradiction between two sections I wrote at different times: when I added step 0 in v11 I did not go back and reconcile the older 6.8 text. This round I searched for every statement about the same two tables (6.7, 6.8, step 0, the tests, section 12) before editing, which is how the contradiction was removed rather than patched.

### E. Self-audit before handing over
- **Searches (brief A):** no "unless `current_user` is the owner" except inside the sentence that withdraws it; no "N+1"; no remaining instruction to revoke client INSERT or UPDATE or DELETE on `study_group_members`; the grants appear once in step 0(d) and 6.8 refers to it; the test list contains the ownership boundary, initialization, stamping and privilege-contract bullets; the version line describes v13.
- **Checked against the app code:** the direct-write inventory of `src` (two deletes) and the RPC list used to decide not to revoke INSERT and UPDATE.
- **Limits stated, not hidden:** the definer status of the four non-batch RPCs, the live bodies of `create_batch_group`, `archive_batch_group` and `restore_batch_group`, and any drifted `is_batch_group` and `group_type` rows are still unmeasured (diagnostic 3 runs H1 and H2 cover the markers and three bodies; the four RPCs are not in diagnostic 3). `grp_batch_writer`, the trigger disable and re-enable, and the grants are marked for real-role and concurrency tests, not asserted. I cannot run SQL.

## Round 40 · QA · 05/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 39 and last QA-reviewed round 38.
- Brief A v13 hashes to full SHA-256 `84d7157b06de9dfc9fc91596e58b3c97a15d41571364f1b10b334071c21bb74e`, matching the status block (`84d7157b06de`).
- Brief B hashes to full SHA-256 `0fe77dec72dc0ea606f62fc37c79cd45cdada70c361fa38a523370b2df40683a`, matching the status block and the Founder-approved hash. It has no content diff from the artifact reviewed in Round 37.
- Follow-up diagnostic 3 hashes to full SHA-256 `a5cf2dc36147571d96fd2bcfdbc5b889ee89445869692df1c4b92b0653a37e26`, matching the status block. It has no content diff from the artifact reviewed in Round 37, so the Round 38 recommendation that the Founder may authorize it stands; it remains unexecuted and its hypotheses remain unresolved.
- No application-code or schema change was present in the reviewed range. The unrelated local `.claude/settings.local.json` modification was not reviewed or changed.

### Blocking findings

None for Founder Gate 1 consideration.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **The initialization design is behaviourally sound, but its SQL lock order is a mandatory implementation condition.** For the stale-stamp example, original participation sequence 1, invitations 2 and 3, and archive transition 4 leave `last_inactive_order_seq = 4`; initialization excludes that non-NULL row, and a rerun changes neither the row nor the counter. A NULL negative row is instead allocated strictly after the current counter, once, and a rerun is idempotent. Because disabling the stamping trigger requires an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `study_group_members`, the migration must obtain that table/trigger lock **before** taking any batch-row lock, then lock batches in ascending id order, initialize only NULL negative rows, re-enable the trigger and commit. Holding a batch-row lock while waiting for the table lock could deadlock with a writer holding a membership-table lock while waiting for that batch row. The SQL review must reject the latter order and must prove that error/rollback leaves the trigger enabled.
2. **The writer-role boundary depends on an exact role-membership contract.** The proposed no-login `grp_batch_writer` ownership makes the invoker guard work as stated: an approved `SECURITY DEFINER` writer executes with that role as `current_user`, while a direct authenticated write does not. The SQL gate must additionally prove from `pg_roles` and `pg_auth_members` that the role is `NOLOGIN`, is not superuser/BYPASSRLS/CREATEROLE/CREATEDB/replication, and is not inherited or settable by `authenticator`, `anon`, `authenticated`, service/application roles or other untrusted roles. Only the trusted migration identity may be able to `SET LOCAL ROLE` for steps 3, 5 and 6. Its object and table privileges must be limited to the enumerated writer implementation.
3. **Use the same cross-column collision-safe token allocation wherever a replacement token is minted.** Step 5 now correctly rejects a shadow UUID that collides with either any invitation token or any group token. The SQL design should apply that same check to rollback/new-batch replacement-token generation rather than relying only on the invitation-column uniqueness constraint; the SQL tests should force both collision classes.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- **No-exception stamping now has the stated behaviour.** Every batch-shaped INSERT or transition from non-negative to negative allocates and overwrites the next sequence; a non-negative INSERT stores NULL; every other UPDATE preserves `OLD.last_inactive_order_seq`. There is no owner/session/setting bypass in the permanent trigger. The one-time initialization is separated from normal trigger behaviour and constrained to the deployment transaction.
- **Stale and forged values are closed on the normal write paths.** Approved transition writers cannot preserve an earlier stamp or supply a future one, and direct callers cannot change batch markers, the stamp, the counter or the lock token because the policies and invoker guard deny them.
- **The single privilege contract is internally consistent.** Step 0(d) is now the sole contract and section 6.8 points to it. It preserves the named non-batch grants while revoking all anonymous access, member-table UPDATE, and TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER from application roles. Batch-shaped direct writes remain excluded by policy and guard.
- **The writer inventory is testable rather than assumed.** The SQL gate requires a catalog of every `SECURITY DEFINER` function capable of writing the three relevant tables and an exact owner set, which is the correct control for discovering unlisted writers.
- Brief B remains the approved presentation design, and diagnostic 3 remains a read-only evidence plan with explicit output and interpretation requirements.

### Residual risks

- Diagnostic 3 is still required before SQL authorization: a positive H1 result requires data correction or an explicit legacy-state decision, and a positive H2 result changes the policy-hardening and regression-test plan.
- The four non-batch RPC bodies remain unmeasured. The retained grants avoid pre-emptively breaking them, but their bodies, owners and effective privileges must be captured at the SQL gate and tested before any later privilege reduction.
- Catalog completeness, trigger ordering, concurrency/deadlock behaviour, exact rollback restoration and the deployed privilege graph can only be finally accepted against the authored SQL and controlled database evidence.

### Disposition

`PASS WITH CONDITIONS`

Brief A v13 at `84d7157b06de` is ready for the Founder’s Gate 1 decisions, provided the table-lock-before-batch-lock ordering, exact role-membership boundary and collision-safe replacement-token requirements above are carried into the SQL design and its tests. This is QA advice, not authorization to execute, deploy, commit or push. Brief B remains unchanged at the approved hash, and the prior recommendation to authorize diagnostic 3 at `a5cf2dc36147` stands.

Handoff: `AWAITING-FOUNDER` — Founder to decide Gate 1 / D1-D11 and whether to authorize diagnostic 3. If Gate 1 is accepted, Claude may draft the migration and test SQL for a separate Gate 2 QA review; no SQL is authorized by this round.

- **Decisions D1 to D11 are confirmed as recommended in brief A section 11**, in these words (D2 is recorded as the option the brief states, since it was marked "needs a Founder choice"): D1 labelled invites with expiry and revoke, recipient binding optional; D2 no push notification on rejection, but rejection is a durable state with history, the student sees the "isn't active" message on reopening the link and the admin sees the earlier attempt; D3 the pending list shows the student's course and via-label always and the email only on an explicit admin "details" action; D4 a Tier B student may be approved into a batch with a "needs content access" banner, through one server-enforced eligibility rule on every request and activation path; D5 the "Full access coming soon" wording is replaced and the form prefilled; D6 "creator-granted / externally fulfilled" wording only; D7 a rejected student may re-request immediately, with history visible to the admin and no cooldown in v1; D8 students see only event kind, time and batch name; D9 anonymous requests are never deduplicated, shown grouped with notification throttling; D10 claims never block one another, with administrator confirm, dismiss, link and supersede audited, and a person's own authenticated submission is not superseded in v1; D11 invite create, list and revoke are for platform admin and super_admin only in v1. Brief B decisions E1 to E10 were confirmed in Round 37.

- **The three conditions are binding** and are written into the SQL work plan as acceptance criteria C1, C2 and C3 (with C4, QA's other Round 40 requirements), each mapped to a file and a proof. Brief A itself is not edited, so its approved hash `84d7157b06de` stays valid.

### B. What I did with "proceed accordingly", and why SQL is not written yet
I wrote **no migration SQL yet, on purpose.** The recent rounds showed that my errors came from stating database facts that I had not measured against saved evidence; five facts that the SQL depends on are still unmeasured, and four of them are exactly what the authorized diagnostic 3 will measure. Writing files before those results would repeat that mistake. What I did instead:

It states what is approved and what is not; the binding conditions C1 to C4 with the proof each needs (for example C1: the table and trigger lock is taken before any batch lock, the reverse order is rejected in review, and a test proves that an error mid-initialization leaves the stamping trigger enabled; C2: a catalog test over `pg_roles` and `pg_auth_members`, `SET ROLE` denials for each untrusted role, and the exact owner set of the writer functions; C3: one shared collision-safe token function used by every replacement and tests that force both collision classes); the facts that must be measured before each file is authored; the file inventory and order for stream A (A-01 to A-11: roles, step 0 policies and guard, invite relation and triggers, status and events, functions, rewritten writers, backfill and initialization, cutover, token overwrite, access requests, EXECUTE revokes) and stream B (B-01 to B-06), each with a rollback and a real-role test file; the standards from the project rules; the frontend dependencies (none authorized); and the gate process.

No user text, name, email or identifier is selected.
3. **Order of drafting once the evidence is in:** stream B file B-01 and B-04 and access requests A-10 depend on no new measurement and can be drafted as soon as QA has audited the plan; the group migration files wait for H1 and H2 (diagnostic 3) and J1 to J3 (diagnostic 4); B-02 waits for H4 and B-03 for H3. Which stream to review first is the Founder's choice (the plan recommends access requests and stream B first, because their blast radius is smaller).

## Round 42 · QA · 05/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 41 and last QA-reviewed round 40.
- Follow-up diagnostic 4 hashes to full SHA-256 `64392d6daff98bbd2f3a1894720e429c6ec5c221a520793d0c695fd97b5a5b13`, matching the status block (`64392d6daff9`).
- The SQL work plan hashes to full SHA-256 `f8d435c13955c20aa96f6295ccef7769ad6b36c9426766e0c125453654c43936` and is frozen by Round 41 commit `2e25bfd`.
- Commit `2e25bfd` adds only the plan and read-only diagnostic 4 under `docs/database/` and changes this discussion record. It contains no migration SQL or application-code change. The unrelated `.claude/settings.local.json` working-tree modification was not reviewed or changed.

### Blocking findings

1. **Diagnostic 4 does not establish the role boundary it is intended to measure.** J1 returns only direct membership edges where one endpoint is one of seven named roles. It does not return `session_user` or `current_user`, and it does not compute the recursive membership/`SET ROLE` closure. An untrusted role can therefore reach `grp_batch_writer` through an unnamed intermediate role without J1 displaying the complete path. The revised diagnostic must identify the SQL Editor execution identity and expose every direct and transitive path by which any role can inherit or set the future writer role, including `inherit_option` and `set_option` on every edge.
2. **Diagnostic 4's J2 cannot support the claimed complete writer inventory.** Its acknowledged source-text regex sees only direct textual `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` forms. It can miss dynamic SQL, indirect function calls, quoted/`ONLY` forms, `MERGE`, writable views and trigger-mediated writes; it also returns no function definitions for the matched rows and cannot inspect the not-yet-created invite relation. Consequently, a writer omitted by the regex remains invisible, which is exactly the C2 violation the evidence is supposed to detect. J2 may be useful as a lead generator, but not as the measure-first completeness evidence. A revised diagnostic/evidence procedure must return enough definitions and call-chain information for an exhaustive reviewed manifest of all relevant `SECURITY DEFINER` writers, with any unavoidable blind spot explicitly closed by the Gate 2 catalog and real-role tests.
3. **C2's proposed proof inherits those blind spots and would not necessarily fail on an omitted writer or a transitive role path.** Checking the owner of a known function set detects a wrong owner only after the set is complete. A-01 and its test must define a recursive membership assertion, the exact trusted migration identity, an exhaustive approved-writer manifest (including overload signatures and the eventual invite relation), and a failure for both an extra role-owned function and an unlisted function capable of performing a protected write.
4. **C3 is not fully mapped to a concrete artifact or a deterministic proof.** The table points to A-06, A-07, A-09 and “the rollback”, but no numbered rollback file owns the replacement-token restoration contract; A-07 normally copies the existing original token rather than allocating a replacement. The plan also does not state how tests force the allocator's next candidate, so “force both collision classes” could become a probabilistic test that never exercises retry or fail-closed behavior. Name the rollback artifact, list every allocation call site, and require a controlled allocator/test seam that proves retry and exhaustion/fail-closed behavior for both cross-column collision classes.
5. **Brief A has approved behaviors with no complete file and proof assignment.** In particular:
   - Section 6.7 and A-I6 require every relevant policy and reader (`sg_select_member`, `cgs_select_member`, `cgs_delete_own_or_admin`, the `get_browsable_*` group-share branches, `get_my_batch_groups` and other discovered readers) to treat only `active` membership as conferring access. No A-file owns that reader/policy audit and its stale-row tests.
   - Section 6.4's event-reader functions and field scopes are absent from A-04, and A-I11's exact RLS/table-ACL/function-EXECUTE proof is not assigned across all claims, invites and events functions. A-11 is limited to functions found executable in G2, which does not cover every newly added or rewritten function.
   - A-10's inventory does not enumerate the approved submission/link/claim/admin-matching contracts: the rewritten `submit_access_request` and student branch of `link_access_request`, administrator confirm/dismiss/link/supersede and their audit rows, both relationship-sensitive readers, immutable `submitter` provenance, `profiles.access_request_ref`, direct-table denial, anonymous grouping/notification throttling and claim/request rate limits. A single A-10 file is acceptable only if its content and TEST inventory explicitly cover those behaviors.
   - Brief A step 6 has no named end-to-end rollback/report artifact owning the restore order, exact surviving-token set, old function bodies, verification and object retirement. Per-file rollbacks do not by themselves prove that system-level contract.
6. **The group cutover order is internally incomplete.** A-09 says the admin-link frontend must be deployed before token overwrite, while sections 1, 6 and 7 describe SQL-before-frontend and a straight SQL-file sequence. The plan needs explicit separately gated phases: deploy and verify the compatibility SQL through A-08; obtain the applicable frontend Gate 5 and deploy/verify the new admin invite reader; only then separately approve, authorize and execute A-09. Its rollback must account for the frontend version active in each phase.
7. **Brief B is not fully covered by B-01 to B-06.** B-05 lists only guard triggers, but B-I5/6.3 also require the `NOT VALID` composite foreign keys on `flashcards` and `notes`. B-02 does not assign the admin-only replacement write path and exact privilege/TRUNCATE closure for all of `disciplines`, `subjects` and `topics`, which is required to keep `BulkUploadTopics.jsx` working. Cross-file dependencies are also missing: B-03 and A-10 depend on B-01's canonical resolver; B-05 depends on the H3 trigger inventory and the subject-key work in B-04; the H2 reading of `get_study_time_stats` belongs in B-I10/B-06 preservation proof as well as the group stream. The B TEST inventory must explicitly own professor denial, unchanged existing totals/reports, every catalogue projection, inactive-course identity, legacy-update behavior, conflict grouping and all-course reconciliation.
8. **The measure-first list is therefore incomplete.** It treats J1/J2 as sufficient despite their gaps, omits the full active-membership reader inventory needed by A-I6/6.7, omits H3 as an input to B-05, and maps `get_study_time_stats` only to A-files despite B-I10. Each unresolved measurement must point to saved evidence and every dependent file; no dependent file may be authored from the current partial inventory.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **C1 has the right file and a potentially effective proof**, provided the A-07 concurrency test deliberately stages the former deadlock order: a writer first holds its membership-table lock while the initializer attempts the table/trigger lock without holding any batch row. It must then prove completion, trigger re-enablement after both success and forced rollback, and no unstamped negative row.
2. **C4 identifies the right implementation areas**, but A-03 TEST and A-07 TEST should name the stale-stamp example, rerun, forged negative and non-negative inputs, unrelated direct and definer updates, non-batch no-op behavior, and the controlled old-writer harness explicitly rather than relying only on a broad reference to brief A section 5.
3. Diagnostic 4 J3 is a useful, bounded capture of the five named function bodies. That useful portion does not cure J1/J2 or make the exact file authorization-ready for its stated purpose.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The plan correctly states that it is a working paper, authorizes nothing, and requires per-hash Gate 2 review before execution can be requested.
- The intended A- and B-stream decomposition is directionally sound, and it correctly preserves real-role tests, rollback files, evidence citations, pinned search paths and lock-order review as file standards.
- C1 preserves the table-lock-before-batch-lock correction; C4 preserves NULL-only initialization, the stale-stamp rerun and the no-exception stamping rule.
- The plan correctly defers group SQL that depends on H1/H2/J1-J3 and catalogue SQL that depends on H3/H4 rather than authoring it against unmeasured assumptions.
- Round 41 grants no new gate beyond **Gate 1 for brief A v13 `84d7157b06de`** and **Gate 3 for diagnostic 3 `a5cf2dc36147`**. Brief B's Gate 1 and earlier diagnostic Gates 3/4 remain prior Founder decisions. Gate 2, Gate 5, Gate 6 and Gate 7 remain not approved; diagnostic 4 has no Gate 3 authorization.

### Residual risks

- The SQL file split may change after diagnostics 3 and 4 reveal overloads, writers, policies, role paths or marker drift. Any changed inventory must remain traceable to the two approved briefs rather than silently narrowing them.
- Static catalog/source inspection cannot alone prove absence of dynamic or indirect writes. The eventual proof needs a reviewed function manifest, privilege boundary and adversarial real-role behavior together.
- Frontend behavior remains outside the present authorization. SQL tests can establish server contracts, but the UI projections, messages and staged invite cutover still require an exact frontend diff and Gate 5 review.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

Follow-up diagnostic 4 at `64392d6daff9` **cannot yet be recommended for Founder authorization**. Revise J1/J2 and return the new exact hash. Revise the SQL work plan so C1-C4 each have named artifacts and deterministic violation-detecting proofs, every approved brief behavior is owned by a migration/rollback/TEST or explicitly deferred by the approved design, the measure-first dependencies are complete, and the A-09 frontend boundary is a separately gated phase.

Handoff: `AWAITING-CLAUDE` — Claude to revise diagnostic 4 and `00_PLAN_sql-work-plan.md`, hash both, update the findings register and return them for QA. No database run, migration execution, deployment, commit/push authorization or additional gate is provided by this round.

## Round 43 · Claude · 05/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA Round 42 (`REVISION REQUIRED`). Claude verified each finding against the files before changing anything; all eight blocking findings are accepted, none is disputed. Two artifacts are revised and returned: follow-up diagnostic 4 **v2** and the SQL work plan **v2**. No SQL was run by Claude (Claude has no database access, so diagnostic 4 v2 has been read and re-read but **not executed**, and its syntax is untested until QA's audit and the Founder's run); no application code changed; no migration file was written. The Round 42 text was committed unchanged as `1c93a12`. This round is committed with the revised files; QA should audit that new commit.

### A. Verification of QA's findings (what I checked, and what I found)
- **1 (role graph):** confirmed. v1 J1 filtered to seven named roles and showed only direct edges; it returned neither the execution identity nor the closure. v2 J1 returns `session_user` and `current_user`, every role with its attributes, every edge with grantor and the admin, inherit and set options, the server's own `pg_has_role` answer (`MEMBER`, `USAGE`, `SET`) for every pair of non-superuser roles, and `pg_default_acl`.
- **2 and 8 (writer inventory):** confirmed. v1 J2 was a regex over three verbs and returned no definitions. v2 replaces it: J2a lists **every** public routine (all overloads) with owner, language, definer flag, search_path, ACL and body flags; J2b computes a call-closure (a routine is in it if its source mentions `study_group` in any form, `invite_token`, a view over the group tables or `EXECUTE`, or if it calls such a routine directly or transitively) with the call edges; J2c-1 and J2c-2 return the full definition of every closure member (two runs so no cell is oversized); J2d returns routines outside `public`, views, materialized views, rewrite rules, triggers and every foreign key that touches the two tables (cascades are writes); J4 returns every policy that mentions the tables or calls a closure routine; J5 returns scheduled jobs by name, schedule and flags only. **I deliberately do not select the scheduled command text**, because a scheduled command can contain a service key. J3 stays as a guaranteed capture.
- **3 (C2 proof):** confirmed. Plan v2 section 2 C2 now requires: the trusted migration identity (the value measured by FU4-J1) asserted at the top of A-01; the single edge `grp_batch_writer` to the trusted identity `WITH SET TRUE, INHERIT FALSE, ADMIN FALSE`; a recursive assertion through `pg_has_role` for every other role; the superuser and CREATEROLE set compared with the J1 evidence; an exact writer manifest with identity arguments (overloads) and a second reviewed non-writer manifest, with a test that **fails when a routine of a freshly computed closure is in neither list** (that is the detector for an omitted writer); the exact privileges of the role.
- **4 (C3):** confirmed. Plan v2 names one allocator created in A-03, lists the four call sites (A-06 `create_batch_group`, A-05 `create_batch_invite`, A-09, A-13), states that A-07 copies and does not allocate, names the rollback artifact (A-13), and defines the controlled seam: an optional candidate list whose production value is always NULL, so the tests can force each collision class (an invite token, another batch's column token, a non-batch group's column token) and the exhaustion path deterministically. **New fact I found while mapping C3:** FU2-index records that `study_groups.invite_token` has **no unique index**. Uniqueness therefore rests on the allocator plus the verification queries; a unique index is recorded in the plan as a PROPOSAL for the Gate 2 review of A-03, **not** as part of the approved brief.
- **5 (files without owners):** confirmed for all four sub-points. Added A-11 (reader and policy audit of brief A 6.7 and A-I6), A-12 (EXECUTE and ACL closure for every added or rewritten function and the claims, invite and events relations, not only the G2 list), A-13 (the end-to-end rollback and report artifact), the events read functions and field scopes of 6.4 into A-04, and split A-10 into A-10a to A-10d with the submission, link, claim, administrator, reader, immutable-provenance, `access_request_ref`, direct-table-denial, grouping, throttling and rate-limit behaviours each named. Plan section 5 maps every approved behaviour to a file and a proof.
- **6 (cutover order):** confirmed. Plan section 6 makes three separately gated phases: A1 compatibility SQL (A-01 to A-08 and its A-11 and A-12 parts), A2 frontend (Gate 5, 6, 7), A3 token overwrite (A-09 alone, its own hash and gates). Each row states the active frontend version and the rollback order (for A3: **frontend first**, then A-13). One point for QA to confirm or correct: in A1 `get_admin_batch_groups` returns the invite token under the field the old screen reads, so the old frontend keeps working through A1; the frontend phase is still gated before A3 as QA required.
- **7 (brief B):** confirmed. Added B-02a (guards) and B-02b (admin write path and privilege and TRUNCATE closure for `disciplines`, `subjects`, `topics`), put the `NOT VALID` composite foreign keys on `flashcards` and `notes` into B-05, added the dependencies B-01 to B-03 and A-10a, B-04 and H3 to B-05, the H2 reading of `get_study_time_stats` into the B-I10 preservation proof, and a stream B TEST inventory (professor denial, unchanged totals and reports, every projection, inactive-course identity, legacy updates, conflict grouping, all-course reconciliation, rename, duplicate and DELETE attempts, TRUNCATE).
- **Non-blocking 1 to 3:** accepted. C1's test now stages the former deadlock order; C4's tests name the stale-stamp example, rerun, forged values, unrelated direct and definer updates, the non-batch no-op and the old-writer harness; J3 is kept.

## Round 44 · QA · 05/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review and exactly matched the requested state: `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 43 and last QA-reviewed round 42.
- Follow-up diagnostic 4 v2 hashes to full SHA-256 `b9f389b7fd1ea446a008cc281ad74ff37d8d8650d8b8bed6b754ef150d41a04f`, matching the status block (`b9f389b7fd1e`).
- SQL work plan v2 hashes to full SHA-256 `c66d39a4ab03933a3968248614932da57ea24c4da75e531045fd8d383ca277a3`, matching the status block (`c66d39a4ab03`). Both files remain identical to Round 43 commit `89fa0b8`.
- Diagnostic 3 on disk still hashes to full SHA-256 `a5cf2dc36147571d96fd2bcfdbc5b889ee89445869692df1c4b92b0653a37e26`. Its four raw evidence files and index remain identical to evidence commit `984ef7e`; all four raw files parse as strict JSON.
- The unrelated `.claude/settings.local.json` working-tree modification was not reviewed or changed.

### Diagnostic 3 evidence and Gate 4

- **H1:** bucket counts sum to the reported 16 groups. Zero bucket has disagreeing batch markers, so the conclusion that no current marker-repair file is needed is supported.
- **H2:** exactly the four requested functions were returned; all are `SECURITY DEFINER` with pinned `search_path` in their definitions. `create_batch_group` sets both markers and relies on the token default; archive and restore lock the group and test only `is_batch_group`; archive closes requested/invited rows and restore leaves them closed; `get_study_time_stats` contains the reported self-or-admin guard.
- **H3:** 32 non-internal public-table triggers were returned, all enabled (`O`). No trigger is attached to `study_groups`, `study_group_members`, `access_requests`, `disciplines`, `subjects`, `study_sessions` or `topics`; the profile, flashcard/note and audit-log readings in the index match the definitions.
- **H4:** the four returned policies and per-table counts agree: two on `disciplines`, one on `subjects`, one on `topics`. With the previously audited RLS baseline, the stated current client-write consequences follow.
- Minor interpretation correction: an anonymous call to `get_study_time_stats(NULL, ...)` is not rejected by the displayed guard because both ids are NULL; it returns zero rows' aggregates rather than another user's data. A non-NULL target id is refused. This does not change the security conclusion or the evidence validity, but the index wording “an anonymous caller is refused” should be corrected in the next Claude round.

**Gate 4 recommendation:** QA recommends that the Founder accept Gate 4 for diagnostic 3 at exact hash `a5cf2dc36147`. This recommendation verifies the authorized read-only diagnostic execution and saved evidence only; it authorizes no migration or deployment.

### Round 42 finding-by-finding check

| Round 42 finding | QA result on Round 43 |
|---|---|
| 1 role graph | **Substantially closed by J1.** It returns execution identity, every role, every direct edge and the server-computed `MEMBER`/`USAGE`/`SET` reachability for every non-superuser source role. The remaining plan contradiction is finding 3 below. |
| 2 writer inventory | **Not closed.** J2a-J2c and J4 compute their inventory and call closure only within schema `public`; J2d captures only non-`public` routines whose own source directly mentions `study_group` or `invite_token`. It does not compute the transitive caller/callee closure, dynamic-SQL seed or full reviewed manifest across all non-system schemas. |
| 3 C2 proof | **Not closed.** Its fresh closure inherits finding 2, and its membership-edge wording is internally inconsistent (below). |
| 4 C3 mapping/proof | **Artifact mapping and deterministic candidate tests are improved, but the behavioral guarantee is not closed.** The allocator's serialization is bypassed by permitted non-batch group creation using the column default (below). |
| 5 brief A coverage | **Closed at plan level.** A-04, A-10a-d, A-11, A-12 and A-13 now own the previously missing event readers, access-request contracts, active-only reader audit, ACL closure and system rollback. |
| 6 cutover order | **Closed at plan level.** A1/A2/A3 are separately gated, the old frontend remains compatible through A1, and A3 requires the new frontend before overwrite with the frontend-first rollback order. |
| 7 brief B coverage | **Partially closed.** The missing catalogue write path, trigger dependencies and card/note foreign keys are now assigned, but the proof inventory still does not cover every approved section (below). |
| 8 measure-first list | **Partially closed.** H3/H2 dependencies and the active-reader audit are corrected, but the incomplete cross-schema writer closure and a positive scheduled-job flag still lack a fail-closed evidence route. |

### Blocking findings

1. **J2-J5 still do not close the complete writer-inventory gap.** All call-graph CTEs in J2b, J2c and J4 restrict `f` to `public`. J2d supplies full definitions only for non-system-schema routines that directly contain `study_group` or `invite_token`; it omits non-`public` dynamic-SQL routines and non-`public` routines that call a public or non-public writer transitively. It also returns a trigger definition but not necessarily the body/call closure of a non-`public` trigger function that writes through helpers. J4 inherits the public-only closure. J5 flags direct group text and names public routines called, but the plan does not stop and require a secret-safe reviewed command/body if either flag is positive. Revise the closure to use schema-qualified routine identities across every non-system schema, return definitions for every closure member, and make any positive job/direct-object lead a blocking body-capture and manifest step. The invite relation must be included by the Gate 2 fresh closure once it exists.
2. **C2's exact role-edge contract contradicts itself and describes nonexistent evidence.** Section 2 first requires one explicit edge granting `grp_batch_writer` to the trusted identity with `SET TRUE`, `INHERIT FALSE`, `ADMIN FALSE`, then says no admin edge may exist “except the creator's implicit one recorded in FU4-J1.” PostgreSQL role creation does not create such an implicit membership edge, FU4-J1 runs before `grp_batch_writer` exists, and an explicit edge with `ADMIN FALSE` cannot also be that exception. Define one exact post-A-01 edge set (normally the single explicit edge already described), compare `pg_auth_members` to it exactly, and treat superuser reachability separately as a role attribute rather than an edge.
3. **C3's allocator is not collision-safe under the permitted non-batch write path.** The allocator's advisory lock serializes only callers of `t001_alloc_token`, while the plan explicitly leaves non-batch group creation on the `gen_random_uuid()` column default. A concurrent non-batch insert can therefore mint a token after the allocator's cross-column check but before its caller commits; neither path shares the lock. A unique index on `study_groups.invite_token` would prevent group-to-group duplicates but cannot prevent a group token from colliding with a token in the separate invite relation. The source test that insists on only four allocator call sites codifies this gap. Every token producer that can participate in either collision domain must use the same serialization/registry mechanism, or the schema must supply an equivalent atomic cross-relation uniqueness guarantee. Tests must stage the concurrent non-batch-insert versus invite/replacement allocation race, not only inject sequential candidates.
4. **Brief B still lacks complete proof ownership.** The traceability table maps only “Brief B 5, 6, 7” and omits the logging foundation in section 4. The stream-B TEST inventory does not explicitly require every valid and invalid `study_sessions` classification/source combination, no backfill of legacy rows, generated-key behavior and display-label tie-breaking, every manual write path, the 120-character/control-character/no-truncation rule across all four text surfaces, or the two-log over-limit alias flow. It also names the subject pair as unique `(`id`, `discipline_id`)`; brief B's composite identity and foreign-key wording use `(`discipline_id`, `subject_id`)` referencing `subjects(discipline_id, id)`. State the column order explicitly and add section 4, B-I2 to B-I4, and all section 8 SQL-test cases to file/test traceability; frontend-only cases may be assigned to the later exact frontend diff rather than silently omitted.

### Non-blocking findings

1. C1 now names A-07/A-07-TEST and the correct table-lock-before-batch-lock behavior. The eventual concurrency script must make its session choreography explicit; the current prose mentions two sessions while also describing a writer waiting on a batch lock, which may require a third lock-holder or a precise barrier sequence.
2. C4 now names the stale-stamp, rerun, forged-value, unrelated-update, non-batch and old-writer cases and maps them to A-03-TEST/A-07-TEST. This closes Round 42's C4 specificity issue at plan level.
3. Diagnostic 4 remains read-only in form: each run is a catalog `SELECT`/CTE query, with J5 reading `cron.job` but not returning command text. The authorization problem is evidence completeness, not database mutation.
4. The plan correctly defers any access-request routine not already captured in RUN 1A to a later small body capture after J2a. Apply the same explicit fail-closed rule to every newly discovered group writer/reader and scheduled job.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- J1 is a material improvement over v1: it removes the seven-role filter, records execution identity/default ACLs and delegates transitive role reachability to PostgreSQL's own `pg_has_role` result.
- J2a-J2d, J3, J4 and J5 provide a much stronger evidence set than the v1 regex, including overload identities, ACLs, definitions, policies, views/rules/triggers/foreign keys and scheduled-job flags.
- The new A-file inventory and traceability close the Round 42 omissions for active-only readers, event readers, relationship-sensitive claims, ACLs and end-to-end rollback.
- The three-phase group cutover now respects the frontend dependency without implying that Gate 5, 6 or 7 has already been granted.
- Diagnostic 3's raw files, index, execution hash and parsed result shapes are coherent and unchanged from the evidence commit.

### Residual risks

- Even an expanded static closure remains an over-approximation and can miss deliberately string-fragmented dynamic SQL. Final assurance still requires the exact manifests, least privileges, runtime guard and adversarial real-role tests together.
- Diagnostic 4 output may be large; each saved run must be checked for a complete, untruncated JSON cell before any Gate 4 recommendation.
- Gate 1 remains limited to the two approved brief hashes. Any material mechanism introduced at Gate 2—such as a token registry or new uniqueness structure—must be shown to preserve the approved behavior and be approved by exact SQL hash.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

Follow-up diagnostic 4 v2 at `b9f389b7fd1e` **cannot yet be recommended for Founder authorization** because the writer closure is still public-centric and cannot detect all writers it is meant to inventory. SQL work plan v2 at `c66d39a4ab03` must be revised for the exact edge contract, atomic collision behavior and complete brief-B proof mapping. Separately, QA **does recommend Gate 4 for diagnostic 3 at `a5cf2dc36147`**.

### A. Verification and response
1. **Writer closure public-centric (blocking 1): confirmed.** J2b, J2c and J4 read only `public` through the CTE `f`, and J2d returned only non-public routines that directly mention `study_group` or `invite_token`. v3 changes the closure itself: `f` now holds every routine of every non-system schema (everything except `pg_catalog`, `information_schema` and the toast and temporary schemas) with a schema-qualified identity, restricted to languages whose body can be read (`plpgsql`, `sql`); the seed is the same four criteria; the closure is now a **recursive caller closure** (a routine joins when it calls a member by `schema.name(` or by a bare name that resolves through the caller's own schema, `public` or its configured `search_path`), so a non-public routine that calls a writer transitively is caught; the call edges, definitions (J2c), triggers (J2d) and policies (J4) all use the same closure; J2a adds a per-schema, per-language count of the routines in every other schema so routines in unreadable languages are visible; J5 now lists non-system routines by schema-qualified name. The recursion joins only closure members against the routine list, which also keeps the run time bounded. **Fail-closed rule (also non-blocking 4):** plan v3 section 3 now states that any uncaptured lead (a closure routine without a saved definition, a reader or writer not on a manifest, a routine in an unreadable language, a `cron.job` row with `command_mentions_study_group` true or calling a closure or manifest routine, a trigger, rule, view or foreign-key action that can write the tables) **blocks Gate 2 for every dependent file** until a further read-only capture (for a job, the command text with secrets redacted) is authorized, run and saved and the item is entered, with its reason, in a manifest; the invite relation joins the seed of the Gate 2 fresh closure once it exists. Residual blind spot, unchanged: string-fragmented SQL without `EXECUTE`.
2. **C2 edge contract contradictory (blocking 2): confirmed, my error.** I wrote an exception for "the creator's implicit edge" without evidence, and J1 runs before the role exists so it cannot record one. Plan v3 C2 defines **one exact post-A-01 edge set**: exactly one `pg_auth_members` row granting `grp_batch_writer` to the trusted identity with `SET` true, `INHERIT` false, `ADMIN` false, and no row in which the role is the member; A-01 creates the role, issues that one grant and revokes any other grant on the role without assuming any; the test compares `pg_auth_members` to this set exactly. Superuser and CREATEROLE reachability is handled as a role attribute (compared with the J1 evidence), not as an edge.
3. **C3 allocator bypassed by non-batch creation (blocking 3): confirmed.** The advisory lock inside the allocator serializes only allocator callers; a non-batch insert taking the column default, or a client insert, does not use it, and a unique index on `study_groups.invite_token` cannot span the invite relation. v3 makes the guarantee structural: **token-domain guard triggers on both relations** (a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF invite_token` trigger on `study_groups`, a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on the invite relation), both taking the **same transaction-level advisory lock before checking** both domains and keeping it until commit, so every producer is serialized whichever path writes the value; the allocator becomes a retry convenience. The tests now stage the **concurrent race** with two real sessions and a barrier (an uncommitted non-batch insert of value T against an invite or allocator use of T, and the mirror), plus a direct client insert, catalog tests and a source test that no producer escapes both mechanisms. **Flagged for QA and Gate 2:** this is a new mechanism (a PROPOSAL, with the separate PROPOSAL of a group-to-group unique index); it implements the approved brief's rule (regenerate on any cross-column collision, fail closed) without changing approved behaviour. QA is asked to confirm that reading; the exact SQL is approved or rejected only at Gate 2.
4. **Brief B proof ownership (blocking 4): confirmed.** Plan v3 section 5 adds rows for brief B 4, B-I2 to B-I4 (every valid and invalid source and classification combination, subject optional, generated keys and the tie-breaker, no backfill, legacy updates), the four manual write paths (the server contract in B-04-TEST, the frontend paths assigned to the Gate 5 diff and Gate 7 test, not omitted), the label rule on four surfaces with boundary cases, the two-log alias flow, and section 8's SQL cases. **Column order stated:** the composite key is (`discipline_id`, `subject_id`) referencing a new unique constraint on `subjects (discipline_id, id)`, the same order on both sides (brief B 4.1); v2 had written the pair backwards.
5. **Correction to my diagnostic 3 reading (QA Round 44, "minor interpretation correction"): QA is right.** The guard is `p_user_id IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid() AND NOT is_admin()`; with both NULL the first operand is false, so an anonymous call with a NULL target id passes the guard and returns zero-valued aggregates (no user data); a non-NULL id other than the caller's is refused. My statement in the evidence index and in my Round 43 chat reply, that an anonymous caller "is refused", was wrong. I corrected the sentence in `T-001_FU3-index_05-10-2026.md` and the plan's measure-first row; the four raw files are unchanged. The security conclusion stands: no other user's data is exposed. The earlier reading that this removes the item from the open list is withdrawn in favour of "minor, information-free exposure, not a lead for the group design".
6. **Non-blocking 1 (C1 choreography): accepted.** Plan v3 C1 now specifies three sessions and a barrier sequence: S1 updates a membership row to a negative status and holds; S2 starts the initializer and must block on the table lock while S3 confirms by `pg_stat_activity` and `pg_locks` that S2 holds no batch row lock; S1 commits; S2 completes; the trigger is enabled after the run and after a forced mid-run error. Non-blocking 2, 3 and the residual risks (large output: each saved run will be checked for a complete, untruncated JSON cell before any Gate 4 request) are accepted.

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 45 and last QA-reviewed round 44.

The raw FU3 files are unchanged. This correction does not alter QA's Round 44 Gate 4 recommendation for diagnostic 3 at `a5cf2dc36147`; Founder acceptance remains pending.

### Round 44 finding-by-finding check

| Round 44 finding | QA result on Round 45 |
|---|---|
| 1 cross-schema writer closure and fail-closed route | **Partially closed.** J2b/J2c/J2d/J4 now use schema-qualified identities and a recursive caller closure across readable routines in every non-system schema, and plan section 3 now blocks dependent Gate 2 files on returned uncaptured leads. Two omissions still prevent this from being a complete closure (blocking finding 1). |
| 2 exact C2 edge contract | **Closed at plan level.** The post-A-01 set is exactly one `pg_auth_members` edge granting `grp_batch_writer` to the trusted identity with `SET TRUE`, `INHERIT FALSE`, `ADMIN FALSE`; no edge has `grp_batch_writer` as member. Superuser/CREATEROLE reachability is tested separately as a role attribute. |
| 3 collision-safe C3 mechanism | **Race primitive improved, approved behavior not preserved.** Both domains now share a transaction-level advisory lock, which closes the check/commit race for participating producers. The guard predicate nevertheless rejects states that approved brief A expressly requires (blocking finding 2). |
| 4 brief B proof ownership | **Closed at plan level.** B-04 uses the approved composite order `study_sessions(discipline_id, subject_id)` to `subjects(discipline_id, id)`, and the traceability/test rows now assign sections 4-8, B-I2 to B-I4, manual paths, legacy/no-backfill, generated keys and label tie-breaking, four-surface text boundaries, and the two-log over-limit alias journey. |
| Non-blocking C1 choreography | **Closed at plan level.** The three-session sequence can detect an initializer that takes a batch lock before obtaining the required table lock, and it checks trigger restoration after success and forced error. |
| Non-blocking fail-closed wording | **Closed for discovered leads, but not for omitted leads.** The new rule correctly stops dependent files for returned closure, manifest, unreadable-language, scheduled-job, trigger/rule/view and foreign-key leads. It cannot stop for an object the diagnostic never places in those result sets. |

### Blocking findings

1. **Diagnostic 4 v3 still cannot establish the complete cross-schema reader/writer closure it claims.** First, every J2/J4 seed is limited to `study_group`, `invite_token`, dynamic SQL and group-table views. It does not seed `content_group_shares`, although approved brief A 6.7/A-I6 and plan A-11 explicitly require the `cgs_select_member`, `cgs_delete_own_or_admin` and group-share reader branches to be reviewed. J2a flags that text only for `public` routines and J4 lists policies, so a non-`public` routine that touches only `content_group_shares` can be absent from the closure, definitions and call edges. Second, a bare-name call edge is accepted only when the callee is in the caller's schema, `public`, or the caller's function-local `proconfig` search path. Runtime resolution can also be affected by applicable database/role settings (and `$user`); an unpinned caller can therefore resolve a non-`public` callee that this test excludes. Because omitted nodes produce no positive result, section 3's otherwise sound fail-closed rule cannot catch either omission. Add the approved group-share relation/readers to the seed and either conservatively include every same-name bare-call candidate across non-system schemas or capture and prove every applicable effective search path before filtering an edge. The Gate 2 fresh closure must apply the same rule to the new invite relation.
2. **The C3 guard triggers contradict approved brief A's required original-invite lifecycle.** Plan v3 says a token must never be equal in the invite relation and `study_groups.invite_token`, and each trigger rejects equality across the domains. Approved brief A requires exactly that equality during compatibility: step 2 dual-writes the original invite with the column token, step 3 backfills and verifies an original invite with exactly the column token, and phase A1 keeps both values equal. Rollback step 6 likewise restores the column from a valid `is_original` invite before the new objects are retired. The proposed invite trigger would reject A-06/A-07, and the group trigger would reject the normal A-13 restore; the plan's claim that the mechanism changes no approved behavior is therefore false. The shared-lock approach can remain, but its predicate needs a narrowly specified, non-forgeable exception for the owning batch's immutable `is_original` mapping during the approved compatibility/backfill and controlled rollback paths, while still refusing unrelated cross-domain collisions. Gate 2 tests must exercise those allowed-equality paths as well as rejection, concurrency and rerun cases.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Diagnostic comments at the top duplicate the J5 description and leave the J3 introductory sentence incomplete. This is editorial, but should be corrected so the run guide accurately enumerates J1-J5.
2. J2a identifies non-`public` routines in unreadable languages only by schema/language counts. The plan's fail-closed rule makes a positive count blocking, which is safe, but the follow-up capture must enumerate identities before the dependent manifest can be reviewed.
3. Diagnostic 4 remains read-only in form. Authorization would still need to be for its exact hash, and any saved result must be checked for complete, untruncated JSON before a Gate 4 request.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- J1 retains the strong all-role graph, direct-edge and server-computed `MEMBER`/`USAGE`/`SET` reachability evidence needed by C2.
- The v3 closure is a substantial improvement over v2: readable routines are no longer restricted to `public`, identities include schema and signature, callers recurse transitively, and J2c returns definitions for closure members.
- The exact C2 edge set is now coherent and has a catalog comparison that would detect an extra edge, a missing edge or wrong `SET`/`INHERIT`/`ADMIN` options.
- A shared transaction-level lock in both token-domain guard triggers is an effective way to serialize default-generated, client-supplied and allocator-generated tokens once the approved original-mapping exception is designed correctly.
- Brief B's ownership and test matrix now cover the omissions identified in Round 44 without changing the approved design.

### Residual risks

- Static source matching remains an over-approximation and can miss deliberately fragmented dynamic SQL. The final safety case still depends on the exact writer/non-writer manifests, least privilege, runtime guards and adversarial real-role tests together.
- Neither diagnostic 4 v3 nor any migration SQL has been executed. This audit does not establish live syntax compatibility or live behavior.
- The C3 exception is security-sensitive: it must not be expressible by an ordinary client merely by supplying `is_original` or matching tokens. Its exact trigger/function ownership and phase behavior require review by SQL hash at Gate 2.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

Follow-up diagnostic 4 v3 at `97c9b9f9f65e` **cannot be recommended for Founder authorization** because the omitted group-share seed and incomplete bare-call resolution can hide cross-schema routines from both the closure and the fail-closed rule. SQL work plan v3 at `71e70dba081c` also requires revision because its C3 guard rejects approved compatibility, backfill and rollback behavior. QA's Round 44 recommendation that the Founder accept Gate 4 for diagnostic 3 at `a5cf2dc36147` stands; no other gate is approved by this round.
