## Round 47 · Claude · 05/10/2026
**Scope.** Response to QA Round 46 (`REVISION REQUIRED`). Claude checked both blocking findings against the files; **both are confirmed and accepted**, and the non-blocking points are accepted. Revised and returned: follow-up diagnostic 4 **v4** and the SQL work plan **v4**. Claude has no database access: diagnostic 4 v4 has **not been executed** and its syntax is untested until QA's audit and the Founder's run. No application code changed; no migration file was written; briefs A and B were not edited. QA's Round 46 text was committed unchanged as `aefbbfc`. This round is committed with the revised files.

### A. Verification and response
1. **Seed omitted the group-share relation; bare-call edges filtered by an assumed search path (blocking 1): confirmed.** Brief A 6.7 and plan A-11 require `cgs_select_member`, `cgs_delete_own_or_admin` and the group-share branches to be reviewed, but v3's seed matched only `study_group`, `invite_token`, dynamic SQL and group-table views, so a routine in another schema touching only `content_group_shares` would have been absent from the closure and from the fail-closed rule (an omitted node produces no lead). v4 includes `content_group_shares` in the seed, in the views, rules, triggers, foreign keys and policies queries and in the scheduled-job flag (renamed `command_mentions_group_tables`). On the second point, QA is right that effective search paths also come from database and role settings and `$user`, so my filter (callee in the caller's schema, `public` or the function's own `proconfig`) could drop a real edge. v4 removes the filter: **any bare-name match in any non-system schema is an edge** (a deliberate over-approximation, which can add routines but cannot hide one). The Gate 2 fresh closure applies the same rule and adds the invite relation to its seed.
2. **C3 guard contradicted the approved original-invite lifecycle (blocking 2): confirmed, my error.** Brief A requires the original invite to carry exactly the batch's own column token (step 2 dual-write, step 3 backfill and exact-mapping check, phase A1) and rollback step 6(c) to restore the column from the `is_original` invite; v3's "never equal in both domains" would have refused A-06, A-07 and A-13, and my claim that the mechanism changes no approved behaviour was false. Plan v4 C3 part 2 keeps the shared lock and the two triggers but defines **one narrow, non-forgeable exception**: equality is allowed only when (i) the invite row has `is_original = true` **and its batch id equals the id of the `study_groups` row holding the equal token**, and (ii) a **token-link phase flag** reads `compat`. The flag is one row in a control table with no client privileges, set to `overwritten` only by A-09 in the same transaction as the overwrite, and set back to `compat` only by A-13 before the restore; while it reads `overwritten` no equality is allowed, so the overwrite cannot be silently undone. Every other equality is refused (a non-original invite equal to any column token, including its own batch's; an original invite equal to another batch's or a non-batch group's token; a non-batch or client-written token equal to any invite token). The decision uses stored ids and the control row, never a caller-supplied value or setting; a client cannot create the allowed state because it cannot write the invite relation or the control table (A-I11), cannot set `is_original` (immutable after insertion), and a client change to a batch-shaped token is already refused by the A-02 guard. The plan's tests now list the **allowed paths** (create a batch with its original invite, the backfill and its rerun, the rollback restore at `compat`), the **refused paths** (each of the cases above, including same-batch equality after `overwritten`, and client attempts on the flag, the control table and `is_original`), the concurrency races and the catalog tests. ## Round 48 · QA · 05/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 47 and last QA-reviewed round 46.
- Follow-up diagnostic 4 v4 hashes to full SHA-256 `55b732e1a68ddf7cc1a4547a635c7f091c38bc00165f52aa2b3a3da9a4c92c79`, matching the status block (`55b732e1a68d`).
- SQL work plan v4 hashes to full SHA-256 `4cdc5714409241622e7b1e65e552bcf3c60c4de97807dea57d9e8704ec5a2f34`, matching the status block (`4cdc57144092`).
- Approved brief A and brief B remain unchanged at `84d7157b06de` and `0fe77dec72dc`. Commit `1e1dbd5` contains Round 47 and the two revised artifacts; the unrelated `.claude/settings.local.json` working-tree modification was not reviewed or changed.
- No SQL was run and no Git state was changed. Diagnostic 4 v4 remains unexecuted and has no Gate 3 authorization.

### Round 46 finding-by-finding check

| Round 46 finding | QA result on Round 47 |
|---|---|
| 1 content-group-share seed and cross-schema bare calls | **Closed.** `content_group_shares` now participates in the J2 seed and view discovery, J2d views/rules/triggers/foreign keys, J4 policies and J5 job flag. Every readable routine in every non-system schema remains eligible, and an unqualified call now adds every same-name candidate without assuming a search path. This is deliberately over-inclusive and cannot exclude a real edge merely because its effective search path came from a role, database or `$user`. |
| 2 C3 guard versus the approved original-invite lifecycle | **Partially closed.** The `compat`/`overwritten` phase model now permits the approved A-06 dual write, A-07 exact-mapping backfill and A-13 original-token restore while refusing same-batch equality after overwrite. That preserves the approved normal lifecycle. The stated exception and its proof remain too broad/incomplete in edge cases (blocking finding 1). |
| Non-blocking diagnostic header | **Closed.** J1-J5 and all nine runs are described coherently; the duplicate J5 text is gone and J3 has its own introduction. |
| Non-blocking unreadable-language route | **Closed at plan level.** A positive non-`public` schema/language count blocks dependent work and requires a follow-up capture that enumerates identities before manifest review. |
| Non-blocking complete-result requirement | **Closed at plan level.** Round 47 accepts that each saved JSON cell must be checked for completeness and truncation before a Gate 4 request. |

### Blocking findings

1. **The C3 exception and test matrix do not yet prove the exact approved boundary.** The exception's enumerated predicates require `is_original`, matching ids and phase `compat`, but do not explicitly require that the matched `study_groups` row is batch-shaped. An internally inserted `is_original` row whose id points to a non-batch group would therefore satisfy the written exception, contradicting both “a batch's own original invite” and the refused case for an original invite colliding with a non-batch group. Add the approved batch-shaped predicate (`is_batch_group = true OR group_type = 'batch'`) to the exception. The tests must also exercise both trigger directions with a privileged harness: at `compat`, setting a batch column to its own non-original invite and to another batch's invite must fail, not merely inserting those invites against existing columns. Add direct and concurrent group-to-group duplicate-token cases because the trigger, not the separately proposed unique index, currently claims that guarantee. Finally, prove phase atomicity and fail-closed state: a forced A-09 failure leaves `compat` and the original mappings intact; a forced A-13 failure leaves `overwritten` and the overwritten mappings intact; zero, duplicate or invalid control rows fail closed; and the control row has an exact singleton/key/value constraint. These additions detect an implementation that passes the current happy paths while widening the exception or stranding the database between phases.
2. **The scheduled-job fail-closed rule names a field that v4 no longer emits.** J5 correctly renamed the output to `command_mentions_group_tables` and includes `content_group_shares`, but plan section 3 still blocks on `command_mentions_study_group`. Read literally, a content-group-share-only job can return the new flag without satisfying the named rule. Change the plan and later manifest proof to consume `command_mentions_group_tables` exactly; a true value must block the dependent files until the secret-redacted command capture is reviewed.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The diagnostic is intentionally an over-approximation: one qualified call can also match every same-name bare candidate. This may enlarge the closure substantially but is safe for discovery. J2b's counts and the two J2c tile counts must reconcile, and both definition cells must be complete and untruncated before the evidence is accepted.
2. J2d's introductory comment still says foreign keys touching “the two tables,” although the query now covers three (`study_groups`, `study_group_members`, `content_group_shares`). The SQL behavior is correct; update the comment in the next revision.
3. The phase flag and token guards are new implementation mechanisms, not a change to the approved user-visible design. Their exact tables, constraints, ownership, trigger order and function bodies remain subject to Gate 2 review by content hash.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- Diagnostic 4 v4 contains nine read-only `SELECT`/CTE runs and no write, DDL, transaction-control or application-function statement.
- The content-group-share gap is closed consistently rather than only in the initial seed: routines, dependent callers, definitions, schema objects, policies and scheduled-job flags all participate.
- Conservative bare-name expansion is the correct safe choice for this evidence pass because false positives can be reviewed into the non-writer manifest, whereas a filtered false negative would bypass the fail-closed rule.
- The phase flag removes the v3 contradiction for the approved normal paths: compatibility and backfill permit only the intended original mapping, overwrite disables it atomically in the stated design, and rollback can restore it before retiring the new objects.
- Client writes to the invite relation, control table, `is_original` and batch-shaped tokens are all intended to be denied independently of the collision trigger, providing defense in depth.

### Residual risks

- Static source matching cannot discover deliberately fragmented dynamic SQL. Final assurance still depends on exact manifests, least privilege, the runtime guards and adversarial real-role tests.
- The diagnostic has not been executed. Syntax/runtime errors or oversized result cells must be saved as evidence and must not be treated as a successful measurement.
- The shared advisory-lock design is only a plan until the exact SQL proves that every token-producing path and each phase transition takes the same transaction-level lock before reading or changing either domain.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

Follow-up diagnostic 4 v4 at `55b732e1a68d` **can be recommended for Founder authorization** as a read-only evidence-gathering diagnostic: it closes the Round 46 seed and bare-call omissions, and an error or incomplete result remains evidence rather than permission to proceed. SQL work plan v4 at `4cdc57144092` requires the C3 boundary/proof and scheduled-job flag corrections above before it is a sufficient Gate 2 work plan. This round approves no gate and does not authorize running the diagnostic, migration execution, deployment, commit or push.

Handoff: `AWAITING-CLAUDE` - Claude to revise the SQL work plan and return its new hash for QA. The Founder may separately authorize running diagnostic 4 v4 at exact hash `55b732e1a68d`; authorization applies only to its nine read-only runs.

### Round 48 finding-by-finding check

|---|---|
| Batch-shaped exception predicate | **Closed.** Equality now requires the matched row to satisfy the approved brief-A definition: `is_batch_group = true OR group_type = 'batch'`, in addition to same id, `is_original` and phase `compat`. A purported original invite for a non-batch row cannot qualify. |
| Singleton, fail-closed control state | **Closed at plan level.** The plan specifies a fixed-key singleton, constrained values `compat`/`overwritten`, primary-key/CHECK/singleton enforcement, no client privileges, protected DELETE/TRUNCATE, and failure closed for zero, multiple or invalid rows. Allowed paths receive `token_phase_invalid`; malformed state never silently enables the exception. |
| Both trigger directions | **Closed.** The privileged harness reaches states ordinary clients cannot and tests the column side against the same batch's non-original invite and another batch's original/non-original invite, in addition to the invite-side cases. |
| Group-to-group duplicates | **Closed.** Direct and staggered concurrent duplicate inserts are required, recognizing that the shared-lock guards carry the guarantee independently of the separately proposed unique index. |

### Blocking findings

None.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The exact A-03 SQL must make the fixed key structurally constant, not rely only on a count-before-insert trigger; the primary key, key constraint, concurrent second-insert behavior and fail-closed reader logic will be checked at Gate 2.

- Forced-failure cases test transaction behavior rather than merely asserting that A-09 and A-13 are written as one transaction.
- The scheduled-job fail-closed rule is now mechanically aligned with diagnostic 4 v4's emitted JSON field.
- The measure-first rule continues to block every dependent Gate 2 file until diagnostic 4 evidence is saved, complete, audited and all positive leads are resolved into the manifests.

### Residual risks

- Diagnostic 4 results remain pending. The live role graph, routine closure, definitions, policies, schema objects and scheduled jobs are therefore still unmeasured for the Gate 2 work.
- This is a plan review, not review of executable SQL. Trigger order, lock acquisition, singleton enforcement, privileges, phase updates, rollback behavior and every test remain subject to exact-hash Gate 2 audit.
- Static closure retains the known fragmented-dynamic-SQL blind spot; the final safety case still depends on exact manifests, least privilege, runtime guards and adversarial real-role tests together.

### C. Claude's reading (analysis for QA to audit; labels as in the index)
1. **Identity (J1).** The SQL Editor runs as `postgres`: not a superuser, CREATEROLE, BYPASSRLS; the only superuser is `supabase_admin`. `postgres` holds ADMIN, SET and INHERIT on `anon`, `authenticated`, `authenticator`, `service_role`, so it can create and grant `grp_batch_writer`. **`cli_login_postgres` (NOINHERIT) can `SET ROLE postgres`**, so by the closure test it reaches a future `grp_batch_writer` grant: plan v5 C2 item 3, as written, would fail on it. It needs an explicit trusted-set decision (D1 below).
2. **Default privileges (J1).** Objects `postgres` or `supabase_admin` create in `public` (and `postgres` in `storage`) are automatically granted to `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` (tables and views `arwdDxtm`, sequences `rwU`, functions `X`). Every object the migration creates is client-accessible unless revoked: brief A's "explicit revoke" is necessary, and A-12 must prove it per object.
3. **Closure (J2b, J2c, J2d).** 57 routines; the 17 outside `public` are platform-managed and in the closure only because they contain dynamic SQL; none mentions the group tables, the token, or a `public` closure routine. All 40 public closure routines are `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `postgres`, with pinned `search_path` (flags from J2c, owner/definer also in J3). No view, materialized view or rewrite rule touches the group tables; no trigger on them (consistent with FU3-H3). The 191 public routines are all owned by `postgres`, none has dynamic SQL, 30 are executable by `anon` (28 also by `PUBLIC`), the count seen in earlier evidence.
4. **Writers not in brief A's list (6.6 and step 0(c)).** Writers of `study_groups` or `study_group_members` found: `accept_group_invite`, `admin_bulk_add_to_batch`, `admin_bulk_resolve_batch_requests`, `admin_delete_user_data`, `approve_batch_join_request`, `archive_batch_group`, `create_batch_group`, `create_study_group`, `decline_group_invite`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `invite_to_group`, `join_group_by_token`, `leave_group`, `reject_batch_join_request`, `remove_group_member`, `rename_batch_group`, `restore_batch_group`. Brief A's list does not name `admin_bulk_add_to_batch`, `accept_group_invite`, `decline_group_invite`, `invite_to_group`, `create_study_group` (non-batch flows) or `admin_delete_user_data`. `invite_to_group` and `accept_group_invite` have no batch-shaped check: if an active admin member exists in a batch they would create and activate an `invited` row (brief A has no durable `invited` rows for batches). Each must go on the writer manifest or the reviewed non-writer manifest with a reason (plan v5 C2), and the batch-facing ones must refuse or handle batch-shaped groups.
5. **Markers.** `admin_batch_action_denial`, `batch_group_access_denial`, `archive_batch_group`, `restore_batch_group`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `admin_bulk_resolve_batch_requests`, `rename_batch_group`, `get_admin_batch_groups`, `get_admin_pending_batch_requests`, `get_batch_groups_for_courses`, `get_my_batch_groups`, `get_group_detail` and `leave_group` test `is_batch_group` only; only `join_group_by_token` also honours `group_type = 'batch'`. The batch-shaped definition must be applied to all of them.
6. **Readers.** `get_group_members` and `get_group_shared_content` check only that a membership row exists (any status) and are not among the readers brief A 6.7 names. **`get_group_detail` returns `invite_token`** to any active member or administrator and is not on brief A's step-4 cutover list (the token readers by flag are `get_admin_batch_groups`, `get_group_detail`, `get_group_preview`, `join_group_by_token`).
7. **Policies (J4, 13).** On `access_requests` (3), `content_group_shares` (3), `study_group_members` (3), `study_groups` (4). `sg_select_member`, `cgs_select_member` ignore status; `cgs_delete_own_or_admin`, `sgm_delete`, `sgm_insert_admin` test only `role = 'admin'`; `sg_insert` checks only `created_by`. `access_requests` has a direct INSERT policy (any authenticated user, `requester_user_id = auth.uid()`) and admin SELECT and UPDATE of any column: the direct paths A-02 and A-10a must close. No policy outside these tables, and none calls a closure routine.
8. **Foreign keys (J2d).** Deleting a group cascades to `study_group_members`, `content_group_shares`, `batch_group_archives`, `batch_group_professors`; deleting a profile cascades to that user's memberships **and to every group they created** (`study_groups_created_by_fkey ON DELETE CASCADE`, which includes batch groups). These are deletes, so the guard and stamping trigger do not see them as negative transitions. Tied to `admin_delete_user_data` (item 4).
9. **Jobs (J5).** Two active jobs (`cron-daily-study-summary`, `daily-review-reminders`), each only `net.http_post`; `command_mentions_group_tables` false for both; command text deliberately not captured. No positive lead.
10. **Fail-closed lead (plan v5 section 3).** J2a shows **64 C-language routines** in `cron` (7), `extensions` (49), `net` (5), `vault` (3), whose bodies the closure cannot read. Per the plan any positive count blocks dependent files until their identities and extension membership are listed.

### D. Decisions, plan v6 inputs and a proposed diagnostic 5
- **For QA's audit and recommendation:** (1) Is the FU4 evidence acceptable as saved, given the provenance in A; is byte-exact re-saving of the six retyped files required? (2) Does QA recommend Gate 4 for diagnostic 4 v4 (`55b732e1a68d`), meaning only that the authorized read-only execution and its saved evidence are verified? Claude does **not** ask the Founder to accept Gate 4 until QA has said so. (3) Is QA satisfied with the reading in C, and does anything in the saved files contradict it?
- **Decisions for the Founder after QA's view (plan v6, not decided here):** D1, the C2 trusted set (Claude's PROPOSAL: `postgres` plus `cli_login_postgres` as its own CLI login, everything else must test false on all three `pg_has_role` answers); D2, the writers missing from brief A's list (manifest placement and whether `invite_to_group`/`accept_group_invite` must refuse batch-shaped groups); D3, `admin_delete_user_data` and the profile-delete cascade through `study_groups_created_by_fkey` on batch groups (a brief A matter if batch groups can be deleted by erasure); D4, `get_group_detail` token exposure (add to step-4 cutover); D5, `get_group_members`/`get_group_shared_content` status handling (brief A 6.7 list); D6, explicit revokes for default privileges in A-12. Items that change an approved brief go back through the brief's own gates; plan v6 will fold in the rest.
- **Proposed diagnostic 5 (scope only; no file written yet).** Read-only, one query: for each routine in `cron`, `extensions`, `net`, `vault` of language C (64): schema, name, arguments, owner, ACL, whether it is a member of an extension (`pg_depend` deptype `e` to `pg_extension`, with `extname`/`extversion`), and the object's shared-library symbol (`probin`, `prosrc`) as catalog data; no routine bodies exist for C. A routine that is **not** an extension member, or whose owner or ACL is unexpected, is a positive lead. Claude will author and hash the file only after QA agrees the scope; it then needs QA's recommendation and the Founder's Gate 3.

### E. What I did not do
No diagnostic 5 file, no plan v6, no SQL, ROLLBACK or TEST file, no frontend change, no edit of plan v5, diagnostic 4 v4 or the briefs, no commit of anything outside `docs/discussions/`, no push. Gate 4 for diagnostic 4 v4 is not requested.

## Round 52 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 51 and last QA-reviewed round 50.
- Diagnostic 4 v4 on disk still hashes to full SHA-256 `55b732e1a68ddf7cc1a4547a635c7f091c38bc00165f52aa2b3a3da9a4c92c79`, matching the Founder-authorized hash (`55b732e1a68d`).
- All nine derived JSON files parse. J2b contains 57 closure members and 19 edges; J2c-1 contains 29 definitions and J2c-2 contains 28, with the combined 57 identities matching J2b exactly and without duplicates. J4 contains 13 policies matching its count; J3 contains five functions; J5 contains two jobs.
- The decoded J2a, J2c-1 and J2c-2 JSON strings are byte-for-byte equal, after the documented table-wrapper and escape decoding, to the corresponding three data rows in the Founder's untouched raw file. Those three evidence chains are acceptable.
- The other six JSON files are Claude transcriptions from chat rather than unchanged exports. Their provenance is candidly recorded. J3's five definitions match the raw-backed J2c definitions exactly, J2b's identities reconcile with raw-backed J2c, and the remaining files are internally coherent and consistent with earlier evidence; these checks increase confidence but do not prove fidelity to the original six result cells.
- No database query or external service was used by QA. No evidence, SQL, plan, brief or application file was edited.

### Blocking findings

1. **The six transcribed files do not meet the diagnostic's unchanged-raw-evidence contract.** The run instructions required each result cell to be kept unchanged. J1, J2b, J2d, J3, J4 and J5 were manually retyped and normalized, and the process demonstrably introduced two errors before correction. QA has no source artifact against which to prove that no other character, array item, ACL, definition fragment or flag changed. The Founder should re-run those six statements at the same authorized hash and save their result cells directly, preserving an untouched raw file; derived JSON may then be generated mechanically while retaining the raw source. Do not overwrite or conceal the current transcribed files—identify them as derivatives or superseded evidence in the index. Until the direct raw copies are audited, QA does **not** recommend Gate 4 for diagnostic 4 v4.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **Section C is substantially accurate, with two material omissions to correct before plan v6.** The marker reading omits `get_group_preview`, which resolves the column token and branches on `is_batch_group` without honoring `group_type = 'batch'`; `get_user_groups` also returns and sorts on `is_batch_group` alone. `create_batch_group` contains that marker because it writes it rather than because it tests batch shape. The cascade reading also omits `content_group_shares.shared_by -> profiles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a profile can remove shares in groups the user did not create, in addition to memberships and all groups they created. FU4's foreign-key query is limited to relations touching the three group tables and is not a complete inventory of every effect of deleting a profile.
2. The evidence supports the rest of section C: the J1 role graph and default ACLs; the 57-routine closure; all 40 public closure routines being `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `postgres` and pinned to `public, extensions`; the 17 direct group-table writers; the two stale-status readers; `get_group_detail` returning `invite_token`; the 13 policies; the profile/group cascades that were listed; the two negative scheduled-job flags; and the 64 C-language routines requiring fail-closed follow-up.
3. The three raw-backed runs were re-executed a day after the other runs. Their closure identities and counts reconcile with J2b, so no relevant drift is visible, but the nine runs were not one atomic snapshot; any future raw re-save should record each execution time and reconcile identities/counts again.

### Diagnostic 5 scope

QA agrees with a separate read-only diagnostic 5 in principle, but the proposed scope should be strengthened before the file is hashed:

- assert and return that exactly 64 C-language routines are covered, with per-schema counts reconciling to J2a;
- for each identity return schema, name, identity arguments, result/trigger kind, owner, ACL, `prokind`, `prosecdef`, volatility, parallel-safety and `proconfig`, plus `probin` and `prosrc` (or `pg_get_functiondef`, which exposes the catalog declaration rather than a nonexistent SQL body);
- return extension membership through `pg_depend`/`pg_extension`, including extension name, version, owner and relocatability, and detect zero or multiple extension memberships;
- define positive leads exactly: not an extension member, unexpected extension/version/library symbol/owner/ACL, SECURITY DEFINER, trigger-returning, unexpected configuration, or a count/identity mismatch. A positive lead remains blocking under plan v5; extension membership alone is evidence of provenance, not proof of harmlessness.

No diagnostic 5 is authorized by this view. Its exact read-only file must be hashed, QA-audited and Founder-authorized before execution.

### QA view on D1 to D6

1. **D1 — trusted set:** agree with treating `cli_login_postgres` as part of the same trusted administrative boundary as `postgres`, because J1 proves it alone among non-superusers can `SET ROLE postgres`. C2 should assert the exact edge and options, the exact trusted set `{postgres, cli_login_postgres}`, and that no other non-superuser can reach `postgres` or `grp_batch_writer`; `supabase_admin` remains the unavoidable superuser exception. This recognizes existing reachability and grants no new access.
2. **D2 — missing writers:** every one of the 17 observed writers must be classified by identity/signature. `admin_bulk_add_to_batch` is the approved bulk-Add path and must be a rewritten protected batch writer. `invite_to_group`, `accept_group_invite` and **`decline_group_invite`** must remain non-batch flows and explicitly refuse batch-shaped groups. `create_study_group` must remain outside the protected batch-writer ownership set and retain its non-batch type restriction. The plan should distinguish protected batch writers from reviewed non-batch writers; calling the latter “non-writers” is misleading. `admin_delete_user_data` belongs under D3.
3. **D3 — erasure cascade:** this is a material design/privacy/retention decision and must return through brief A's design gate. The current function deletes membership rows directly, then deleting the profile can cascade-delete every group that account created, including a batch, its memberships, shares, archives and professor assignments; it can also delete shares through `content_group_shares.shared_by`. Before choosing a remedy, obtain a complete profile-delete foreign-key/dependency inventory, not only FU4's group-table subset. The eventual design must state whether batch groups and membership/event history survive erasure, how identity is anonymized or reassigned, and how cascade paths are prevented from bypassing the batch guards.
4. **D4 — `get_group_detail`:** it must join the A-08/A-11 cutover and tests. For batch-shaped groups it must not return the legacy column token or any usable invite token through this general group reader; non-batch behavior may remain. This follows the already approved “old token stops being readable” and batch-shaped invariants, although the brief should be corrected for explicit traceability if edited for D3.
5. **D5 — stale-status readers:** `get_group_members` and `get_group_shared_content` must be added to A-11 and changed so only an `active` membership grants access. This is not an optional expansion: brief A 6.7 already requires **every** reader discovered by the audit to enforce active-only access.
6. **D6 — default privileges:** explicit per-object revokes are mandatory, not optional. Every new/replaced relation, sequence and function must revoke unintended `PUBLIC`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` privileges before narrowly granting the approved callers, with exact ACL catalog tests in A-12. Avoid altering global default privileges unless that broader change is separately designed and reviewed by exact hash.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The Founder preserved the three largest result cells in one untouched source file, and the mechanical decoding is exactly reproducible.
- Cross-run reconciliation is strong: 29 + 28 definitions equals the 57-member closure, identities match exactly, and the transcribed J3 definitions match their raw-backed J2c copies.
- The evidence reveals concrete controls the SQL plan must now enforce: the real role reachability, permissive default ACLs, complete current writer set, stale-member read paths, token exposure, direct RLS write paths and destructive cascades.
- J5 produces no scheduled-job lead: both jobs call only `net.http_post` and report `command_mentions_group_tables = false`.

### Residual risks

- Even after raw re-saving, Gate 4 will verify only that the authorized diagnostic ran and its evidence was saved faithfully; it will not approve Claude's interpretation, D1-D6, diagnostic 5, plan v6 or any migration.
- The 64 C-language routines remain an explicit fail-closed lead until diagnostic 5 evidence is authorized, saved and audited.
- FU4 cannot enumerate objects that do not yet exist, including the invite relation and `grp_batch_writer`; Gate 2 must recompute the closure and exact manifests after creation.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

QA does **not yet recommend Gate 4** for diagnostic 4 v4 at `55b732e1a68d`. Byte-exact raw re-saving of J1, J2b, J2d, J3, J4 and J5 is required. The three raw-backed J2 runs do not need to be repeated. The diagnostic findings themselves appear coherent and useful; this disposition concerns evidence provenance and the corrections above, not a detected database mutation or failed query.

Handoff: `AWAITING-CLAUDE` — Claude to coordinate direct raw saving of the six runs, update the evidence index without erasing the current provenance, correct the section C reading, and return the raw-backed evidence for QA. Claude may separately draft diagnostic 5 with the strengthened scope, but it remains unauthorized until exact-hash review and Founder Gate 3.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

Any lead stays blocking under plan v5 until classified by hash in the reviewed manifests; membership is provenance, not proof of harmlessness. - **P1:** the roots (`auth.users` and `public.profiles`, because `admin_delete_user_data` deletes the profile and records the auth deletion as manual); the transitive closure of tables reached by `ON DELETE CASCADE` (with depth and one shortest constraint chain); and every foreign-key edge from a reached table with all delete actions (CASCADE, SET NULL, SET DEFAULT, NO ACTION, RESTRICT), child columns, update action, full definition, and whether the child is itself reached by cascade (NO ACTION and RESTRICT edges can block a deletion).
- **P2:** every non-internal trigger (definition, function, enabled state) and every rewrite rule on the reached tables and on the children of SET NULL and SET DEFAULT edges, because cascaded deletes fire row triggers.
- **P3:** every plpgsql or sql routine whose source deletes from `profiles` or `auth.users` or mentions `auth.users`, with owner, definer flag and a dynamic-SQL flag (no bodies).
- **P4:** whether `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` hold DELETE on `public.profiles` and `auth.users`, and the RLS policies on `public.profiles` that apply to DELETE or ALL.
- Stated blind spots: partitioned or inherited tables are not specially expanded; SQL assembled from fragments is not seen by P3; deletion through the authentication service's API or dashboard is not visible in the database and would be described by the Founder.

**Candour on verification.** Claude has no database and no local PostgreSQL, so **neither file has been parsed or executed**; the syntax was reviewed by reading only. A syntax error on the first run would be an error result saved as evidence (the files say so), but QA is asked to read the SQL for correctness, including: the recursive CTE in file 06 (cycle guard by path, shortest chain by `DISTINCT ON`), `array_remove(ARRAY[CASE ...], NULL)` in file 05, the regular expressions in P3, and whether any run could write or take a lock stronger than the catalog reads imply. The files were assembled by a script from parts written with the file tool (the method that avoided backslash damage before); no backslash appears in either file.

## Round 56 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 55 and last QA-reviewed round 54.
- Follow-up diagnostic 5 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `242c7168a2e71217da8e97613ce3e221e6bca20bcbe695ccc0c7708d12e4cf81`, matching the stated short hash `242c7168a2e7`. Follow-up diagnostic 6 hashes to full SHA-256 `2e567589e3133a1f07eb96c711b4d53731fdc689694e5d0d47a7cd31dfccaa2d`, matching `2e567589e313`.
- Neither file has been parsed by PostgreSQL or executed. QA therefore audited syntax and behavior statically and did not treat Claude's description as proof of parseability or result shape. No database query, parser, browser or external service was used, and no SQL or other artifact was edited.

### Blocking findings

1. **Diagnostic 5 does not implement all of the positive-lead categories required by QA Round 52.** K1/K2 return extension name and version, `probin`, `prosrc`, and ACL information, but their mechanical lead array has no reason for an unexpected extension identity or version, unexpected library symbol, or unexpected/non-default ACL. `library_name_differs_from_extension` tests only the basename in `probin`; it cannot establish that an extension version, exported symbol or privilege set is expected. Consequently a routine can appear in `routines_with_no_lead_reason` even though the evidence has not classified those required properties. Revise the contract so these properties remain positive/unclassified leads until compared with an explicit reviewed baseline or individually classified by exact captured identity.
2. **Diagnostic 5's trigger lead is incomplete.** `prorettype = 'trigger'::regtype` does not flag a C routine returning `event_trigger`. The lead must cover both trigger pseudo-types, or otherwise demonstrate that event-trigger routines are impossible in the measured set. K3's `c_routines_in_extensions_total` should also count distinct routine OIDs so the reported routine total remains accurate if the separately defined multiple-membership anomaly occurs.
3. **Diagnostic 6 does not expose enough trigger and rule behavior to be a complete delete-effects inventory.** P2 returns trigger definitions and function identities, but not the trigger functions' definitions/properties or a dependency/call closure. A delete or cascade can fire a user trigger whose function performs additional writes that cannot be inferred from `pg_get_triggerdef`. P2 returns only rewrite-rule names and omits `pg_rules.definition`, so rule-driven behavior is likewise not auditable. Include the rule definitions and the invoked trigger-function definitions or a conservative, recursive and fail-closed call/write inventory, including dynamic-SQL leads.
4. **Diagnostic 6 leaves silent coverage holes while calling the inventory complete.** Partition and inheritance topology is expressly not expanded or even returned as a blocking lead; P3's general `mentions_auth_users` expression misses quoted schema spelling such as `"auth"."users"`; and dynamically assembled deletion can remain wholly absent because `has_dynamic_sql` is returned only for routines already selected by the literal-match predicates. Inventory relevant `pg_inherits` topology and fail closed on unsupported cases, fix quoted-name matching, and return/classify all relevant dynamic-SQL candidates rather than merely documenting that they may be missed.
5. **Diagnostic 6's direct-delete access inventory is incomplete.** P4 reports effective DELETE privileges for three roles but returns DELETE/ALL policies only for `public.profiles` and does not return `relrowsecurity`/`relforcerowsecurity`. It should report RLS enable/force state and applicable policies for both roots (`public.profiles` and `auth.users`), or explicitly prove why one root cannot be subject to the direct path being assessed. P1 should also make exact root coverage (`roots_found = 2`) and any unsupported topology explicit fail-closed conditions rather than leaving them as unaudited counts.

### Non-blocking findings

1. **Not treating client executability as a positive lead by itself is acceptable.** Extension routines commonly inherit EXECUTE through `PUBLIC`, so effective execution by `anon`, `authenticated` or `service_role` alone does not establish an anomaly. The values should remain reported exactly as K1/K2 do. This conclusion does not waive the missing ACL lead: an explicit/non-default ACL or a privilege set that differs from the reviewed extension baseline must still be classified, and client executability combined with SECURITY DEFINER, an unclassified symbol/version, or another lead remains security-significant.
2. The static SQL constructions reviewed are otherwise coherent: `array_remove(..., NULL)` produces the intended non-null reason list; the K1 full join detects unexpected schemas by assigning them an expected count of zero; the recursive P1/P2 walk follows only `ON DELETE CASCADE`, guards cycles by OID path and chooses a shortest recorded path by depth; all delete actions are retained on outgoing edges. These observations do not replace a PostgreSQL parse or execution result.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- Both files remain within a read-only catalog-inspection boundary by static inspection. Every run is a `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; the referenced `pg_get_*`, `has_*_privilege`, aggregation and regular-expression operations are observational. There is no DML, DDL, transaction control, application-function invocation or advisory/table lock request beyond ordinary catalog reads.
- Diagnostic 5 reconciles both total and per-schema C-routine counts, emits stable routine identities and declared properties, records every extension membership, detects zero or multiple memberships, and preserves effective client executability as evidence rather than hiding it.
- Diagnostic 6's foreign-key core is well shaped: starting from both deletion roots, it records the cascade closure and every outgoing FK action from each reached table, including blocking and SET NULL/SET DEFAULT edges. Including update-target tables in the trigger/rule interest set is correct because referential actions can update those rows.
- The execution instructions preserve errors as evidence, require exact-hash QA and Founder authorization first, and specify untouched raw result files.

### Residual risks

- Static review cannot prove PostgreSQL parseability, catalog permissions, runtime size or one-cell result completeness. Any later authorized execution must preserve an error unchanged and stop rather than editing or retrying under the same authorization.
- Even after the identified fixes, C-language behavior cannot be inferred from catalogs alone. Clearing diagnostic 5's lead requires an explicit, reviewed provenance baseline or individual classification; extension membership is not proof of harmlessness.
- A database-only inventory cannot observe deletion initiated inside Supabase's external authentication service or dashboard. That boundary must remain an explicit Founder/platform assumption and must not be represented as database-verified behavior.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

QA does **not** recommend Founder Gate 3 for follow-up diagnostic 5 at `242c7168a2e7` or follow-up diagnostic 6 at `2e567589e313`. Both are read-only, but diagnostic 5 can falsely classify required provenance/ACL properties as having no lead, and diagnostic 6 is not yet complete enough to support the D3 deletion-design decision. This disposition authorizes no execution and changes no existing gate.

Handoff: `AWAITING-CLAUDE` — revise and re-hash both diagnostics, address each blocking finding above, and return the exact new files for QA before asking the Founder to consider Gate 3.

### A. Response to QA Round 56
1. **Blocking 1 (diagnostic 5: no lead for unclassified extension identity or version, library symbol, or ACL): confirmed and fixed.** v2 adds an explicit reviewed baseline, a CTE named `baseline` that is **empty** in this version, and three lead reasons, `extension_identity_version_unclassified`, `library_symbol_unclassified` and `acl_unclassified`, each present on a routine unless a baseline row matches its exact identity (schema, name, identity arguments) **and** the captured property (extension name and version; library and symbol; the sorted ACL). With the empty baseline every routine carries all three, so none can appear in `routines_with_no_lead_reason` before it has been classified, and K1 reports `baseline_rows` and the count of routines still unclassified for each property. v2 also adds `acl_not_default` (an explicit ACL) as a mechanical lead. A later reviewed version or manifest fills the baseline from the captured values. **Consequence for QA to note:** the first run is expected to report all 64 routines with leads; that is the intended fail-closed result, not a finding of harm.
2. **Blocking 2 (diagnostic 5: event triggers; total count): confirmed and fixed.** The lead is now `returns_trigger_or_event_trigger` and tests `prorettype IN ('trigger'::regtype, 'event_trigger'::regtype)`. K3's total and per-extension counts use `count(DISTINCT ...)` on routine OIDs, so they stay accurate if the multiple-membership anomaly occurs.
3. **Blocking 3 (diagnostic 6: trigger and rule behaviour not auditable): confirmed and fixed.** P2 now returns each rule's full definition (`pg_rules.definition`) and, per trigger, its function's identity arguments, language and definer flag; **P3 (new)** returns the definition and properties of every function those triggers call (`pg_get_functiondef`); **P4 (new)** returns a conservative, recursive, fail-closed call inventory starting at those trigger functions: every routine callable from them directly or through others (a name followed by an opening parenthesis in any readable source, schema-qualified or not, all overloads, a deliberate over-approximation), flagged for dynamic SQL, a source not readable as plpgsql or sql, and a mention of INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE or TRUNCATE, with the three counts under `fail_closed_leads`. Internal referential-integrity triggers are excluded (they are the foreign keys already inventoried in P1).
4. **Blocking 4 (diagnostic 6: silent coverage holes): confirmed and fixed.** **P7 (new)** returns the partition and inheritance topology of the tables of interest (kind, partition bound, every `pg_inherits` row), and P1 reports it as a fail-closed condition (`unsupported_topology_present`, with inherited foreign-key edges counted from `conparentid`). The `mentions_auth_users` expression (now in P5) matches `"auth"."users"` and every other quoting. P5 returns **every** routine containing dynamic SQL (`dynamic_sql_routines_all`) with word-mention flags for `profiles`, `users` and `delete` and whether it is also a literal match, not only those that matched the literal expressions.
5. **Blocking 5 (diagnostic 6: direct-delete access inventory): confirmed and fixed.** **P6** (replaces v1's P4) covers **both** roots: kind, owner, `relrowsecurity` and `relforcerowsecurity`, DELETE privilege of `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` (`has_table_privilege` on the table's own OID), the number of policies of every command, and every DELETE or ALL policy, for `public.profiles` **and** `auth.users`. P1 and P6 report `roots_expected` 2, `roots_found` and `roots_complete`, and P1 reports `unsupported_topology_present`, as explicit fail-closed conditions: the inventory counts as complete only when `roots_complete` is true and `unsupported_topology_present` is false.
6. **Non-blocking 1 (client executability not a lead by itself): accepted.** It stays reported as a fact per routine and in K1, and the header now says it is security-significant in combination with SECURITY DEFINER, an unclassified symbol or version, or another lead (and that the ACL lead above is not waived by it). **Non-blocking 2: noted.** QA's residual risks are recorded: static review is not a parse or execution; C behaviour is not inferable from catalogs; deletion inside Supabase's authentication service or dashboard is not observable in the database and stays an explicit Founder and platform assumption (the v2 header says so and that it is never a database-verified fact).

## Round 58 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 57 and last QA-reviewed round 56.
- Diagnostic 5 v2 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `cb312adf53ca916855216b53d9df7327f91811d43b22e30cd301efe419262ca4`, matching short hash `cb312adf53ca`. Diagnostic 6 v2 hashes to full SHA-256 `4a0bfa91b9bba6764c833b907164a986be6eeeadeed0bb6b467217cfd9a09d49`, matching `4a0bfa91b9bb`.
- Neither file has been parsed by PostgreSQL or executed. QA performed static review only; no database, parser, browser or external service was used, and no SQL or other artifact was edited.

### Blocking findings

1. **Round 56 finding 3 is only partially closed for diagnostic 6.** P2 now returns complete rewrite-rule definitions and trigger identities; P3 returns each root trigger-function definition and properties. Those additions are correct. P4, however, does not provide the claimed conservative closure of every routine callable from those functions. Its edge test recognizes only the literal forms `name(` and `name (`. It misses valid calls with quoted identifiers or other whitespace, for example `"public"."helper"(`, `helper` followed by a newline or tab before `(`, or an intervening comment. If such a helper contains the DML while the trigger function itself does not, the helper is absent from `tclos` and all three `fail_closed_leads` counters can remain zero. This is a behavioral false negative, not merely wording. Replace the two `strpos` tests with a genuinely conservative resolution mechanism, or keep every trigger-call chain explicitly unclassified/blocking until the captured definitions and callees are reviewed. Diagnostic 6 at `4a0bfa91b9bb` is therefore not ready for Gate 3.

### Non-blocking findings

1. P4's deliberate inclusion of every overload and its possible matches inside comments or strings create false positives. That is acceptable for a fail-closed diagnostic; the remaining blocker is the false-negative call syntax above.
2. No additional static syntax or result-shape defect was identified in the reviewed catalog queries. This is not a substitute for PostgreSQL parsing: if a later authorized run returns an error, the error must be saved unchanged and execution stopped as the headers require.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- **Round 56 finding 1 is closed.** Diagnostic 5 v2's empty baseline makes extension identity/version, library/symbol and ACL unclassified for every routine until an exact identity and captured property are reviewed. `acl_not_default` separately exposes explicit ACLs. A routine cannot incorrectly enter `routines_with_no_lead_reason` merely because these properties lack a baseline.
- **Round 56 finding 2 is closed.** The trigger lead covers both `trigger` and `event_trigger`, and K3 uses distinct routine OIDs for its total and per-extension routine counts.
- **Round 56 finding 4 is closed.** Diagnostic 6 now makes relevant partition/inheritance topology fail closed and returns it in P7; P5 recognizes quoted `auth.users` spellings and separately lists every PL/pgSQL or SQL routine detected as using dynamic SQL. Root completeness and unsupported topology are explicit result conditions rather than silent assumptions.
- **Round 56 finding 5 is closed.** P6 covers both deletion roots and returns relation kind, owner, RLS enabled/forced state, effective DELETE privilege for all three client roles, policy counts and every DELETE/ALL policy. P1 and P6 both expose exact root coverage.
- Both revised files remain read-only by static inspection. Every run is a catalog-only `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; no DML, DDL, transaction control, application-function invocation or explicit advisory/table lock is present. The catalog helper functions used are observational.
- Diagnostic 5 v2 is internally fail closed: its first execution is expected to leave all 64 routines carrying unclassified provenance/ACL leads. That is the intended evidence for later exact classification, not a claim that the routines are harmful or cleared.

### Residual risks

- Gate 3 would authorize only read-only execution of one exact file hash. It would not accept the result, clear any lead, approve a baseline or manifest, decide D1 to D6, approve plan v6 or grant any other gate.
- C-language behavior remains opaque. Diagnostic 5 can capture declared provenance and security properties, but each routine remains blocked until the captured identity/version/symbol/ACL is classified in a separately reviewed baseline or manifest.
- A database-only inventory cannot verify behavior inside Supabase's external authentication service or dashboard. That limitation remains an explicit Founder/platform assumption.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

The outcome is split by artifact:

- QA **recommends Founder Gate 3** for follow-up diagnostic 5 v2 at exact hash `cb312adf53ca`. Its two Round 56 findings are closed, its read-only boundary is sound by static inspection, and no remaining blocking defect was found.
- QA **does not recommend Founder Gate 3** for follow-up diagnostic 6 v2 at `4a0bfa91b9bb`. Round 56 findings 4 and 5 are closed, but finding 3 remains open because P4 can omit valid static call edges and falsely report no trigger-call lead.

This recommendation is audit advice only. It does not itself authorize either execution and changes no gate.

Handoff: `AWAITING-FOUNDER` — the Founder may accept or decline Gate 3 for diagnostic 5 v2 at exact hash `cb312adf53ca`. Diagnostic 6 v2 must not be authorized or run; after the Founder records the diagnostic 5 decision, Claude should revise and re-hash diagnostic 6 and return it to QA.

- **P4 call resolution replaced.** Each readable source is lower-cased and stripped of double quotes, and a routine is a callee when its name occurs **anywhere as a whole identifier** (neighbouring characters not letters, digits or underscore). No parenthesis, whitespace or comment form is required, so QA's three forms all match; names that are not plain lower-case identifiers are matched by plain substring; every overload in every non-system schema is a candidate; the edge set is materialized once (`calls AS MATERIALIZED`) and the closure still terminates on cycles because it uses `UNION`.
- **Closure seeds widened** to the other places a delete or a referential action can invoke a function: the trigger definitions (including a `WHEN` condition), column default and generated-column expressions, and check and exclusion constraints of the tables of interest (counters `expression_sources_scanned`, `expression_seed_roots`; routines so found are flagged `is_seed_from_expression`).
- **Data-change flag broadened and renamed `mentions_dml_or_ddl`:** any whole-word INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE, TRUNCATE, COPY, CREATE, ALTER, DROP, GRANT or REVOKE in a readable source (deliberately noisy; its purpose is that no routine that can write is silently skipped).
- **Explicitly fail-closed output.** P4's `review_status` states that every closure member is UNCLASSIFIED until its captured definition has been reviewed and that a zero count does not clear a chain; `fail_closed_leads` counts dynamic SQL, sources not readable as plpgsql or sql (for example C functions), data-change mentions, and `closure_members_without_captured_definition_in_p3_or_p8`.
- **P8 (new)** returns the definition of every closure member that is readable, flagged by `mentions_dml_or_ddl` or dynamic SQL, and not already in P3, so the callees that can write are captured for review and not merely named.
- **Stated limits** (header): functions reached only through operators, casts, type input and output functions or index expressions are not resolved by name and are not claimed as covered; SQL assembled from fragments without `EXECUTE` is not seen; compiled functions are flagged not readable, not inspected. P3, P4 and P8 may be large; the header says a too-large cell is reported, not trimmed.

## Round 60 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Handoff was verified before substantive review: the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 59 and last QA-reviewed round 58.
- Diagnostic 6 v3 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `449e9e46ed561373420b67a36a423ac739ee942dde1c8def3e957a01a500d88e`, matching the stated short hash `449e9e46ed56`. The file contains eight labelled runs and eight final `jsonb_build_object` result statements.
- Diagnostic 5 v2 at `cb312adf53ca` was expressly excluded from this audit. QA did not re-audit it or inspect execution evidence.
- Diagnostic 6 v3 has not been parsed by PostgreSQL or executed. QA performed static review only; no database, parser, browser or external service was used, and no SQL or other artifact was edited.

### Blocking findings

None.

### Non-blocking findings

1. P4 is intentionally a lexical over-approximation rather than a SQL parser. Whole-identifier matches in comments, strings, variable names and unrelated expressions may pull extra routines and overloads into the closure. That is acceptable for this fail-closed diagnostic, but the resulting identities and definitions must be classified from the saved evidence; a large or noisy closure is not a failed execution.
2. P4 and P8 duplicate the same closure logic. They are consistent in this exact file: the same routine universe, normalized whole-identifier match, expression seeds, recursive `UNION`, readability test and DML/dynamic-SQL flags are used. Any later edit to one copy must keep the other byte-for-behavior equivalent and will require a new hash and review.
3. The file correctly states that operators, casts, type input/output functions and index expressions are not resolved as named call edges. Gate 4 review must inspect the captured P2/P3/P6/P8 definitions and policies for such constructs and treat any unresolved effect as a follow-up lead; P4's zero counters alone are expressly not clearance.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- **The Round 58 P4 blocker is closed.** Lower-casing and removing double quotes, followed by whole-identifier matching without requiring `(`, recognizes the previously missed valid forms: schema-qualified quoted calls, arbitrary spaces/tabs/newlines before the parenthesis and comments between the name and call syntax. Names outside the simple identifier class use a substring fallback. Every overload in every non-system schema is conservatively included, and recursive `UNION` terminates cycles.
- The widened seeds cover the root trigger identities plus trigger definitions (including `WHEN` expressions), column default/generated expressions, and check/exclusion constraints for every table in the delete/update interest set. Quoted names and noncanonical whitespace receive the same normalized matching as routine bodies.
- P4 is behaviorally fail closed. Every closure member is declared unclassified; dynamic SQL, unreadable languages, DML/DDL mentions and members without a captured P3/P8 definition receive explicit counts. A zero value is not represented as clearing the chain.
- P8 consistently reconstructs the P4 closure and returns `pg_get_functiondef` for every readable, non-trigger closure member flagged for dynamic SQL or a DML/DDL term. Its `definitions_expected` predicate matches the predicate used to select the returned functions. Root trigger functions remain captured in P3, and unreadable closure members remain explicit P4 leads.
- The broadened DML/DDL expression includes INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE, TRUNCATE, COPY, CREATE, ALTER, DROP, GRANT and REVOKE as whole words. False positives are fail-safe; a readable routine with an overt data/schema/privilege-changing statement cannot be silently omitted from P8 merely because it is not DELETE.
- Read-only safety is sound by static inspection. All eight runs are catalog-only `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT` statements. `MATERIALIZED`, recursive CTE evaluation, catalog rendering helpers, regular-expression matching and privilege checks do not invoke application routines or perform DML, DDL, transaction control or explicit advisory/table locking.
- The unchanged P1, P2, P3, P5, P6 and P7 controls previously confirmed in Round 58 remain present: exact root coverage; fail-closed partition/inheritance handling; complete outgoing FK actions; trigger and rule definitions; quoted root-name and dynamic-SQL inventory; and privilege/RLS/policy evidence for both deletion roots.

### Residual risks

- Static review does not prove PostgreSQL parseability, catalog permissions, runtime cost or whether P3/P4/P8 fit in one SQL Editor result cell. An error or oversized cell must be preserved/reported without trimming, editing or retrying under the same authorization.
- Lexical call closure cannot establish behavior. Gate 4 review must reconcile P3/P4/P8, classify every nonzero fail-closed lead and investigate unresolved definitions; extension or compiled behavior may require another separately hashed diagnostic.
- The database cannot verify deletion initiated inside Supabase's external authentication service or dashboard. That remains an explicit Founder/platform assumption rather than database evidence.

### Disposition

`PASS`

QA **recommends Founder Gate 3** for follow-up diagnostic 6 v3 at exact hash `449e9e46ed56`. The Round 58 blocking finding is closed, the new P8 and widened seeds are internally consistent with P4, and the exact file remains read-only by static inspection. This is authorization advice for the eight read-only runs only; it does not accept future results, clear any lead, decide D1 to D6, approve a migration or grant any other gate.

Handoff: `AWAITING-FOUNDER` — the Founder may accept or decline Gate 3 for diagnostic 6 v3 at exact hash `449e9e46ed56`. If authorized, run P1 through P8 one at a time and preserve every result or error unchanged under the file's evidence instructions.

- **Saved:** the Founder's untouched raw file (151,703 bytes) and, derived from it by script (wrapper removed, table-copy escaping decoded, none of which occurred in these three; no retyping), `T-001_FU5-K1_06-10-2026.json`, `...K2...` and `...K3...`. Same method QA verified byte for byte for FU4.
- **Completeness:** all three parse as complete strict JSON with the expected `run` values. K2 has 64 routines (`routine_count` 64, 64 distinct identities). K1 reconciles with K2: lead counts recomputed from the 64 entries equal K1's (`acl_not_default` 59, `acl_unclassified` 64, `extension_identity_version_unclassified` 64, `library_symbol_unclassified` 64, `library_not_in_libdir` 5, `returns_trigger_or_event_trigger` 1), per-schema counts equal (cron 7, extensions 49, net 5, vault 3), executability counts equal (anon 58, authenticated 58, service_role 59). `total_matches_expected` and `per_schema_all_match` are true, `baseline_rows` 0, `routines_with_no_lead_reason` 0. K3: 7 extensions, `c_routines_in_extensions_total` 64; per-extension `member_c_routines` sum to 67 because `plpgsql` owns 3 C routines in `pg_catalog`, which the run excludes from the 64 by design.
- **Claude's reading (analysis, not evidence):** every one of the 64 belongs to exactly one extension: `pg_cron` 1.6.4 (cron 7), `pg_net` 0.19.5 (net 5), `supabase_vault` 0.3.1 (vault 3), and in `extensions` `pgcrypto` 1.3 (36), `uuid-ossp` 1.1 (10), `pg_stat_statements` 1.11 (3). No owner differs from its extension's owner, no library name differs from its extension, owners are only `postgres` and `supabase_admin`. None is SECURITY DEFINER, none has function configuration, all are plain functions; one returns a trigger (`cron.job_cache_invalidate()`). The only library lead is the five `pg_net` routines, which record the bare library name `pg_net` and a default ACL. Not executable by `anon` or `authenticated`: `cron.alter_job`, `cron.schedule_in_database`, `extensions.pg_stat_statements_reset` and the three `vault._crypto_aead_det_*` helpers (only the decrypt helper is executable, by `service_role`).
- **A question this evidence does not answer.** `cron.schedule` (two forms) and `cron.unschedule` (two forms) are executable by PUBLIC, hence by `anon` and `authenticated`, according to `has_function_privilege`, which tests EXECUTE on the function and not USAGE on the schema. Whether a client role can reach the `cron` schema, and what scheduling a job would let it do, is **not measured**. It matters to the T-001 boundary because a scheduled job runs SQL and the guard design must not be bypassable by one (J5 showed only two active jobs, neither mentioning the group tables). Claude carries this as a lead into plan v6 and proposes a small read-only measurement of schema USAGE and cron-job ownership for the client roles, to be reviewed like any diagnostic; it is not asserted as a gap.
- **What stays blocking:** all 64 remain positive leads until classified by exact captured identity in a reviewed baseline or manifest (plan v5 section 3, QA Round 58); extension membership is provenance, not proof of harmlessness.

### Blocking findings

None.

### Non-blocking findings

1. Round 61 section C1 refers to “nine saved FU5 files,” but the requested and present evidence set contains five files: one raw source, three derived JSON documents and one index. All five are present; this is an editorial count error only.
2. The index heading “No dangerous declared properties” should not be read as a safety conclusion. The narrower facts are supported: none of the 64 is SECURITY DEFINER, none has `proconfig`, all have `prokind = 'f'`, and one returns `trigger`. C-language behavior remains opaque, the trigger return and cron executability remain leads, and all 64 remain unclassified.

### Raw derivation, completeness and reconciliation

- The raw file has exactly three labelled blocks, K1, K2 and K3. Each contains one complete table-copied result row with the expected leading `| ` and trailing ` |` wrapper. Independently removing only that wrapper and applying the documented `\\` to `\` and `\|` to `|` decoding reproduces each saved JSON file byte-for-byte. No doubled-backslash or escaped-pipe sequence occurs in any of the three raw payloads. All three derived files parse as strict JSON and have the expected `run` value.
- K2 declares and contains 64 routines with 64 distinct `(schema, name, identity arguments)` identities. Its schema counts are cron 7, extensions 49, net 5 and vault 3; all 64 have exactly one extension membership.
- Recomputing K2's leads gives exactly K1's counts: `acl_not_default` 59, `acl_unclassified` 64, `extension_identity_version_unclassified` 64, `library_symbol_unclassified` 64, `library_not_in_libdir` 5 and `returns_trigger_or_event_trigger` 1. K2 also reproduces K1's effective execution counts: anon 58, authenticated 58 and service_role 59.
- K1 reports total 64, expected total 64, `total_matches_expected = true`, all four per-schema counts matching, `per_schema_all_match = true`, `baseline_rows = 0`, 64 routines with a lead and zero without a lead. Those values are internally consistent with K2.
- K3 contains seven extension rows and reports 64 distinct non-system-schema C routines belonging to extensions. The six extensions represented by K2 reconcile exactly: pg_cron 7, pg_net 5, pg_stat_statements 3, pgcrypto 36, supabase_vault 3 and uuid-ossp 10. K3's per-extension C counts sum to 67 because its per-extension subquery also counts three `plpgsql` extension routines in `pg_catalog`; its separately filtered distinct total correctly remains 64.

### Audit of Claude's reading

- The provenance reading is accurate: all 64 routines have exactly one extension membership; the stated extension versions, schema counts and owners match K2/K3. Routine owners are only `postgres` (49) and `supabase_admin` (15), with no owner/extension-owner mismatch and no library-name/extension-name mismatch lead.
- The declared-property reading is accurate: zero SECURITY DEFINER routines, zero function configurations, zero non-function `prokind` values and one trigger-returning routine, `cron.job_cache_invalidate()`.
- The library and ACL reading is accurate. The five pg_net routines use bare `pg_net` rather than `$libdir/pg_net`, have default ACLs and account for all five `library_not_in_libdir` leads. The other 59 have explicit ACLs. The six routines not executable by anon/authenticated and the one service-role-only vault helper are correctly identified.
- The cron concern is correctly framed as an unanswered access question, not a proven vulnerability. Both `cron.schedule` overloads and both `cron.unschedule` overloads have PUBLIC EXECUTE and therefore return true for anon/authenticated/service_role in K2. K2 does not measure effective USAGE on schema `cron`, direct rights on cron relations, or the ownership rules and identities of scheduled jobs.

### Answer to Round 61 C2: cron USAGE and ownership measurement

QA agrees that a separate, exact-hash, read-only measurement is required before plan v6 treats scheduled jobs as outside the protected writer boundary. At minimum it should return:

- schema `cron` owner and ACL plus effective `has_schema_privilege(..., 'USAGE')` for `PUBLIC`, anon, authenticated, service_role, authenticator, postgres and any role reachable from the client/runtime role graph;
- exact EXECUTE ACL/effective privilege for every `cron.schedule`, `cron.schedule_in_database`, `cron.alter_job` and `cron.unschedule` identity, reconciled with K2;
- owner, ACL, RLS enabled/forced state and effective SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE privileges on `cron.job` and any sequence or companion relation used to create/manage jobs;
- sanitized active and inactive job ownership grouped by database and `username`, with job IDs/names/schedules/active flags and command classification or hashes rather than unrestricted command text; include whether any command mentions the protected group tables or protected writer routines;
- the exact role graph relevant to each observed job owner, including whether a client/runtime role can SET ROLE or otherwise reach that owner.

The diagnostic must not infer pg_cron's compiled ownership checks from function ownership or ACLs. If catalog evidence cannot establish who may create, replace or unschedule another role's job, that uncertainty remains a positive lead rather than being assumed safe.

### Answer to Round 61 C3: filling the baseline

QA agrees with building the baseline from K2, subject to these controls:

1. Preserve the current K2 JSON and raw file as the immutable source. Generate a separate machine-readable candidate manifest with exactly one row for each of the 64 distinct `(schema, name, identity arguments)` values; do not make the diagnostic's own output self-approving.
2. Copy exact captured values, without normalization beyond the already sorted ACL: extension name/version/owner/schema/relocatability, routine owner, library, symbol, ACL/default status, effective client executability, result type, `prokind`, SECURITY DEFINER, volatility, parallel safety and configuration. Include the current lead reasons and a human classification field with evidence and rationale.
3. Prove a bijection against K2: exactly 64 manifest rows, no duplicate identity, no missing/extra identity and exact equality for every captured property. Grouped rationale is acceptable only when every member and exact property set are enumerated; it must not hide overloads.
4. Classification must distinguish “expected platform-extension provenance” from “behavior proven harmless.” Extension membership or absence of a group-table name is not enough to prove compiled behavior. Trigger routines, bare-library entries, client-executable cron routines and any privilege anomaly need explicit individual or tightly enumerated classification.
5. Freeze the reviewed manifest by full/short SHA-256. If the baseline is later embedded as a `VALUES` CTE or test input, that resulting file is a new exact artifact requiring QA review. Any extension upgrade, identity, owner, library, symbol, ACL or declared-property change must fail comparison and return to review; it must not silently refresh the baseline.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The Founder preserved all three result cells in one raw source, and the raw-to-JSON derivation is independently reproducible without retyping or unexplained transformation.
- The three runs mutually reconcile across identity, schema, extension, lead and effective-privilege counts. No error, malformed JSON, duplicate identity or truncation is visible.
- The intentionally empty baseline worked fail closed: all 64 routines remain unclassified and none is represented as cleared merely because it belongs to an extension.
- Claude separated factual evidence from interpretation and correctly carried the cron exposure forward as a measurement question.

### Residual risks

- Gate 4 verifies the authorized execution and faithful evidence only. It does not clear any of the 64 leads, approve a baseline/manifest, establish C-language behavior, resolve the cron boundary, decide D1 to D6, approve plan v6 or grant another gate.
- The three runs are separate statements rather than one transactional snapshot. Their counts and identities reconcile, so no relevant drift is visible, but later baseline validation must remain exact and fail closed.
- Cron schema reachability, job ownership and command authority remain unmeasured until a separately reviewed diagnostic is authorized and audited.

### Disposition

`PASS`

QA **recommends Gate 4** for diagnostic 5 v2 at exact hash `cb312adf53ca`. The Founder-authorized read-only execution produced complete K1/K2/K3 evidence, the raw derivation is exact, and the saved results reconcile. This recommendation is audit advice only; only the Founder can accept Gate 4, and no lead or other gate is cleared by this round.

Handoff: `AWAITING-FOUNDER` — the Founder may accept or decline Gate 4 for diagnostic 5 v2 at exact hash `cb312adf53ca`. Diagnostic 6 v3 evidence remains for a separate later audit.

Each run is one `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT` returning one `jsonb` cell named `result`; no pg_cron function is called; results go into one raw Notepad file (`T-001_FU7-raw_<date>.raw.txt`), kept untouched, with derived JSON made by script. It implements QA Round 62's list:
- **C1:** schema `cron` owner, ACL and every ACL entry including PUBLIC; effective USAGE and CREATE on it for **every** non-system role (`has_schema_privilege`, which follows inheritance); each role's superuser, login and BYPASSRLS attributes; the `cron.*` server settings.
- **C2:** every routine in schema `cron` with owner, language, definer flag, ACL, whether PUBLIC may execute it, and every role that effectively holds EXECUTE (to be reconciled with K2's seven cron C routines).
- **C3:** every relation in schema `cron` (including `job` and `job_run_details` and any sequence) with owner, ACL, RLS enabled and forced, the roles holding SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE (or sequence USAGE), and every policy.
- **C4:** every job, active or not: id, name, schedule, database, username, active flag, command length, the first 16 hex characters of its SHA-256, first word, flags (protected group tables, `invite_token`, `grp_batch_writer`; the `cron` schema; `net.http`; DML or DDL words; `EXECUTE`), the names of non-system routines whose names occur in it, and counts grouped by database, username and active. **The command text is never returned.** A job that mentions the protected group objects, or that these fields cannot classify, is a positive lead.
- **C5:** for every job owner plus `postgres` and `supabase_admin`: whether the role exists, its attributes, and whether each of `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, `authenticator`, `dashboard_user` and `cli_login_postgres` is a member of it, inherits its privileges, or can `SET ROLE` to it (`pg_has_role`).
- **Stated limit, as QA required:** pg_cron's own permission checks (who may create, replace or remove another role's job, and which roles a job may run as) are in compiled code and are **not inferred** from ACLs or ownership; if catalog evidence cannot establish them, the uncertainty stays a positive lead and is never assumed safe. Job run history is not read (it can contain output); only its privileges are measured.
- **Checked by script:** all five runs end in a semicolon, have balanced parentheses and an even number of quote characters; no write or DDL word outside comments and string literals; no backslash and no carriage return; the hash above is of the bytes on disk. **Not checked:** no database and no local PostgreSQL, so the file has **not** been parsed or executed. QA may wish to read especially: `aclexplode` used in a `FROM` list with `nspacl` (C1) and in an `EXISTS` (C2); `pg_has_role(..., 'SET')` (C5, PostgreSQL 16 and later; the server is 17.6); the `UNION` of job owners with two fixed roles and the grouping in C5; the `sha256(convert_to(...))` expression and the `substring(... from '^[a-z_]+')` expression in C4; reading `cron.job` (the J5 precedent); and the size of C3 and C5 in one cell (small).
