## Round 64 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Before substantive review, QA verified the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 63 and last QA-reviewed round 62.
- Diagnostic 6 v3 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `449e9e46ed561373420b67a36a423ac739ee942dde1c8def3e957a01a500d88e`, matching the Founder-authorized short hash `449e9e46ed56`. Diagnostic 7 v1 hashes to full SHA-256 `e79f647efe95ec9be4d0a399c43479c53828be92de57c81496f2d753dbb7eeb6`, matching the artifact submitted for review.
- QA audited the FU6 raw source, P1 through P8 JSON files and index; independently reproduced the raw derivation; reconciled the result sets; and statically reviewed all five statements in diagnostic 7 v1. No database query, browser or external service was used, and no evidence, diagnostic, plan, brief or application file was edited.

### Blocking findings

1. **Diagnostic 7 C3 does not fully measure sequence authority.** For a `relkind = 'S'` relation, C3 returns only `has_sequence_privilege(..., 'USAGE')` and deliberately makes its SELECT and UPDATE fields null. PostgreSQL sequences have distinct `USAGE`, `SELECT` and `UPDATE` privileges; UPDATE is especially relevant to changing sequence state. Round 62 C2 required effective access to every sequence or companion relation used to create or manage jobs. A revision must return effective `USAGE`, `SELECT` and `UPDATE` role sets for every cron sequence (and keep INSERT/DELETE explicitly not applicable), while retaining SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE for ordinary companion relations.
2. **Diagnostic 7 C4 does not implement its stated fail-closed classification rule.** Round 63 says a job that the returned fields cannot classify is a positive lead, but C4 emits no `classification_requires_review`/`classification_complete` result and defines no exact condition for it. A job with a null/unusual first token and no lexical flag or matched routine can therefore look negative without the result saying it is unclassified. The revision must make the conservative outcome explicit: every job remains a lead unless an enumerated rule classifies it, and an unclassified count/list must be returned. The job fingerprint should also be the full SHA-256, not only 16 hexadecimal characters, so later evidence can establish exact command equality without returning command text.

These are completeness/fail-closed defects in the proposed measurement, not evidence that pg_cron is currently exploitable. Diagnostic 7 v1 is still read-only by static inspection.

### Non-blocking findings

1. The FU6 set has **ten**, not eleven, files: one raw file, eight derived JSON files and one index. All ten expected files are present. Round 63's “eleven files” is an editorial count error.
2. The FU6 index says “17 more fire on UPDATE only.” The evidence actually contains 13 UPDATE-only triggers plus four INSERT-and-UPDATE triggers: 17 additional triggers fire on UPDATE, but only 13 fire on UPDATE *only*. This does not affect the dependency or erasure conclusions.
3. Diagnostic 7 C4's `mentioned_routines` entries are schema/name strings rather than exact routine identities and can repeat a name for overloads. This is acceptable as a conservative lexical lead detector, but no matched name may be treated as one resolved overload or as behaviorally cleared. Exact identity must be resolved in the reviewed follow-up evidence/manifest.

### FU6 raw derivation, completeness and reconciliation

- The raw file is 337,139 bytes and hashes to `241cdc7a4283cf8dacb7486e07888b887164cc6629e5cc541ed2cc87d341beba`. It has exactly eight labelled blocks, P1 through P8, with one wrapped table-copy row in each. Independently removing only the leading `| ` and trailing ` |`, then decoding `\\` to `\` and `\|` to `|`, reproduces all eight saved JSON files byte-for-byte. P3 contains 1,781 doubled-backslash pairs and four escaped pipes; P8 contains 295 and eight; the other six contain neither. Every derived file is strict, complete JSON with its expected `run` value.
- P1 reconciles to 40 reached tables and 70 listed edges: two roots, 33 tables at depth 1 and five at depth 2; 49 CASCADE, 20 SET NULL and one NO ACTION edge. `roots_complete` is true, unsupported topology is false, and inherited/partition counters are zero. The sole NO ACTION edge is `flashcard_batch_provenance.created_by -> public.profiles`.
- P2 has 43 tables of interest, 34 triggers, 30 distinct trigger functions and zero rewrite rules. P3 contains exactly those 30 functions; P4 has the same 30 roots. All trigger functions are PL/pgSQL; 19 distinct functions are SECURITY DEFINER. The 34 rows include 20 SECURITY DEFINER trigger uses because one definer function is used by more than one trigger.
- P4's 50 listed closure members reproduce its schema distribution and all four fail-closed counters: one dynamic-SQL member, 24 DML/DDL-word members, four unreadable members and 16 members without a captured definition. P5 contains 248 scanned routines and exactly the 11 listed dynamic-SQL matches. P6 finds both roots and reports `roots_complete = true`. P7 contains the same 43 tables, no inheritance row and no partitioned table. P8 expected and returned four definitions.

### Audit of Claude's FU6 reading

- The cascade reading is supported. The closure reaches `study_groups`, `study_group_members`, `content_group_shares` and `batch_group_professors` at depth 1 and `batch_group_archives` at depth 2. Thus deleting a creator's profile currently cascades through the creator-owned study group, including a batch group, and its dependent membership/share/archive/professor rows. The stated SET NULL group edges also match P1.
- The trigger reading is supported subject to non-blocking finding 2. No trigger is attached to a protected group table; there are no rewrite rules; and the P3/P8 bodies do not name `study_group*`, `content_group_shares`, `batch_group*`, `access_requests` or `invite_token`. `fn_guard_admin_audit_log_immutable` does permit the observed foreign-key SET NULL form while rejecting direct deletion and other updates.
- P5 supports the narrow statements that `admin_delete_user_data` is the only scanned routine whose captured definition deletes from `profiles`, `get_user_retention_stats` mentions `auth.users`, and no captured routine deletes from `auth.users`. P6 supports the RLS/policy/privilege description: `auth.users` has no client DELETE privilege; `profiles` gives DELETE privilege to authenticated and service_role but has no DELETE/ALL policy, so authenticated is denied by RLS default-deny while service_role's previously measured BYPASSRLS remains material.
- None of those observations behaviorally clears P4. The four unreadable C routines may be lexical over-approximation, but the evidence does not prove that. The service/dashboard deletion path and code outside captured database definitions also remain assumptions, not catalog proof.

### Answers to Round 63 section D

1. **Gate 4:** QA recommends Founder Gate 4 for diagnostic 6 v3 at exact hash `449e9e46ed56`. The authorized eight runs are present, the raw-to-JSON derivation is exact, and the evidence is complete and internally reconciled. Gate 4 verifies execution and preservation only; it clears no closure lead and grants no other gate.
2. **How to classify the 24 and 16 closure leads:** all 24 DML/DDL-word members already have definitions in this evidence—20 in P3 and four in P8—so another definition-capture query is not needed for them. Their bodies must still be behaviorally classified, individually or in tightly enumerated groups, before Gate 2; absence of a protected table name is not by itself clearance. Of the 16 uncaptured members, 12 are non-C helpers and need exact definitions plus the call/seed edge that placed each in the closure, because P4 does not expose that edge. The four C identities are not accepted merely as extension members. `extensions.gen_random_uuid()` and `extensions.uuid_generate_v4()` may be classified only through the exact, pinned diagnostic-5 candidate baseline with explicit provenance and behavior rationale. Both `cron.schedule` overloads remain open pending a corrected diagnostic 7 and review of pg_cron's side effects and authority. For all four, the lexical parent/seed edge should be retained so an over-approximation can be distinguished from an actual call without silently dropping the lead.
3. **D3 erasure question:** yes, the cascade evidence is sufficient to return the erasure-survival question to brief A's design gate. It establishes that current profile deletion removes creator-owned batch groups and their dependent access/history rows. It is not sufficient to approve implementation: the design must explicitly choose preservation, anonymization/reassignment or deletion semantics and then prove the chosen rollback and migration behavior. The outstanding closure classifications remain pre-implementation constraints.

### Diagnostic 7 safety, correctness and scope

- **Read-only safety confirmed by static inspection:** each run is one `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`; it reads catalogs, settings and `cron.job`; it does not call a cron routine, execute job text, mutate a database object or return unrestricted command text. C1/C2 cover schema/routine owners, ACLs, PUBLIC/effective access and role attributes. C3 covers all cron relations, ACLs, RLS and policies apart from the sequence-privilege omission above. C5 uses transitive `pg_has_role` checks for the six specified runtime/dashboard sources against every observed job owner plus postgres and supabase_admin, and preserves compiled-code uncertainty as a lead.
- The uses of `aclexplode`, `sha256(convert_to(...))`, the C5 owner union/grouping and PostgreSQL 17 `pg_has_role(..., 'SET')` are statically coherent. The file has not been parsed or executed, so this is not runtime validation.
- C4 appropriately avoids command text and returns ownership, schedule, activity, lexical flags and routine-name leads, but it must be revised as blocking finding 2 describes before this measurement is complete and fail closed.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The Founder preserved one untouched raw source, and the derivation is independently reproducible even for the two escaped large payloads.
- FU6's independent runs reconcile across roots, tables, triggers, functions, closure members and extra definitions; no truncation, malformed JSON, duplicate identity problem or unexplained count drift was found.
- Diagnostic 7 separates measured catalog authority from unobservable compiled pg_cron behavior, enumerates active and inactive jobs, and avoids exposing scheduled command text.

### Residual risks

- Gate 4 for diagnostic 6 does not establish transactionally consistent snapshots across P1 to P8, clear any of the 50 closure members, approve a deletion design, resolve D1 to D6, approve plan v6 or authorize SQL implementation.
- Diagnostic 7 v1 has not been parsed or run and is not recommended for execution. Cron schema reachability, sequence authority, job ownership and command classification remain unmeasured until a corrected exact artifact is reviewed, Founder-authorized and its results audited.
- The diagnostic-5 candidate baseline/manifest has not yet been produced or approved. Extension membership and lexical absence of protected object names remain provenance observations, not proof of harmless behavior.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

For diagnostic 6 v3, QA's artifact-specific result is `PASS`, and QA **recommends Founder Gate 4** for exact hash `449e9e46ed56`.

For diagnostic 7 v1, QA **does not recommend Founder Gate 3** for exact hash `e79f647efe95`. Claude should revise C3 to measure all relevant sequence privileges and revise C4 to make unclassified jobs fail closed with a full command SHA-256, then submit the new exact hash for QA review. ## Round 66 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Before substantive review, QA verified the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 65 and last QA-reviewed round 64.
- Diagnostic 7 v2 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `75ec6e61f39c05f38930b690fcaec5947e44b5d53677bde8ed7cbb0483809202`, matching the submitted short hash `75ec6e61f39c`. It supersedes unapproved, unrun v1 `e79f647efe95`.
- QA statically reviewed all five C1–C5 statements and Round 65 section D2. No database query, browser or external service was used, and no SQL, evidence, plan, brief or application file was edited.

### Blocking findings

None.

### Closure of the two Round 64 blocking findings

1. **C3 sequence authority — closed.** Every cron sequence now gets separate effective-role lists from `has_sequence_privilege` for `SELECT`, `UPDATE` and `USAGE`. INSERT and DELETE are null for sequences and `insert_delete_applicable` is false; non-sequence relations retain effective SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE lists. `sequence_count` makes the sequence inventory independently reconcilable. This measures all three PostgreSQL sequence privileges requested by Round 64.
2. **C4 fail-closed classification and exact fingerprint — closed.** Each job now has a full 64-hex SHA-256, an explicit `classification_rule`, and `classification_requires_review = true` whenever no enumerated rule matches. Aggregate classified/unclassified counts and unclassified job IDs are returned. The sole rule, `R1_http_post_only`, is implemented as the conjunction stated in Round 65: first word `select`; a `net.http` mention; no protected-object, cron-schema, DML/DDL or EXECUTE flag; and exactly one matched non-system routine name, `net.http_post`. Null or unusual first words, extra matched names and any adverse flag fail closed. The name array is DISTINCT and ordered, so its exact `text[]` comparison is deterministic. The result fields are sufficient to recompute whether the rule matched.

### Non-blocking findings

1. `R1_http_post_only` classifies only the lexical shape of the stored SQL. It does **not** establish that `net.http_post` is behaviorally harmless, that its remote destination cannot write protected data, or that scheduled jobs are outside the writer boundary. Round 65 and the file state this limit correctly. Plan v6 and the later evidence reading must retain the compiled pg_net behavior and external endpoint/caller boundary as separate positive leads; `classification_requires_review = false` must not be used as behavioral clearance.
2. `unclassified_job_ids` will be JSON null, not an empty array, when no job requires review because its `jsonb_agg` is not wrapped in `COALESCE`. `jobs_requiring_review = 0` disambiguates this, so it is not a correctness or Gate 3 blocker; the evidence parser should accept that representation.
3. `mentioned_routine_identities_all_overloads` deliberately lists every overload sharing a matched routine name. It remains conservative lexical evidence and must not be read as proof that every listed overload—or any particular overload—was invoked.

### Read-only safety and static correctness

- The file contains five statements, one for each C1–C5 run. Each is a `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`. Apparent write words occur only in comments, privilege-name string literals, output labels and command-classification regular expressions. No cron routine, application routine, dynamic SQL, transaction control, DML or DDL is executed.
- C1 and C2 read catalog/settings data and evaluate schema/routine privileges. C3 reads catalog and policy metadata and evaluates table or sequence privileges according to relation kind. C4 and C5 read `cron.job` but neither returns nor executes `command`; C4 exposes only length, full hash and derived flags/names. The catalog and hashing functions used are read-only.
- The C3 `CASE` branches call `has_sequence_privilege` only for sequences and `has_table_privilege` for the other admitted relation kinds. The three sequence privilege names are valid. C4's DISTINCT ordered name array makes the rule comparison stable; its all-overload identity array is also ordered. A null command, if the catalog ever allowed one, would not satisfy R1 and would remain unclassified. C5 only calls `pg_has_role` for source roles selected from `pg_roles` and for an existing target role.
- The full-hash expression, `encode(sha256(convert_to(jb.command, 'UTF8')), 'hex')`, is coherent for the stated PostgreSQL 17 server. The uses of `aclexplode`, `has_*_privilege`, `pg_has_role(..., 'SET')`, array equality and JSON aggregation are statically coherent. The file has not been parsed or executed, so runtime validity and result size remain for the authorized run to prove.

### Diagnostic 8 scope in Round 65 D2

The proposed purpose is adequate: preserve the derivation path for all 50 P4 closure members and capture exact definitions for the 12 readable, non-C helpers missing from P3/P8. Before a diagnostic 8 file is submitted, the scope should explicitly require all of the following so the result is reproducible and fail closed:

1. Reuse the same interest-table, routine-identity normalization and conservative lexical matcher as diagnostic 6 P4, and reconcile the live result against the saved P4 set: 50 exact `(schema, name, identity arguments)` members, 30 trigger-function roots and 34 expression-seed roots. Any missing, extra or ambiguous identity is a positive lead, not an automatic baseline refresh.
2. Return **all** direct provenance edges, not one chosen predecessor or a spanning tree. For routine-to-routine edges, include exact caller and every matched callee identity. For roots, distinguish direct trigger-function roots from expression seeds. For each expression seed, identify the source object and type: trigger plus table, column default/generated expression plus table/column, or check/exclusion constraint plus table/constraint.
3. Include the normalized matched name/token and enough source evidence to reproduce each lexical match, while continuing to label every edge as an over-approximation rather than a proven runtime call. Cycles and multiple parents must be preserved. Return reconciliation counts showing that every one of the 50 members is reachable from at least one recorded root and that no recorded edge points to an identity omitted from the inventory.
4. Enumerate the expected 12 non-C target identities and return `pg_get_functiondef` plus language, owner, SECURITY DEFINER, volatility, configuration/search-path and ACL metadata for each. Missing, duplicate, unreadable, changed-language or extra results must fail closed. The four C identities need their parent/seed edges but not a fabricated SQL definition; they remain governed by the diagnostic-5 baseline and diagnostic-7 evidence.
5. Preserve the raw result and derived JSON under the existing evidence protocol, with per-run expected/actual counts and exact-identity reconciliation. If split into multiple runs, cross-run drift or mismatch must remain a lead.

With those controls made explicit in the eventual file, the diagnostic 8 scope is adequate. This is scope advice only, not Gate 3 advice for a file that does not yet exist.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- V2 makes the sequence and ordinary-relation privilege semantics explicit instead of overloading one field ambiguously.
- C4 now fails closed mechanically and preserves an exact command fingerprint without exposing potentially secret command text.
- The file continues to separate observed catalog authority from pg_cron's compiled ownership checks and from the behavior of pg_net or an external HTTP destination.
- C1–C5 together still cover schema access, routine execution, relation/sequence access, sanitized job inventory and reachability from the six runtime/dashboard roles specified in Round 62.

### Residual risks

- Gate 3 would authorize only these five read-only runs. It would not validate their future results, clear a cron or pg_net lead, prove who pg_cron permits to create/replace/remove another role's job, approve plan v6 or grant Gate 2.
- The five runs are separate snapshots. Their saved evidence must reconcile across cron routines, relations, job owners and role reachability; an error or mismatch is evidence and must not be repaired by editing or selectively rerunning the SQL.
- Diagnostic 8, its exact 12-helper list, the 50-member classification worksheet, the C-routine baseline and the D3 design decision do not yet have approval through this review.

### Disposition

`PASS WITH CONDITIONS`

QA **recommends Founder Gate 3** for diagnostic 7 v2 at exact hash `75ec6e61f39c`, for its five read-only runs only. The conditions are interpretive and evidentiary: R1 is lexical classification only; every compiled/external behavior lead remains open; preserve every result or error unchanged; and return the complete C1–C5 evidence for a separate Gate 4 audit. No other gate is recommended or approved.

Handoff: `AWAITING-FOUNDER` — the Founder may authorize or decline Gate 3 for diagnostic 7 v2 at exact hash `75ec6e61f39c`. If authorized, run C1 through C5 one at a time under the file's evidence instructions. Diagnostic 8 should be drafted only with the scope controls above and must return as a new exact-hash artifact for QA review before any execution request.

## Round 68 · QA · 06/10/2026

### Scope and preconditions

- Before substantive review, QA verified the status block was `AWAITING-QA`, owner `QA`, last round 67 and last QA-reviewed round 66.
- Diagnostic 8 v1 on disk hashes to full SHA-256 `6b248a383c7a4f19adc12ca30096c1a29b7895fb5192a7075b47b4c1c740bfc8`, matching the submitted short hash `6b248a383c7a`.
- QA audited all four E1–E4 statements against the five Round 66 scope controls and checked the 12 E3 identities against the saved diagnostic-6 P4 closure. No database query, browser or external service was used, and no SQL, evidence, plan, brief or application file was edited.
- The Founder's batching instruction is recorded in Round 67. QA will audit future artifacts in one appended QA round per explicitly submitted batch. Batching changes review grouping only: every artifact must still be identified by its own exact hash, and findings and gate advice remain per artifact.

### Blocking findings

1. **E1 does not perform exact-set reconciliation; its “match” flags compare counts only.** `closure_members_match`, `trigger_function_roots_match` and `expression_seed_roots_match` test only whether the live counts are 50, 30 and 34. One saved identity can disappear while one different identity appears and all three flags can remain true. This does not implement Round 66 control 1, which required reconciliation against the saved exact `(schema, name, identity arguments)` sets and made every missing, extra or ambiguous identity a positive lead. V2 must embed the saved expected sets, or their unambiguous canonical frozen digests, and return explicit missing/extra lists for the 50 closure members and the 30/34 flagged subsets. Count reconciliation should remain, but must not be described as identity matching.
2. **E2 does not preserve the required seed-source types.** Both an ordinary default and a generated-column expression are labeled `column_default`; both a check and an exclusion constraint are labeled `constraint`. The returned source text can distinguish CHECK from EXCLUDE, but it cannot reliably establish default versus generated status for all 43 interest tables, because E4 covers only five group tables. Round 66 control 2 required source object *and type*. V2 must label defaults and generated columns separately using `pg_attribute.attgenerated`, and label check and exclusion constraints separately using `pg_constraint.contype`, while preserving the source object, text and matched token.

### Non-blocking findings

1. E2's two zero-valued reconciliation conditions are largely structural consequences of its own recursive CTE: every callee of a reachable caller enters `tclos`, and every non-seed closure member entered through a recorded `calls` edge. They remain useful consistency checks, but they are not independent proof that the live closure equals saved P4; the exact-set comparison required by blocking finding 1 supplies that proof.
2. E2 does not repeat each routine caller's source text. That is acceptable if the evidence is reviewed as one set: P3/P8 already preserve definitions for 34 readable members and E3 captures the remaining 12, while the four C members cannot be source-read and therefore originate no `calls` edge. The Gate 4 audit must actually join those definitions to E2 and reproduce the lexical matches; a matched token alone is not behavioral proof of a call.
3. E4 uses the read-only built-in `format_type`, which is omitted from the header's catalog-function inventory. This is a documentation omission only; it does not change safety.

### Read-only safety and static correctness

- The file contains four statements, one for each E1–E4 run. Each is a `SELECT` or `WITH RECURSIVE ... SELECT`. No application, cron or extension routine is called; no dynamic SQL, transaction control, DML or DDL is executed. Catalog text is read through `pg_get_*` functions only.
- E1 and E2 reproduce diagnostic 6 P4's cascade-interest construction, routine filter, normalized-name matcher and recursive `UNION` closure. Adding matched tokens and source metadata does not alter the routine-to-routine predicate. Joining `pg_attribute` to `pg_attrdef` is catalog-consistent and does not intentionally change the seed set.
- E3's enumerated identities exactly match the 12 readable, previously uncaptured P4 members, including their recorded identity arguments. Its exact joins, definition capture, sorted ACL, and missing/duplicate/unreadable lists are statically coherent. `pg_get_functiondef`, language, owner, SECURITY DEFINER, volatility, configuration, kind and ACL satisfy Round 66 control 4. Null rather than `[]` for an empty issue list is unambiguous when `found_count = expected_count` and all three issue fields are reviewed.
- E4 fixes the five table names in schema `public`, requires ordinary or partitioned tables, returns every live non-dropped column with type/nullability/identity/generated/default metadata, and returns check, unique, primary-key and exclusion constraints. Combined with the already saved foreign-key evidence, this is sufficient catalog scope for choosing the D3 creator-link mechanism. Its table-count check cannot substitute for reviewing the returned columns and constraints.
- Static inspection found coherent SQL constructs, but the file has not been parsed or executed. Result size—especially E2—and live catalog drift remain for an authorized run to establish after revision.

### Round 66 control-by-control result

1. Same matcher and count reproduction: **partially met**; the matcher is behaviorally preserved, but exact saved-set reconciliation is absent (blocking finding 1).
2. All provenance edges and typed roots/sources: **partially met**; all edge families and source objects are returned, but two source-type pairs are collapsed (blocking finding 2).
3. Reproducible lexical evidence, cycles/multiple parents and reachability checks: **met with the non-blocking cross-evidence requirement above**. E2 returns all matched edges from reachable callers, preserves multiple parents and cycles through the full `calls` relation, supplies tokens/source text for seeds, and labels every edge an over-approximation.
4. Exact definitions and fail-closed capture of the 12 readable helpers: **met**.
5. Raw/derived evidence protocol and cross-run drift handling: **met in instructions**, but only a future authorized execution and Gate 4 evidence audit can verify compliance.

### Strengths or confirmed controls

- The 12-helper list is exact and frozen rather than selected by a loose name-only predicate.
- E2 returns all reachable routine edges rather than choosing a convenient path, so alternate parents and cycles are retained.
- Source expressions and helper definitions are catalog text, not executed code, and the four compiled members are not represented with fabricated definitions.
- E4 adds the precise nullability/generated/default and constraint evidence needed by the Founder-approved D3 survival decision without mutating the approved brief.

### Residual risks

- Even after revision, the closure remains a conservative lexical over-approximation. Comments and strings can create false edges, while operators, casts, type support functions, index expressions and fragmented dynamic SQL can escape name matching.
- E1–E4 are separate snapshots. Their exact identity sets, definitions and edge endpoints must reconcile at Gate 4; an error, truncation or mismatch must be preserved as evidence, not repaired by editing or selective rerun.
- This review does not classify the 50 members, approve the C-routine baseline, select the D3 detach/reassignment design, revise brief A, approve plan v6 or grant any gate for diagnostic 7.

### Disposition

`REVISION REQUIRED`

QA **does not recommend Founder Gate 3** for diagnostic 8 v1 at exact hash `6b248a383c7a`. Claude should add exact expected-set reconciliation to E1 and preserve the four distinct seed-source types in E2, then submit the revised file under a new hash as the next review batch. The file is read-only by static inspection; the refusal is due to incomplete fail-closed evidence behavior, not a mutation risk.

Handoff: `AWAITING-CLAUDE` — revise diagnostic 8 as above and return the new exact hash in one batch. The Founder may independently decide the already recommended Gate 3 for unchanged diagnostic 7 v2 (`75ec6e61f39c`).

## Round 69 · Claude · 06/10/2026
**Scope.** This round is a **batch** (the method the Founder approved in Round 67): (A) Gate 3 for diagnostic 7 v2 recorded, and its evidence saved and checked, for QA's audit and Gate 4 recommendation; (B) follow-up diagnostic 8 **v2**, revised and re-hashed after QA Round 68; (C) brief C v1 (points 6 and 7), the first slice, for QA's design audit; (D) what Claude did not do. QA's Round 68 was committed unchanged as `35b99e5` before any edit. No SQL was run by Claude (no database access); no application code, plan, approved diagnostic or brief was edited; diagnostics 5 v2, 6 v3 and 7 v2 are unchanged (hashes re-checked on disk). **Each artifact below is identified by its own exact hash and each finding and gate request is per artifact.**

### A. Diagnostic 7 v2: Gate 3 recorded; evidence saved and checked (index `docs/discussions/evidence/T-001_FU7-index_06-10-2026.md`, committed `46939fb`)
- **Saved:** the Founder's untouched raw file (40,066 bytes, five labelled blocks) and, by script from it (no retyping; no escaped backslash or pipe occurred), `T-001_FU7-C1_06-10-2026.json` to `...C5...`, plus the index: seven files.
- **Completeness:** all five parse as complete JSON with the expected `run` values. C2's 7 routines equal the seven `cron` routines of diagnostic 5's K2 with the same executability split; C3 4 relations (2 sequences) and 2 policies equal their lists; C4's 2 jobs equal the two jobs of FU4 J5; C5 2 roles equal their list.
- **Claude's reading (analysis, for QA to audit):** **no client role can reach the `cron` schema.** The schema ACL grants USAGE to `postgres` and USAGE and CREATE to `supabase_admin` only; effective USAGE is held by `postgres`, `supabase_admin`, `supabase_etl_admin` and `supabase_read_only_user`, and **`anon`, `authenticated`, `authenticator`, `service_role`, `dashboard_user` and `cli_login_postgres` hold neither USAGE nor CREATE**. So the PUBLIC EXECUTE on `cron.schedule` and `cron.unschedule` found in diagnostic 5 is not reachable by those roles (this answers Round 61 C2 for the six roles measured); the saved role graph (FU4 J1) shows none of them can reach `supabase_etl_admin` or `supabase_read_only_user`. `cron.job` and `cron.job_run_details` have row-level security with the policy `username = CURRENT_USER`. The two active jobs both run as `postgres`, mention no protected group object, no `cron` schema, no DML or DDL and no dynamic SQL, and the only non-system routine name in either is `net.http_post`. Only `cli_login_postgres` can SET ROLE to `postgres`, as in the plan's trusted set. None of the 96 saved routine definitions mentions `cron.` or `pg_cron`.
- **Observation about the rule, not about the jobs:** `R1_http_post_only` matched neither job because `first_word` is null for both (the command probably begins with a non-letter, such as a newline, which `btrim` does not remove). Both jobs therefore show as "requiring review" by construction, and `unclassified_job_ids` is a real array here. This is the rule failing closed on a formatting detail; no change is proposed to a file that has run.
- **Still open (stated in the index):** pg_cron's own permission checks; the behaviour of the compiled `net.http_post` and of the external endpoints the two jobs call; a point-in-time read, not one snapshot with FU5 or FU6.
- **Request to QA:** audit the seven FU7 files as for FU4 to FU6 (reproduce the derivation from the raw file, check completeness and reconciliation and the reading) and say whether Gate 4 is recommended for diagnostic 7 v2 (`75ec6e61f39c`).

A missing, extra or ambiguous identity is a positive lead and refreshes nothing. The 254 expression-source total stays a count.

### C. Brief C v1: `docs/discussions/T-001_brief-C_review-badge-and-heatmap.md`, short sha256 `241a1d9e2d34` (full `241a1d9e2d34992ea7ea73e578352d83102e440aa82319b00610277676d28b9e`), a design brief, not SQL
The first slice in the Founder's agreed order (Round 67 section C). It covers **point 6** (the Review badge and the other "due" numbers) and **point 7** (the heatmap), built only from evidence already in the thread (Round 2 findings 6.1 to 6.8 and 7.1 to 7.4, RUN 1B, RUN 3, RUN 8, F5), with each fact labelled VERIFIED, PROPOSAL or OPEN. In short:
- **Point 6.** Cause (VERIFIED, Round 6 and QA): the badge is fetched once and never refreshed after Pause, Resume, Remove, Re-add, review completion or course switch; the enrollment mismatch is real but small (RUN 8: 59 mismatch rows of 4,138, from one account, all own cards with no enrollment row). Proposal: one meaning of "due" for all four numbers (nav badge, Dashboard "Reviews due", Progress "due today", Dashboard Forward Load): the cards the Review page would let the student review today; `get_due_forecast` and `get_due_forecast_buckets` get the enrollment predicate of `get_my_cards`; **`get_study_queue` is not changed**; a small shared refetch after the listed actions; no polling.
- **Point 7.** `get_study_heatmap` returns one total per date; about 95 percent of recorded hours are offline (RUN 3). Proposal: a new RPC returning in-app (`study_mode` plus `practice_mode`), offline (`manual`), total and reviews per date (in-app plus offline equals the total); a hover, focus and pinned-tap tooltip, keyboard reachable, with accessible names, Escape to close and reduced motion; local-date handling without `toISOString()`. Course and subject stay in brief B's day-detail view.
- **Files (inventory only):** C-01 forecast functions, C-02 heatmap RPC (each with ROLLBACK and a real-role TEST), C-03 frontend; SQL first, then frontend, then live verification; no dependency on briefs A or B.
- **Proof:** the badge changes within one request after each listed action; all four "due" numbers agree with the Review page (a read-only RUN 8-style comparison returns zero mismatches except the open account); in-app plus offline equals the old total for every day; keyboard and screen-reader checks.
- **Decisions for the Founder (recommendations in the brief):** D-C1 one meaning of "due"; D-C2 the own cards without an enrollment row (59, or 103 over any status and date): find the cause first, no data change yet; D-C3 offline is exactly `source = 'manual'`, in-app is `study_mode` and `practice_mode`.
- **Request to QA:** audit brief C v1 as a design (accuracy of every cited fact against the thread and the saved evidence, completeness of the design and of the proof list, anything missing or over-reaching) and say whether it is ready for the Founder's Gate 1; findings per artifact, as for any brief.
